Uniqueness of Asymptotically Conical Gradient Shrinking Solitons in G 2 G_{2} -Laplacian Flow
Mark Haskins, Ilyas Khan, and Alec Payne

Abstract
. We prove a uniqueness result for asymptotically conical (AC) gradient shrinking solitons for the Laplacian flow of closed G 2 G_{2} -structures: If two gradient shrinking solitons to Laplacian flow are asymptotic to the same closed G 2 G_{2} -cone, then their G 2 G_{2} -structures are equivalent, and in particular, the two solitons are isometric. The proof extends Kotschwar–Wang’s argument for uniqueness of AC gradient shrinking Ricci solitons. We additionally show that the symmetries of the G 2 G_{2} -structure of an AC shrinker end are inherited from its asymptotic cone; under a mild assumption on the fundamental group, the symmetries of the asymptotic cone extend to global symmetries.



1.
Introduction

This paper is aimed at improving the current understanding of a natural class of noncompact singularity models to Laplacian flow, a geometric flow of closed and nondegenerate 3-forms on a
7
-dimensional spin manifold. A major hope for Laplacian flow is that it will provide a parabolic approach to the construction of
G_{2}
-holonomy metrics on spin
7
-manifolds. The latter are interesting to Riemannian geometers because their Ricci tensor vanishes. Currently, such
G_{2}
-holonomy metrics are the only known source of (nonflat) Ricci-flat metrics on compact, simply-connected manifolds of odd dimension. Existing constructions of
G_{2}
-holonomy metrics on compact manifolds are all based on the singular elliptic perturbation method pioneered by Joyce
[
19
]
and since developed by various authors
[
20
,
31
,
21
,
9
,
14
]
.

In this parabolic approach, one might initially hope that the Laplacian flow exists for all time and converges in infinite time to a torsion-free
G_{2}
-structure, i.e. a nondegenerate 3-form which induces a metric with holonomy contained in
G_{2}
. In practice, one expects a Laplacian flow with generic initial data to encounter both finite-time and infinite-time singularities
[
4
, §6.2]
. The simplest (and often most important) type of finite-time singularity model for a geometric flow is a shrinking soliton, a natural class of self-similar solution. Shrinking solitons for both Ricci flow and mean curvature flow have been extensively studied, and a variety of examples and classification results are now known: we refer the reader to
[
6
,
1
]
for overviews of this extensive body of work. By contrast, the study of shrinkers in Laplacian flow (and of Laplacian flow solitons more generally) is still in its infancy; a number of examples have been constructed recently
[
33
,
38
,
15
,
18
,
32
]
, but almost no classification results are available.

A key property that distinguishes Laplacian flow from Ricci flow or mean curvature flow is the absence of compact shrinkers. This is a consequence of the fact that the volume element increases monotonically under Laplacian flow. Therefore, for Laplacian flow an important task is to understand what asymptotic geometries can occur for complete noncompact Laplacian shrinkers and which of these geometries model finite-time singularities of the flow on a compact manifold. Once a certain type of asymptotic geometry is known to occur for shrinkers, it then becomes natural to ask what asymptotic data is needed to determine the shrinker completely. As the asymptotic geometry of a shrinker models the macroscopic structure of a singularity of the flow, unique determination of a shrinker from its asymptotics gives strong information about how the singularity forms and potentially how to continue the flow past this singularity.

Very recently, Haskins–Nordström showed that shrinkers that are asymptotic to smooth cones arise in Laplacian flow
[
18
]
—as
is well-known in the context of Ricci flow and mean curvature flow shrinkers
[
11
,
26
,
34
,
2
,
41
]
.
Work by Lu Wang and later Bernstein on shrinking hypersurfaces in mean curvature flow
[
3
,
40
]
, by Kotschwar–Wang on gradient Ricci shrinkers
[
29
]
, and Khan for higher codimension mean curvature flow shrinkers
[
25
]
has shown that such asymptotically conical (AC) shrinkers exhibit a perhaps surprising degree of rigidity: they are uniquely determined by their asymptotic cone. Related elliptic problems do not share such strong rigidity properties. For instance, it is well-known that AC Kähler Ricci-flat metrics with a given asymptotic cone often occur in non-trivial finite-dimensional moduli spaces. It turns out, however, that the strong rigidity properties of AC shrinkers have a parabolic origin: in strong backward uniqueness results for parabolic subsolutions.

In upcoming work, Haskins–Juneman–Nordström prove that the highly-symmetric AC Laplacian shrinkers constructed in
[
18
]
are determined by their asymptotic cones, within the class of shrinkers sharing the same symmetries
[
17
]
. The high degree of symmetry allowed these authors to establish rigidity with ODE methods. The rigidity results obtained in this special case suggested the further possibility that any AC Laplacian shrinker would be uniquely determined by its asymptotic cone, just as in Ricci flow and in mean curvature flow. Removing the symmetry assumptions puts the question beyond the scope of ODE methods.
In this paper, we use PDE methods to prove that gradient AC Laplacian shrinkers are indeed determined by their asymptotic cones.

We now proceed with some more precise definitions and the statements of our main results.
Laplacian flow (introduced by Bryant in
[
4
]
) is a one-parameter family of closed
3
-forms
t\mapsto\varphi(t)
which represent
G_{2}
-structures and solve

\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial t}=\Delta_{\varphi}\varphi,

for
t\in[0,T)
, where
\Delta_{\varphi}\varphi
is the Hodge Laplacian of
\varphi
. See
[
5
,
35
,
13
]
for additional background on this flow.

The gradient shrinking Laplacian soliton equation is

\Delta_{\varphi}\varphi=-\tfrac{3}{2}\varphi+{\mathcal{L}}_{\nabla f}\varphi,

where
\varphi
is a closed
G_{2}
-structure on
M
and
f
is a smooth function on
M
. Any solution to the gradient shrinking Laplacian soliton equation determines a self-similarly shrinking solution to Laplacian flow. See
[
18
, §2]
for a survey of what is known about Laplacian solitons.

We can now state the main theorem (see Definition
3.1
for what it means for a shrinker to be asymptotic to a cone along an end):

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
(M_{1},\varphi_{1},f_{1})
and
(M_{2},\varphi_{2},f_{2})
be two gradient Laplacian shrinkers asymptotic to the closed
G_{2}
-cone
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
along the ends
V_{1}\subset M_{1}
and
V_{2}\subset M_{2}
, respectively. Then there exist ends
W_{1}\subset V_{1}
and
W_{2}\subset V_{2}
and a diffeomorphism
\Psi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W_{1}\rightarrow W_{2}
such that
\Psi^{*}\varphi_{2}=\varphi_{1}
.

We briefly sketch the proof of this theorem. Broadly speaking, our proof extends the methodology employed by Kotschwar–Wang
[
29
]
for Ricci shrinkers to the setting of Laplacian flow. Kotschwar–Wang reduce the question of uniqueness of AC shrinkers in Ricci flow to a parabolic backward uniqueness problem. Starting at any AC shrinking end, there is an associated Ricci flow which converges to the asymptotic cone in finite time. The goal then is to prove backward uniqueness for Ricci flows which converge to the same cone in finite time. However, the difference of two AC shrinking ends with the same asymptotic cone does not satisfy a strictly parabolic equation, and due to the inapplicability of DeTurck’s trick (see
[
27
, §1]
), there is no obvious way to break the gauge invariance of the problem. To
overcome this problem Kotschwar–Wang construct an ODE-PDE system for the evolution of the differences of appropriate geometric quantities and prove that backward uniqueness holds for this ODE-PDE system
[
29
, §3]
. They prove the parabolic backward uniqueness property for this system using Carleman estimates, as pioneered in the work of Escauriaza–Seregin–Šverák on backward uniqueness results for the heat equation
[
10
]
.

However, the application of these methods in our setting is far from straightforward due to several significant differences between Laplacian flow and Ricci flow. The starting point for the proof is the fact that each
G_{2}
-structure
\varphi
induces a Riemannian metric
g_{\varphi}
on
M
, which then evolves along with
\varphi
under Laplacian flow. Indeed, we might first hope that the techniques of
[
29
]
would apply to these underlying metrics after comparing the Ricci flow equation to the evolution of the metric under Laplacian flow:

(1.1)

\frac{\partial g_{ij}}{\partial t}=-2\Ric_{ij}-\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}-4T_{i}^{\;l}T_{lj},

where
T=T_{ij}
is the torsion
2
-form of the closed
G_{2}
-structure (see (
2.1
) for its definition). The torsion terms in (
1.1
) are second order in the metric, scale like curvature, and are not “small” compared to the Ricci curvature.

The additional torsion terms can have a dramatic influence on the geometry of Laplacian shrinkers. For instance, there exist examples of complete gradient shrinking Laplacian solitons due to Fowdar which have exponential volume growth and constant negative scalar curvature
[
15
]
, starkly contrasting with Ricci shrinkers which have nonnegative scalar curvature and at most Euclidean volume growth
[
7
]
. In fact, for a closed
G_{2}
-structure, the scalar curvature
R
satisfies
R=-|T|^{2}
, meaning that we are always working in the setting of nonpositive scalar curvature. In the AC setting, the torsion and hence the scalar curvature are both asymptotically small. Intuitively, therefore, the AC condition ameliorates the negative scalar curvature of closed
G_{2}
-structures, thus somewhat constraining the geometry of AC
Laplacian shrinkers.

The first major departure from the approach of Kotschwar–Wang, then, is that even in the AC setting, the shrinker potential
f
satisfies substantially weaker properties than in Ricci flow. More specifically, in the analysis of gradient Ricci shrinkers a key role is played by the following identity for the shrinker potential
f

R+{\left\lvert\nabla f\right\rvert}^{2}-f=0.

In the AC setting, the Laplacian flow analogue of this identity (see Lemma
3.6
) contains an
additional term with logarithmic growth; this turns out to change significantly the nature of the backward uniqueness problem for the ODE-PDE system that we must solve. The large error terms resulting from this phenomenon are ultimately consolidated in an error term growing logarithmically in the radial variable and lead to a necessarily much weaker Carleman estimate than found in Kotschwar–Wang (see Proposition
5.15
). This requires a new approach on the PDE level to the proof of backward uniqueness, involving a careful subdivision of the domain and judicious absorption of “bad terms” (see Proposition
6.2
). In Appendix
A
, we showcase how these techniques can be used to prove the following extension of the main theorem of
[
10
]
:

Theorem A.1.

Let
Q_{R,T}
be the parabolic domain
({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus B_{R})\times[0,T]
, and let
u\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}Q_{R,T}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
satisfy

(1.2)

|\Delta u+\partial_{t}u|\leq M|\nabla u|+(N|x|^{\delta}+M)|u|\;\;\text{ and }\;\;|u(x,t)|\leq Me^{M|x|^{2}},

for constants
M,T,R,C,D>0
and
0<\delta<1/2
. Then, if
u(x,0)\equiv 0
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus B_{R}
,
u
vanishes identically in
Q_{R,T}
.

The second major complication, beyond the differences between Ricci and Laplacian flow on the metric level, is the fact that the objects of Laplacian flow are closed nondegenerate
3
-forms and that the induced
metric
structure contains strictly less information than the corresponding
3
-form. The point at which this becomes relevant is in the setup of the parabolic backward uniqueness problem in Section
4
. In this section, we construct Laplacian flows that interpolate between an AC shrinker
\varphi
and its conical limit
\varphi_{C}
.

However, the flows as constructed converge
a priori
only to
some
closed
G_{2}
-cone
\varphi_{D}
and not necessarily to the common limit
\varphi_{C}
given in Theorem
1.1
. Thus, it is necessary to show that
\varphi_{C}=\varphi_{D}
, in order that the limit
\varphi_{1}(t)-\varphi_{2}(t)
vanishes as
t\rightarrow 0
and the backward uniqueness problem be well-defined. Kotschwar–Wang used Cheeger–Colding theory for the limits of metric spaces in order to identify the two metric cones associated to
g_{D}
and
g_{C}
. As we are working at the level of the
3
-form, we cannot rely on this theory to make this identification. Ultimately, we overcome this obstacle by comparing the
C^{k}
-topologies of convergence generated by
g_{D}
and
g_{C}
for sections of
\Lambda^{3}(TM)
and showing that the rescaling limit of
\varphi
is the same in both of these topologies. Furthermore, we should point out that our construction is not limited to the case of 3-forms: it generalizes the reparametrization procedure in
[
29
]
to flows of an
(r,s)
-tensor with the induced bundle metric.

After proving Theorem
1.1
, we also obtain a number of corollaries. Corollary
1.2
is a global statement which follows from Theorem
1.1
after using an argument for global extension of isometries
[
30
, Theorem 5.2]
.

Corollary 1.2
.

Let
(M_{1},\varphi_{1},f_{1})
and
(M_{2},\varphi_{2},f_{2})
be two complete gradient Laplacian shrinkers asymptotic to the closed
G_{2}
-cone
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
along the ends
V_{1}\subset M_{1}
and
V_{2}\subset M_{2}
, respectively. Suppose that for some
x_{0}\in V_{1}
and
y_{0}\in V_{2}
, the homomorphisms
\pi_{1}(V_{1},x_{0})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(M_{1},x_{0})
and
\pi_{1}(V_{2},y_{0})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(M_{2},y_{0})
induced by inclusion are surjective.

Then, there exists a diffeomorphism
\Psi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}M_{1}\to M_{2}
such that
\Psi^{*}\varphi_{2}=\varphi_{1}
.

Theorem
1.1
is then used to prove Theorem
1.3
, which says that ends of AC shrinkers have the same symmetries as their asymptotic cones. For a closed
G_{2}
-structure
(M,\varphi)
, we define
\Aut(M,\varphi)
to be all the diffeomorphisms
F\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}M\to M
such that
F^{*}\varphi=\varphi
. The group
\Aut(M,\varphi)
describes the symmetries of the
G_{2}
-structure.

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
(M,\varphi,f)
be a gradient shrinking Laplacian soliton asymptotic to the closed
G_{2}
-cone
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
along the end
V\subset(M,\varphi)
. Then, there is
r>0
, an end
W
of
(M,\varphi)
, and a diffeomorphism
F\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma}=\Sigma\times(r,\infty)\to W
such that
\gamma\mapsto F\circ\gamma\circ F^{-1}
is an isomorphism from
\Aut({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
to
\Aut(W,\varphi)
.

Here,
\Aut({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
refers to the
G_{2}
-automorphisms fixing the vertex of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}
. The Ricci flow analogue of Theorem
1.3
, which establishes an isomorphism between isometry groups, was proven by Kotschwar–Wang in
[
30
]
and relies on their uniqueness result for AC Ricci shrinkers. Given Theorem
1.1
, we may adapt the argument of
[
30
]
(see Section
7
). The argument involves extending known results on local real analyticity of Laplacian flow
[
36
]
to show that the
\varphi_{C}
and
\varphi_{D}
are analytic with respect to a common real analytic structure. As a consequence, despite the fact that the asymptotic cone of a shrinker does not itself satisfy the shrinker equation, we deduce the following (see Corollary
7.4
,
cf.

[
30
, Cor. 3.2]
):

The asymptotic cone of an AC Laplacian shrinker must have a real analytic cross-section.

As a corollary of Theorem
1.3
, we find that under a mild topological assumption, the symmetries can be extended to the entire shrinker, not just an end.

Corollary 1.4
.

Let
(M,\varphi,f)
be a connected, complete gradient shrinking Laplacian soliton asymptotic to the closed
G_{2}
-cone
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
along the end
V\subset(M,\varphi)
. If for some
x_{0}\in V
, the homomorphism
\pi_{1}(V,x_{0})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(M,x_{0})
induced by inclusion is surjective, then
\Aut({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
embeds into
\Aut(M,\varphi)
.

Finally, we note that the techniques developed to prove the results of this paper are sufficiently flexible that we expect our approach to be relevant beyond Laplacian flow. Further applications of these techniques may include proving similar results for other geometric flows or finding general criteria under which a flow’s shrinkers satisfy an analogous uniqueness property.

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
Preliminaries on
G_{2}
-structures

Throughout this paper,
M
will denote a connected, smooth
7
-manifold. A
G_{2}
-structure on
M
is a reduction of the structure group of the frame bundle to the group
G_{2}\subset GL(7)
. This is a purely topological condition, equivalent to
M
being orientable and spinnable
[
16
]
. It is well-known that any
G_{2}
-structure can be uniquely associated to a particular
3
-form
\varphi\in\Omega^{3}(M)
defining the
G_{2}
-structure, called a
G_{2}
-form. Such a
3
-form must satisfy the property that for each
x\in M
, there is an isomorphism
\iota\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}T_{x}M\to{\mathbb{R}}^{7}
with
\iota^{*}\varphi_{0}=\varphi_{x},
. Here,
\varphi_{0}
is the
3
-form on
{\mathbb{R}}^{7}=\operatorname{Im}({\mathbb{O}})
given by

\varphi_{0}(u,v,w)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\langle u\times v,w\rangle=\langle uv,w\rangle,

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
is the Euclidean inner product and
uv
is multiplication of
u
and
v
in the octonions.

Every
G_{2}
-structure we consider in this paper will be closed. This means that the associated
3
-form
\varphi
is a closed form, i.e.
d\varphi=0
. If
\varphi
is also co-closed, i.e.
d^{*}\varphi=0
, then the
G_{2}
-structure is said to be torsion-free. This is equivalent to the
G_{2}
-structure having zero torsion, in the sense of the torsion of the
G
-structure
[
12
]
.

For any
G_{2}
-structure
\varphi
on
M
,
g=g_{\varphi}
is a metric and an orientation canonically associated to
\varphi
by the relation

g_{\varphi}(u,v)\Vol_{g_{\varphi}}=\iota_{u}\varphi\wedge\iota_{v}\varphi\wedge\varphi,

which holds for any vector fields
u,v
on
M
. We will often suppress the subscript notation in
g_{\varphi}
unless we need to specify the dependence of the metric on the
G_{2}
-structure. An important fact is that if a
G_{2}
-structure is torsion-free, then this is equivalent to
\Hol(M,g_{\varphi})\subseteq G_{2}

[
12
]
.

There is a Hodge star
\star_{\varphi}
, using the metric and orientation associated to the
G_{2}
-form
\varphi
. The torsion of a
G_{2}
-structure is given by a two-tensor
T_{ij}
satisfying

(2.1)

\nabla_{i}\varphi_{jkl}=T_{i}^{\;m}(\star_{\varphi}\varphi)_{mjkl}.

If the
G_{2}
-structure is closed, then
T_{ij}
is a two-form and hence is anti-symmetric. Also, in the closed case,
T_{ij}
is divergence-free,
g^{ij}\nabla_{i}T_{jk}=0
, and
T_{ij}
satisfies the crucial relation

(2.2)

R=-|T|^{2},

where
R
is the scalar curvature of the metric
g_{\varphi}
. See
[
4
,
23
,
24
,
35
]
for additional details on these identities.

2.2.
Scaling of geometric quantities

The scaling behavior of
G_{2}
-structures plays an important role throughout this paper, so for concreteness, we state the following basic result describing this behavior.

Lemma 2.1
.

If
\varphi
is a
G_{2}
-structure on
M
,
\Psi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}M\rightarrow M
is a diffeomorphism, and
\lambda>0
is a constant, consider the pulled-back and rescaled 3-form
\lambda\Psi^{*}\varphi
, which is also a
G_{2}
-structure on
M
. Then, the corresponding metric
g_{\lambda\Psi^{*}\varphi}
is given by
\lambda^{\frac{2}{3}}\Psi^{*}g_{\varphi}
. Moreover,
the following identities are satisfied:

(1)

(Hodge Laplacian)

(2.3)

\Delta_{\lambda\Psi^{*}\varphi}\lambda\Psi^{*}\varphi=\lambda^{\frac{1}{3}}\Psi^{*}(\Delta_{\varphi}\varphi)

(2)

(Hodge Dual)

(2.4)

\star_{\lambda\Psi^{*}\varphi}(\lambda\Psi^{*}\varphi)=\lambda^{\frac{4}{3}}\Psi^{*}(\star_{\varphi}\varphi)

(3)

(Torsion)

(2.5)

T_{\lambda\Psi^{*}\varphi}=\lambda^{\frac{1}{3}}\Psi^{*}T_{\varphi}

where
T
denotes the torsion tensor regarded as a
2
-tensor.

Proof.

The proofs follows from straightforward calculation in local coordinates using
the relation between the associated metric
g_{\varphi}
and
\varphi
.
The result can also be seen as a special case of the behavior of a
G_{2}
-structure
under a conformal rescaling, e.g as described in
[
22
, §3.1]
.
We explain how the scaling of the torsion tensor
T
(regarded as a 2-tensor) follows from the scaling behavior of the other geometric quantities.
We can ignore the pullback
\Psi^{*}
(since
\Psi
is an isometry) and consider the torsion tensor
\tilde{T}
corresponding to
\tilde{\varphi}=\lambda\varphi
.
Recall that, regarded as a
(1,1)
-tensor,
T
satisfies

\nabla_{X}\varphi=T(X)\lrcorner\star_{\varphi}\varphi,

for all smooth vector fields
X
.

Then using the fact that covariant differentiation is invariant under rescaling and the rescaling behavior for
\varphi
and
\star_{\varphi}\varphi
we see that as a
(1,1)
-tensor
the torsion is rescaled by
\lambda^{-1/3}
. So when we use the metric (which rescales by
\lambda^{2/3}
)
to regard
T
as a 2-tensor instead it will rescale by a factor of
\lambda^{1/3}

as claimed.

∎

2.3.
Estimates on the metric from estimates on the 3-form

In this subsection, we get explicit estimates on the metric associated to a perturbed
G_{2}
-form.

Proposition 2.2
.

Let
\varphi,\tilde{\varphi}
be two
G_{2}
-forms on a smooth
7
-manifold
M
, and let
g,\tilde{g}
be their corresponding induced metrics. There is an
\epsilon>0
such that if
|\tilde{\varphi}-\varphi|_{\varphi}\leq\epsilon
, we have

(2.6)

|\tilde{g}-g|_{\varphi}\leq C|\tilde{\varphi}-\varphi|_{\varphi},

where the norm
|\cdot|_{\varphi}
is taken with respect to
g=g_{\varphi}
and
C>0
is an absolute constant. Alternatively, if the bound
|\tilde{\varphi}-\varphi|_{\varphi}\leq\epsilon
is not known and
|\tilde{g}-g|_{\varphi}\leq\kappa
, then

(2.7)

|\tilde{g}-g|_{\varphi}\leq C(1+\kappa)|\tilde{\varphi}-\varphi|_{\varphi},

where
C>0
is an absolute constant.

Proof.

Define
\gamma\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\tilde{\varphi}-\varphi
. For any vector fields
u,v
on
M
,

\displaystyle\tilde{g}(u,v)\Vol_{\tilde{\varphi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}(\iota_{u}\tilde{\varphi})\wedge(\iota_{v}\tilde{\varphi})\wedge\tilde{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}(\iota_{u}(\varphi+\gamma))\wedge(\iota_{v}(\varphi+\gamma))\wedge(\varphi+\gamma)

Then, there exists a
7
-form
{\mathcal{G}}(u,v)
on
M
depending on the vector fields
u,v
such that

(2.8)

\tilde{g}(u,v)\Vol_{\tilde{\varphi}}=g(u,v)\Vol_{\varphi}+{\mathcal{G}}(u,v)

and

(2.9)

|{\mathcal{G}}(u,v)|_{\varphi}\leq\frac{1}{6}|\gamma|_{\varphi}\big(|\gamma|_{\varphi}^{2}+3|\gamma|_{\varphi}|\varphi|_{\varphi}+3|\varphi|_{\varphi}^{2}\big)|u|_{\varphi}|v|_{\varphi},

where the bars
|\cdot|_{\varphi}
denote the norm taken with respect to the metric
g
induced by
\varphi
.

The most direct way to estimate the difference
\tilde{g}-g
is to expand
\Vol_{\tilde{\varphi}}
around
\Vol_{\varphi}
using the expansion
[
4
, (6.7)]
. By the decomposition of
3
-forms on
M
into irreducible representations of
G_{2}
, the form
\gamma
can be expressed as

(2.10)

\gamma=3b_{0}\varphi+\star_{\varphi}(b_{1}\wedge\varphi)+b_{3},\;\;\;b_{0}\in\Omega^{0}(M),b_{1}\in\Omega^{1}(M),\text{ and }b_{3}\in\Omega^{3}_{27}(M,\varphi),

where
3b_{0}\varphi
,
\star_{\varphi}(b_{1}\wedge\varphi)
, and
b_{3}
lie in the mutually
g
-orthogonal spaces
\Omega^{3}_{1}(M,\varphi)
,
\Omega^{3}_{7}(M,\varphi)
, and
\Omega^{3}_{27}(M,\varphi)
, respectively. We now obtain bounds on the forms
b_{0},b_{1},b_{3}
. The following estimate on
b_{3}
is immediate.

(2.11)

|b_{3}|_{\varphi}=|\pi^{3}_{27}(\gamma)|_{\varphi}\leq|\gamma|_{\varphi},

since
\pi^{3}_{27}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\Omega^{3}(M)\rightarrow\Omega^{3}_{27}(M,\varphi)
is an orthogonal projection operator with respect to
g
.

Now, notice that as in
[
35
, §2]
, we have that
\varphi_{ijk}\varphi_{abl}g^{ia}g^{jb}=6g_{kl}
. Contracting once more, we find that
|\varphi|_{\varphi}=7
, where we are taking the norm in
\Omega^{3}(M)
. Thus,

\displaystyle|3b_{0}\varphi|_{\varphi}

\displaystyle=|\pi^{3}_{1}(\gamma)|_{\varphi}\leq|\gamma|_{\varphi}

(2.12)

\displaystyle|b_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq|\gamma|_{\varphi}.

Finally, we estimate
b_{1}
. First, we note that
\star_{\varphi}(b_{1}\wedge\varphi)=-b_{1}^{\#}\lrcorner\star_{\varphi}\varphi
. Now, calculating as on
[
35
, pg. 226]
,

(2.13)

\gamma^{ijk}(\star_{\varphi}\varphi)_{ijkl}=(\gamma\lrcorner\star_{\varphi}\varphi)_{l}=24(b_{1})_{l}.

Fix
l
. By Cauchy-Schwartz,

\displaystyle|(b_{1})_{l}|=\frac{1}{24}|\gamma^{ijk}(\star_{\varphi}\varphi)_{ijkl}|\leq 7|\gamma|_{\varphi},

and therefore
|b_{1}|_{\varphi}=O(|\gamma|_{\varphi})
.

Finally, we appeal to the expansion
[
4
, (6.7)]
, which gives us a formula for the difference of the volume forms, in terms of
b_{0},b_{1},
and
b_{3}
.

(2.14)

\star_{\tilde{\varphi}}1=\bigg(1+7b_{0}+\bigg(14(b_{0})^{2}+\frac{2}{3}|b_{1}|^{2}_{\varphi}-\frac{1}{6}|b_{3}|^{2}_{\varphi}\bigg)+r(b_{0},b_{1},b_{3})\bigg)\star_{\varphi}1,

where, as we have shown,
b_{0},b_{1},b_{3}
are all
O(|\gamma|_{\varphi})
and
r
has cubic decay in its arguments. Let
u,v
be unit vectors with respect to
g
. Plugging (
2.14
) into (
2.8
), we obtain

\tilde{g}(u,v)(1+7b_{0}+Q(b_{0},b_{1},b_{3}))\Vol_{\varphi}=g(u,v)\Vol_{\varphi}+{\mathcal{G}}(u,v)

(\tilde{g}(u,v)-g(u,v))\star_{\varphi}\Vol_{\varphi}=(\tilde{g}(u,v)-g(u,v))(-7b_{0}-Q(b_{0},b_{1},b_{3}))\star_{\varphi}\Vol_{\varphi}\\
+g(u,v)(-7b_{0}-Q(b_{0},b_{1},b_{3}))\star_{\varphi}\Vol_{\varphi}+\star_{\varphi}{\mathcal{G}}(u,v)

\implies|\tilde{g}-g|_{\varphi}\leq C|\tilde{g}-g|_{\varphi}|\gamma|_{\varphi}+C|\gamma|_{\varphi}+\star_{\varphi}{\mathcal{G}}(u,v),

where
C
depends on the coefficients of the expansion
7b_{0}+Q(b_{0},b_{1},b_{3})
. If we set
\epsilon=1/2C
and assume that
|\gamma|_{\varphi}\leq\epsilon
, then we can absorb the first term on the left-hand side into the right-hand side and obtain

|\tilde{g}-g|_{\varphi}\leq C|\gamma|_{\varphi}+2\star_{\varphi}{\mathcal{G}}(u,v).

Since
|{\mathcal{G}}(u,v)|_{\varphi}
is
O(|\gamma|_{\varphi})
by (
2.9
) and the Hodge star is an isometry, we have

|\tilde{g}-g|_{\varphi}\leq C|\tilde{\varphi}-\varphi|_{\varphi}.

This proves the first statement of the proposition. In case that the bound
|\gamma|_{\varphi}\leq\epsilon
is unavailable but the bound
|\tilde{g}-g|_{\varphi}\leq\kappa
holds, we obtain

\displaystyle|\tilde{g}-g|_{\varphi}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa|\gamma|_{\varphi}+C|\gamma|_{\varphi}+\star_{\varphi}{\mathcal{G}}(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\kappa)|\gamma|_{\varphi}.

This proves the second statement in the proposition.
∎

Lemma 2.3
.

We can obtain the following bounds on the difference of the inverse metrics and the Hodge stars associated to
\varphi
and
\tilde{\varphi}
.

|\tilde{g}^{-1}-g^{-1}|_{\varphi}\leq C|\tilde{g}^{-1}|_{\varphi}|\tilde{\varphi}-\varphi|_{\varphi}.

and for an arbitrary
k
-form
\beta

(2.15)

|\star_{\varphi}\beta-\star_{\tilde{\varphi}}\beta|_{\varphi}\leq C|\tilde{\varphi}-\varphi|_{\varphi}|\beta|_{\varphi}.

Here,
C
depends on
\kappa
if the second set of bounds from Proposition
2.2
is used.

Proof.

The first estimate follows immediately from the basic inequality

|\tilde{g}^{-1}-g^{-1}|_{\varphi}\leq C|\tilde{g}^{-1}||\tilde{g}-g|_{\varphi}.

We now prove the second statement. For
\alpha
and
\beta
arbitrary
k
-forms,

(2.16)

(\star_{\varphi}\beta-\star_{\tilde{\varphi}}\beta)\wedge\alpha=g_{\varphi}(\beta,\alpha)\Vol_{g_{\varphi}}-g_{\tilde{\varphi}}(\beta,\alpha)\Vol_{g_{\tilde{\varphi}}},

by the definition of the Hodge star. Taking the Hodge star again of this expression, by the same reasoning as in the proof of Proposition
2.2
, we get

\displaystyle g_{\varphi}(\beta,\alpha)\star_{\varphi}\Vol_{g_{\varphi}}-g_{\tilde{\varphi}}(\beta,\alpha)\star_{\varphi}\Vol_{g_{\tilde{\varphi}}}

\displaystyle=g_{\varphi}(\beta,\alpha)-g_{\tilde{\varphi}}(\beta,\alpha)+g_{\tilde{\varphi}}(\beta,\alpha)O(|\gamma|)

\displaystyle=O(|\gamma|_{\varphi}|\beta|_{\varphi}|\alpha|_{\varphi}).

Thus,

|\star_{\varphi}\beta-\star_{\tilde{\varphi}}\beta|_{\varphi}\leq C|\gamma|_{\varphi}|\beta|_{\varphi}.

∎

In the next proposition, we obtain similar estimates to those in Proposition
2.2
for covariant derivatives of
g-\tilde{g}
with respect to the Levi-Civita connection on
(M,g)
.

Proposition 2.4
.

Under the same assumptions as Proposition
2.2
, for
k\in\mathbb{N}

(2.17)

|\nabla^{k}(\tilde{g}-g)|_{\varphi}\leq C_{k}\bigg(1+\sum_{j=0}^{k}|\nabla^{j}(\tilde{g}-g)|_{\varphi}+\sum_{j=0}^{k}|\nabla^{j}\varphi|_{\varphi}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{k}|\nabla^{j}(\varphi-\tilde{\varphi})|_{\varphi}\bigg).

where
C_{k}>0
depends on the order of differentiation
k
.

Proof.

We return to the relation

(2.18)

\tilde{g}(u,v)-g(u,v)=\tilde{g}(u,v)\Big(-7b_{0}-Q(b_{0},b_{1},b_{3})\Big)+\star_{\varphi}{\mathcal{G}}(u,v),

where

(2.19)

Q(b_{0},b_{1},b_{3})=\bigg(14(b_{0})^{2}+\frac{2}{3}|b_{1}|^{2}_{\varphi}-\frac{1}{6}|b_{3}|^{2}_{\varphi}\bigg)+r(b_{0},b_{1},b_{3})

and
\lim_{|\gamma|\to 0}\frac{|r(b_{0},b_{1},b_{3})|_{\varphi}}{|\gamma|_{\varphi}^{2}}=0
.
The proof of the proposition involves taking the
k
-times derivative of (
2.18
). We illustrate the case
k=1
: the proof in the cases
k>1
follows almost exactly the same steps.

Taking the covariant derivative of (
2.18
) with respect to the Levi-Civita connection
\nabla=\nabla^{g}
,

(2.20)

|\nabla(\tilde{g}-g)|_{\varphi}\leq C|\nabla\tilde{g}|_{\varphi}\Big(|b_{0}|_{\varphi}+|Q(b_{0},b_{1},b_{3})|_{\varphi}\Big)+|\nabla(\star_{\varphi}{\mathcal{G}}_{ij})|_{\varphi}\\
+C|\tilde{g}|_{\varphi}\Big(|\nabla b_{0}|_{\varphi}+\big(|b_{0}|_{\varphi}+|b_{1}|_{\varphi}+|b_{3}|_{\varphi}\big)\big(|\nabla b_{0}|_{\varphi}+|\nabla b_{1}|_{\varphi}+|\nabla b_{3}|_{\varphi}\big)\Big),

where we write
\star_{\varphi}{\mathcal{G}}_{ij}
to denote the two-tensor
(u,v)\mapsto\star_{\varphi}{\mathcal{G}}(u,v)
. Computing the covariant derivative with respect to
g
and using a normal coordinate system centered at a point
p\in M
,

(2.21)

|\nabla(\star_{\varphi}{\mathcal{G}}_{ij})|_{\varphi}\leq C\Big((1+|\gamma|_{\varphi}+|\gamma|^{2}_{\varphi})|\nabla\gamma|_{\varphi}+(|\gamma|_{\varphi}+|\gamma|^{2}_{\varphi})|\nabla\varphi|_{\varphi}\Big).

Assuming that
|\nabla^{i}\gamma|_{\varphi}<1
for
i=0,1
and using the bounds on
b_{0}
,
b_{1}
, and
b_{3}
found above, we may combine (
2.21
) with (
2.20
) to find

(2.22)

|\nabla(\tilde{g}-g)|_{\varphi}\leq C\big((|\nabla\varphi|_{\varphi}|\gamma|_{\varphi}+|\nabla\gamma|_{\varphi})+|\nabla\tilde{g}|_{\varphi}|\gamma|_{\varphi}\\
+|\tilde{g}|_{\varphi}(|\nabla b_{0}|_{\varphi}+|\gamma|_{\varphi}\big(|\nabla b_{0}|_{\varphi}+|\nabla b_{1}|_{\varphi}+|\nabla b_{3}|_{\varphi}\big))\big).

By definition,
b_{0}=\langle\gamma,\varphi\rangle
, so

|\nabla b_{0}|_{\varphi}\leq C(|\gamma|_{\varphi}+|\nabla\gamma|_{\varphi})
.
By
[
35
, (2.11)-(2.12)]
and the assumption that
\nabla\varphi
is
O(1)
with respect to the
g
-norm, we have that
\star_{\varphi}\varphi
and
\nabla\star_{\varphi}\varphi
are also
O(1)
with respect to the
g
-norm. Note that
\varphi
is automatically
O(1)
with respect to the
g
-norm by
[
35
, (2.3)]
. So, by differentiating (
2.13
), we find that

|\nabla b_{1}|_{\varphi}\leq C(|\gamma|_{\varphi}+|\nabla\gamma|_{\varphi})
. Finally, by differentiating (
2.10
) and applying the bounds on
|\nabla b_{0}|
and
|\nabla b_{1}|
, we find that

|\nabla b_{3}|\leq C(|\gamma|_{\varphi}+|\nabla\gamma|_{\varphi}).

Applying these bounds on
|\nabla b_{0}|_{\varphi}
,
|\nabla b_{1}|_{\varphi}
, and
|\nabla b_{3}|_{\varphi}
to (
2.22
), we conclude that

|\nabla\tilde{g}|_{\varphi}\leq C(1+|\tilde{g}|_{\varphi}+|\nabla\tilde{g}|_{\varphi}+|\nabla\varphi|_{\varphi})(|\varphi-\tilde{\varphi}|_{\varphi}+|\nabla(\varphi-\tilde{\varphi})|_{\varphi}).

The proof in the case of larger values of
k
is almost identical, with the coefficients
C_{k}
depending on the number of terms that arise from the product rule under iterated differentiation.
∎

Remark 2.1
.

From (
2.17
), we can obtain estimates of the form

|\nabla^{k}(\tilde{g}-g)|_{\varphi}\leq C_{k}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{k}|\nabla^{j}(\varphi-\tilde{\varphi})|_{\varphi}\bigg),

where
C_{k}
will depend on a priori estimates on
|\nabla^{j}(\tilde{g}-g)|_{\varphi}
,
|\nabla^{j}(\tilde{\varphi}-\varphi)|_{\varphi}
, and
|\nabla^{j}\varphi|_{\varphi}
. These will vary depending on context, so we leave our expression in the more general form (
2.17
).

3.
Properties of AC
G_{2}
-shrinkers

3.1.
Basic features of closed AC
G_{2}
-structures

We define the asymptotic conicality of a shrinking, closed
G_{2}
-end along the lines of
[
29
, Definition 1.1]
and show that this definition implies the expected asymptotics of all relevant quantities.

Let
(M,\varphi)
be a 7-dimensional manifold with a closed
G_{2}
-structure. For the moment, we consider only its Riemannian structure, i.e. the data
(M,g_{\varphi})
. An
end
of
M
is an unbounded connected component
V
of
M\setminus K
for some compact subset
K
in
M
. We will consider ends
V
of
M
that are asymptotic to closed
G_{2}
-cones. A
closed
G_{2}
-cone
over a closed Riemannian 6-manifold
(\Sigma^{6},g_{\Sigma})
is a manifold
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
such that
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}=(0,\infty)\times\Sigma^{6}
, and the 3-form
\varphi_{C}
is a closed
G_{2}
-structure whose associated metric is
g_{C}=dr^{2}+r^{2}g_{\Sigma}
. For
a\geq 0
, let
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{a}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(a,\infty)\times\Sigma
and define the
dilation map

\rho_{\lambda}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0}
for
\lambda>0
, given by
\rho_{\lambda}(r,\sigma)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\lambda r,\sigma)
.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
V
be an end of
M
. We say that
(M,\varphi)
is
asymptotic to the
G_{2}
-cone
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},\varphi_{C})
along
V
if, for some
r_{0}>0
, there is a diffeomorphism
\Phi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\rightarrow V
such that
\lambda^{-3}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}\Phi^{*}\varphi\rightarrow\varphi_{C}
as
\lambda\rightarrow\infty
in
C^{3}_{\text{loc}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}},g_{C})
.

Lemma 3.1

(
[
29
, Lemma A.1]
)
.

If
V
is an end of
M
,
\Phi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{a}\rightarrow V
is a diffeomorphism for some
a>0
, and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then

(3.1)

\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow\infty}\lambda^{-3}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}\Phi^{*}\varphi=\varphi_{C}\;\;\text{ in }\;\;C^{k}_{loc}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{C}),

holds if and only if

\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}b^{l}||\nabla^{l}_{g_{C}}(\Phi^{*}\varphi-\varphi_{C})||_{C^{0}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{b},g_{C})}=0

for each
l=0,1,2,\ldots,k
.

Proof.

Let
k,\lambda\geq 1
and
b>a
. The statement follows from the identity

\sup_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{b}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{2b}}\Big|\nabla_{g_{C}}^{k}(\lambda^{-3}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}(\Phi^{*}\varphi-\varphi_{C}))\Big|_{g_{C}}=\sup_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{\lambda b}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{\lambda 2b}}\lambda^{k}\Big|\nabla_{g_{C}}^{k}(\Phi^{*}\varphi-\varphi_{C})\Big|_{g_{C}}.

Note that
\varphi_{C}
is not necessarily parallel with respect to
\nabla_{g_{C}}
and that
\varphi_{C}
is invariant under dilation and rescaling, i.e.
\lambda^{-3}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}\varphi_{C}=\varphi_{C}.

∎

We now show that asymptotic conicality of
G_{2}
-structures implies asymptotic conicality on the level of metrics.

Corollary 3.2
.

If
(M,\varphi)
is asymptotic to the
G_{2}
-cone
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},\varphi_{C})
in
C^{k}_{\text{loc}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{C})
, then
\lambda^{-2}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}\Phi^{*}g\to g_{C}
as
\lambda\to\infty
in
C^{k}_{\text{loc}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{C})
.

Proof.

Lemma
3.1
implies that

\sup_{x\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r}}|\nabla^{k}_{g_{C}}(\Phi^{*}\varphi-\varphi_{C})|(x)\rightarrow 0,

as
r\rightarrow\infty
. Thus, for each
k
, we can find an
r_{k}
such that
||\Phi^{*}\varphi-\varphi_{C}||_{C^{k}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{k}},g_{C})}<\epsilon_{k}
, as defined in Propositions
2.2
and
2.4
. Furthermore, the conicality of
\varphi_{C}
yields the decay
|\nabla^{j}\varphi_{C}|\rightarrow 0
for all derivatives of order
j>0
. Then Proposition
2.4
gives bounds of the form

|\nabla^{k}_{g_{C}}(\lambda^{-2}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}\Phi^{*}g-g_{C})|_{\varphi_{C}}\leq C_{k}|\nabla^{k}_{g_{C}}(\lambda^{-3}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}\Phi^{*}\varphi-\varphi_{C})|_{\varphi_{C}}.

This bound, combined with the fact that
\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}b^{k}||\nabla^{l}_{g_{C}}(\Phi^{*}\varphi-\varphi_{C})||_{C^{0}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{b},g_{C})}=0
, implies that
\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}b^{l}||\nabla^{l}_{g_{C}}(\Phi^{*}g-g_{C})||_{C^{0}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{b},g_{C})}=0
. According
[
29
, Lemma A.1]
, this is equivalent to
C^{k}_{\text{loc}}
-convergence, which concludes the proof of the corollary.
∎

An immediate consequence of Corollary
3.2
is the following version of
[
29
, Lemma A.1]
.

Corollary 3.3

(
[
29
, Lemma A.1]
)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a Riemannian manifold,
V
an end of
M
, and
\Phi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{a}\rightarrow V
a diffeomorphism for some
a>0
. For any nonnegative integer
k
we say that the property
(AC_{k})
holds if

(
AC_{k}
)

\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow\infty}\lambda^{-3}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}\Phi^{*}\varphi=\varphi_{C}\;\;\text{ in }\;\;C^{k}_{loc}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{C}).

Then

(a)

(

⁢
A

C
k

) implies that

\lambda^{-2}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}\Phi^{*}g\to g_{C}\text{ as }\lambda\to\infty,

which holds if and only if

\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}b^{l}||\nabla^{l}_{g_{C}}(\Phi^{*}g-g_{C})||_{C^{0}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{b},g_{C})}=0

for each
l=0,1,2,\ldots,k
.

(b)

If (
AC_{0}
) holds, then the metrics
\Phi^{*}g
and
g_{C}
are uniformly equivalent on
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{b}}
for any
b>a
, and, for all
\epsilon>0
, there exists
b>a
such that, for
(r,\sigma)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{b}
,

(3.2)

(1-\epsilon)|r-b|\leq\bar{r}_{b}(r,\sigma)\leq(1+\epsilon)|r-b|,

where
\bar{r}_{b}(x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=d_{\Phi^{*}g}(x,\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{b})
.

(c)

If (
AC_{2}
) holds, then for any
b>a
, there exists a constant
K=K(b,g_{\sigma})>0
such that

(3.3)

\sup_{x\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{b}}(\bar{r}_{b}^{2}(x)+1)|\Rm(\Phi^{*}g)|_{\Phi^{*}g}(x)\leq K.

Proof.

The first part of statement (a) is a restatement of Corollary
3.2
. The rest of the proof proceeds as in
[
29
]
.
∎

3.2.
Parametrizing closed AC
G_{2}
-shrinkers

A Laplacian soliton for the Laplacian flow of closed
G_{2}
-structures on
M
is a quadruple
(M,\varphi,X,\lambda)
satisfying

(3.4)

\Delta_{\varphi}\varphi=\lambda\varphi+{\mathcal{L}}_{X}\varphi,

where
\varphi
is a closed
G_{2}
-structure,
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and
X
is a vector field on
M
. The soliton is
shrinking
if
\lambda<0
,
expanding
if
\lambda>0
, and
steady
if
\lambda=0
. From this point onward, we consider only the case of shrinkers with dilation constant
\lambda=-3/2
, and we therefore suppress
\lambda
in all further notation.
There is no loss of generality in this assumption since rescaling a shrinking soliton
changes its dilation constant.

The shrinker equation then implies the following identity for the soliton metric.

Proposition 3.4

(
[
35
, Proposition 9.4]
)
.

The metric
g
associated with a shrinking Laplacian soliton
(M,\varphi,X)
in local coordinates satisfies the equation

(3.5)

-\Ric_{ij}-\frac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}-2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}=-\frac{1}{2}g_{ij}+\frac{1}{2}({\mathcal{L}}_{X}g)_{ij}

and the vector field
X
satisfies
d^{*}(X\lrcorner\varphi)=0
.

When
X
is the gradient of some potential function
f
, we call the triple
(M,\varphi,f)
a
gradient
shrinking Laplacian soliton. For short, we may call it a
G_{2}
-shrinker. In this case, the identity (
3.5
) can be rewritten as

(3.6)

-\Ric_{ij}-\frac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}-2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}=-\frac{1}{2}g_{ij}+\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}f.

We can regard the previous equation as a perturbation of the shrinking gradient Ricci soliton equations

-\Ric_{ij}=-\frac{1}{2}g_{ij}+\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}f

by terms quadratic in the torsion tensor
T
.
We use the previous equation to prove that any gradient shrinking Laplacian soliton with quadratic curvature decay admits a reparametrization that is compatible in a suitable sense with the level sets of the potential function
f
.

Lemma 3.5

(
[
29
, Lemma A.2]
)
.

Suppose that
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}},\varphi,f)
is a gradient shrinking Laplacian soliton end with associated metric
g
, which satisfies

(3.7)

(\bar{r}^{2}(x)+1)|\Rm(g)|\leq K

for some constant
K>0
, where
\bar{r}(x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=d_{g}(x,\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{2r_{0}})
, and in addition
\lim_{r_{i}\rightarrow\infty}\bar{r}(r_{i},\sigma_{i})\rightarrow\infty
for all sequences
(r_{i},\sigma_{i})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
with
r_{i}\rightarrow\infty
as
i\rightarrow\infty
. Then there exists
s_{0}>0
, a closed
6
-dimensional manifold
\bar{\Sigma}
, and a map
\bar{\Phi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{s_{0}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
, where
{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{s_{0}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(s_{0},\infty)\times\bar{\Sigma}
,
with the following properties:

(1)

\bar{\Phi}
is a diffeomorphism onto its image, and
\bar{\Phi}({\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{s_{0}})
is an end of the closure of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{2r_{0}}
.

(2)

For all
(s,\bar{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{s_{0}}
,

\bar{f}(s,\bar{\sigma})=\frac{s^{2}}{4},\;\;\;\text{ and }\;\;\;\frac{\partial\bar{\Phi}}{\partial s}=\bar{f}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\bar{\nabla}\bar{f}}{|\bar{\nabla}\bar{f}|^{2}_{\bar{g}}}.

(3)

There exists a constant
N>0
such that, for all
(s,\bar{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{2s_{0}}
,

N^{-1}(s-1)\leq\bar{s}(s,\bar{\sigma})\leq N(s+1)\;\;\;\text{ and }\;\;\;(s^{2}+1)|\Rm(\bar{g})|_{\bar{g}}(s,\bar{\sigma})\leq N.

Here,
\bar{f}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=f\circ\bar{\Phi}
,
\bar{g}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\bar{\Phi}^{*}g
, and
\bar{s}(x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=d_{\bar{g}}(x,\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{2s_{0}})
.

Proof.

The proof is a minor adaptation of
[
29
, Lemma A.2]
. More specifically, it is only necessary to find some
r_{0}>0
such that
f
is proper and has no critical points on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
: at this point the proof of Kotschwar–Wang can be repeated verbatim.
Notice that due to the quadratic curvature decay assumed, the left-hand side of (
3.6
) decays at
O(r^{-2})
and hence

\nabla^{2}f-\tfrac{1}{2}g

decays quadratically as in the case of gradient Ricci shrinkers.
We can therefore (mostly) follow the argument of Kotschwar–Wang: Let
x\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{2r_{0}}
and
\gamma\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}[0,l]\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{2r_{0}}
be a unit-speed, minimizing geodesic with
\gamma(0)=x_{0}\in\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{2r_{0}}
,
\gamma(l)=x
, and
l=\bar{r}(x)
.
Combining the quadratic decay of
\nabla^{2}f-\tfrac{1}{2}g
with

\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}(f\circ\gamma)(t)=\nabla^{2}f(\dot{\gamma}(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))

yields that

\frac{1}{2}-\frac{K}{t^{2}+1}\leq\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}(f\circ\gamma)(t)\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{K}{t^{2}+1}.

Integrating once yields

\frac{\bar{r}(\gamma(t))}{2}-N_{1}\leq\frac{d}{dt}(f\circ\gamma)(t)\leq\frac{\bar{r}(\gamma(t))}{2}+N_{1},

where
N_{1}
depends on
K
and
\sup_{\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{2r_{0}}}|\nabla f|
.
In particular, the lower bound implies that

\frac{d}{dt}(f\circ\gamma)(l)=\nabla f(\gamma(l))\cdot\dot{\gamma}(l)\geq\frac{\bar{r}(x)}{4}.

Thus
\nabla f
cannot be zero for sufficiently large
x
. Integrating the previous inequalities yields

(3.8)

\frac{\bar{r}^{2}(\gamma(t))}{4}-N_{2}\,(\bar{r}(x)+1)\leq f(x)\leq\frac{\bar{r}^{2}(\gamma(t))}{4}+N_{2}\,(\bar{r}(x)+1),

where
N_{2}
depends on
K
and
\sup_{\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{2r_{0}}}(|f|+|\nabla f|)
. This implies that
f
is proper. The previous inequalities together with the properness of
f
and the non-vanishing of its gradient are enough to carry out the remainder of the argument in
[
29
, Lemma A.2]
.
∎

Remark 3.1
.

Kotschwar–Wang used the fact that gradient Ricci shrinkers satisfy the identity

R+{\left\lvert\nabla f\right\rvert}^{2}=f
to establish that
f
has no critical values on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
for
r_{0}
sufficiently large. There is an analogous but more complicated identity for gradient Laplacian shrinkers that we discuss shortly, but we did not make use of this identity in the proof above.

Henceforth, we will assume we are working in the parametrization described in Lemma
3.5
, but for the sake of clarity we will relabel
{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{s_{0}}
as
{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{0}}
and continue to refer to the radial parameter on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{0}}
as “
r
”, instead of “
s
” as in the statement of the lemma. In the rest of the section, we will write the reparametrized shrinker as
({\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{0}},\varphi,f)
, where
\varphi=\bar{\Phi}^{*}\varphi
, etc., to reduce notational complexity. However, this convention will not carry over to the next section.

We now prove an estimate for the growth of an important quantity associated to a shrinker.

Lemma 3.6
.

On any AC gradient Laplacian shrinker
({\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{0}},\varphi,f)
,

(3.9)

R+|\nabla f|^{2}-f=O(\log r).

Proof.

Let
\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{7}\}
be a system of geodesic normal coordinates in the neighborhood of a point
p
. Taking the divergence of (
3.6
) yields

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\nabla_{j}\left(\Ric_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}+\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}f-\frac{1}{2}g_{ij}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{i}R+\nabla_{i}\Delta f-R_{jijk}\nabla_{k}f+\nabla_{j}\left(\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\right),

where the second equality uses the contracted Bianchi identity. Taking the trace of (
3.6
) yields

\Delta f=-\frac{2}{3}R+\frac{7}{2},

where we have used the identity
-R=|T|^{2}
, which holds for any closed
G_{2}
-structure. Substituting this expression for
\Delta f
into the previous equation we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}\nabla_{i}R+R_{ik}\nabla_{k}f+\nabla_{j}\left(\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}\nabla_{i}R+\bigg(-\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}\delta_{ik}-2T_{i}^{\;l}T_{lk}+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{ik}-\nabla_{i}\nabla_{k}f\bigg)\nabla_{k}f

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\nabla_{j}\left(\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}\nabla_{i}R+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{i}f-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{i}|\nabla f|^{2}-\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}\nabla_{i}f-2T_{i}^{\;l}T_{lk}\nabla_{k}f

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\nabla_{j}\left(\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{i}R+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{i}f-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{i}|\nabla f|^{2}-\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}\nabla_{i}f-2T_{i}^{\;l}T_{lk}\nabla_{k}f+\nabla_{j}2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj},

where in the second equality we again used (
3.6
), and in the fourth that
R=-|T|^{2}
.
We can rewrite the last equality as

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}(R+|\nabla f|^{2}-f)=-\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}\nabla_{i}f-4T_{i}^{\;l}T_{lk}\nabla_{k}f+4\nabla_{j}T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}.

At this point, a further reduction can be made. By
[
35
, Proposition 9.4]
,
d^{*}(\nabla f\lrcorner\varphi)=0
. However, by
[
35
, (9.10)]

d^{*}(\nabla f\lrcorner\varphi)=(\curl(\nabla f))^{\flat}+(\nabla f\lrcorner T)^{\flat}.

Since
\nabla f
is a gradient,
\curl(\nabla f)=0
and thus
\nabla f\lrcorner T=0
. This allows us to remove the term involving
\nabla f\lrcorner T
from the right hand side.

(3.10)

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}(R+|\nabla f|^{2}-f)=-\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}\nabla_{i}f+4\nabla_{j}T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}.

Note that the right-hand side of this equation is
O(r^{-1})
by Lemma
3.3
and Lemma
3.5
, since

\bigg|-\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}\nabla_{i}f+4\nabla_{j}T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\bigg|\leq C(|T|^{2}|\nabla f|+|T||\nabla T|).

Now, select a point
x_{0}
on
\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{0}}
. For any
x\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{2r_{0}}
, take a unit-speed, minimizing geodesic
\gamma\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}[0,l]\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{0}}
with
\gamma(0)=x_{0}
and
\gamma(l)=x
. Integrating (
3.10
), we see

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{l}\frac{d}{dt}\big((R+|\nabla f|^{2}-f)\circ\gamma(t)\big)dt\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{l}|\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}(R+|\nabla f|^{2}-f)|dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{l}C\big((|T|^{2}|\nabla f|+|T||\nabla T|)(\gamma(t)\big)|\dot{\gamma}(t)|dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\bar{r}(x)}\frac{C}{(t+1)^{-1}}dt

\displaystyle=C\log(r),

where
\bar{r}(x)
is the distance of
x
to the boundary, and
r
is the radial parameter. The final equality comes from the comparison (3) in Lemma
3.5
.
∎

In the following remark, we recall some well-known properties of complete gradient Ricci shrinkers
and point out that they all fail in general on complete gradient Laplacian shrinkers.

Remark 3.2
.

On any complete gradient Ricci shrinker
(M,g,f)
with dilation constant
\lambda=-\tfrac{1}{2}

we may (by adding a constant to the shrinker potential function
f
) assume that

(3.11)

R+{\left\lvert\nabla f\right\rvert}^{2}-f=0.

Then
(M,g,f)
has the following properties:

(1)

M
has finite fundamental group
[
42
, Theorem 1.1]
.

(2)

The scalar curvature
R
is strictly positive (except on the Gaussian shrinker on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
)
[
39
, Theorem 3]
.

(3)

\sqrt{f}
is a
\tfrac{1}{2}
-Lipschitz function, that is, it satisfies
{\left\lvert\sqrt{f(x)}-\sqrt{f(x_{0}})\right\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}r(x)
, where
r(x)
denotes the distance between
x
and the reference point
x_{0}
.

(4)

f
satisfies the estimate
f(x)\geq\frac{1}{4}(r(x)-c_{1})^{2}
, where
c_{1}
is a positive constant depending on
n
and on the metric
g
on the unit ball
B_{x_{0}(1)}

[
7
, Proposition 2.1]
.

(5)

g
has at most Euclidean volume growth
[
7
, Theorem 1.2]
.

(6)

g
has at least linear volume growth
[
37
, Theorem 6.1]
.

Fowdar
[
15
, §5.2]
constructed explicit complete gradient Laplacian shrinkers on
7
-manifolds of the form

\mathbb{R}\times P^{6}
where
P^{6}
is the total space of a principal
T^{2}
-bundle over a hyperKähler
4
-manifold
(M^{4},\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{3})

endowed with a connection
\theta=(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
whose curvature is
(\omega_{2},\omega_{3})
.
One particular case that arises is when
M^{4}
is a flat
4
-torus
\mathbb{T}^{4}
and
P^{6}
is the Iwasawa manifold
I^{6}
,
a compact complex nilmanifold with first Betti number
b^{1}(I^{6})=4
. The closed
3
-form
\varphi

\varphi=dt\wedge\left(\sqrt{3}\,e^{\frac{t}{6}}\,\omega_{1}+\tfrac{1}{3}e^{\frac{t}{3}}\,\theta_{1}\wedge\theta_{2}\right)-e^{\frac{t}{3}}(\omega_{3}\wedge\theta_{1}-\omega_{2}\wedge\theta_{2})

is a gradient Laplacian shrinker on
{\mathbb{R}}\times I^{6}
with dilation constant
\lambda=-\tfrac{1}{2}
and soliton vector field
X=\tfrac{5}{2}\partial_{t}
.
The metric induced by
\varphi
is

g_{\varphi}=dt^{2}+\sqrt{3}e^{\frac{t}{6}}\,g_{\mathbb{T}^{4}}+\tfrac{1}{3}e^{\frac{t}{3}}(\theta_{1}\otimes\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}\otimes\theta_{2}).

g_{\varphi}
defines a complete metric on
\mathbb{R}\times I^{6}
with two ends, one of finite volume and the other with exponential volume growth; it has constant negative scalar curvature
R\equiv-\tfrac{3}{4}
;
the soliton potential is the linear function
f=\tfrac{5}{2}t
.

This example shows that none of the properties of complete gradient Ricci shrinkers
recalled above holds for complete gradient Laplacian shrinkers.

4.
Setting up the Parabolic Problem

In order to apply the parabolic Carleman estimates, we must obtain Laplacian flows from our
G_{2}
-shrinkers whose limits at the singular time coincide. Thus, when we construct a flow from our shrinker, we obtain a
dynamical limit
whose relationship with the
rescaling limit
given in Definition
3.1
is unknown. In this section, we construct a flow such that the dynamical limit coincides with the rescaling limit. In particular, this implies that given any two
G_{2}
-shrinkers asymptotic to the same cone in the sense of Definition
3.1
, flows can be constructed on the ends of each shrinker that coincide at the terminal time-slice.

The general outline of the argument proceeds as follows. A version of
[
29
, Proposition 2.1]
, Proposition
4.1
, establishes a Laplacian flow on the end
V\subset M
which interpolates between the original shrinker
\varphi
and a smooth limit
\varphi_{D}
, which is a
G_{2}
-cone. Then, adapting an argument of Conlon–Deruelle–Sun in
[
8
]
, we show that
\varphi
has asymptotic cone
\varphi_{D}
with respect to the
C^{k}
-topology associated to the metric
g_{\varphi_{D}}
. Finally, we show that the topology of convergence in
C^{k}_{loc}(V,g_{\varphi_{D}})
is equivalent to that of convergence in
C^{k}_{loc}(V,g_{\varphi_{C}})
—and hence that
\varphi_{C}=\varphi_{D}
.

The following proposition is an adaptation of
[
29
, Proposition 2.1]
which constructs a shrinking flow associated to
\varphi
, establishes several of its important properties, and establishes the existence of the dynamic limit
\varphi_{D}
. In the sequel, we often consider the backward Laplacian flow and correspondingly the “time-reversed” spacetime
{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau)
, for a time parameter
t<0
,
\tau=-t
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Given a gradient Laplacian shrinker
(M,\varphi,f)
asymptotic to the regular cone
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},\varphi_{C})
along the end
V\subset M
, there exist constants
K_{0},N_{0},
and
r_{0}>0
, and a smooth family of maps
\Psi_{\tau}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
defined for
\tau\in(0,1]
satisfying:

(1)

For each
\tau\in(0,1]
,
\Psi_{\tau}
is a diffeomorphism onto its image and
\Psi_{\tau}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}})
is an end of
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}}
.

(2)

By an abuse of notation, let
\varphi
also denote the pullback of
\varphi
to
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
. The family of closed
G_{2}
-forms
\varphi(x,\tau)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\tau^{3/2}\Psi_{\tau}^{*}\varphi(x)
is a self-similar solution to the backward Laplacian flow

(4.1)

\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial\tau}=-\Delta_{\varphi}\varphi,

for
\tau\in(0,1]
that extends smoothly as
\tau\searrow 0
to
\varphi(x,0)\equiv\varphi_{D}(x)
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{0}}^{\Sigma}
. Furthermore, the family of metrics
g(x,\tau)=\tau\Psi_{\tau}^{*}g(x)
evolves by the modified backward Ricci flow

(4.2)

\frac{\partial g_{ij}}{\partial\tau}=2\Ric_{ij}+\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+4T_{i}^{\;l}T_{lj},

and each
g(\cdot,\tau)
is the metric induced by
\varphi(\cdot,\tau)
. Consequently, we find that the family
\{g(x,\tau)\}_{\tau\in(0,1]}
extends smoothly as
\tau\searrow 0
to the conical metric
g(x,0)\equiv g_{D}(x)
, induced by
\varphi_{D}
.

(3)

For all nonnegative integers
m
we have the curvature decay estimate

(4.3)

\sup_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,1]}(r^{m+2}+1)|\nabla^{m}\Rm(g)|_{\varphi(\tau)}\leq K_{0},

where
r
denotes the radial function on the cone
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0}
and the norm and covariant derivatives are taken with respect to the metric
g(\tau)
induced by
\varphi(\tau)
.

(4)

Let
f
be the function on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times(0,1]
defined by
f(x,\tau)=\Psi_{\tau}^{*}f(x)
. The product
\tau f
converges smoothly as
\tau\rightarrow 0
to
r^{2}/4
on
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{a}}
for all
a>r_{0}
, and satisfies

(4.4)

r^{2}-N_{0}\log(r)\leq 4\tau f\leq r^{2}+N_{0}\log(r),\;\;\;\tau\nabla f=\frac{r}{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial r},

and

(4.5)

\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}(\tau f)=-\tau O(\log r),\;\;\tau^{2}|\nabla f|^{2}-\tau f=-\tau^{2}O(\log r),

(4.6)

\tau\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}f=\frac{g_{ij}}{2}-\tau\Ric_{ij}-\frac{\tau}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}-2\tau T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}.

on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times(0,1]
for some constant
N_{0}>0
.

Remark 4.1
.

In general, we will not distinguish between objects (e.g.
\varphi,g,f
, etc.) defined on the end
V\subset M
and their pullbacks to
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
.

4.0.1.
Proof of Proposition
4.1

We adopt the format of the proof in
[
29
]
, separating the proof of the proposition into the proofs of several claims. For the proofs of the first two claims, we will parametrize the shrinker
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}},\varphi,f)
by the local diffeomorphism
\bar{\Phi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{1}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
defined in Lemma
3.5
, and consider the pulled-back shrinker
({\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{1}},\bar{\varphi},\bar{f})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=({\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{1}},\bar{\Phi}^{*}\varphi,\bar{\Phi}^{*}f)
.

We first prove
[
29
, Claim 2.3]
which relates the integral curves of
\bar{\nabla}\bar{f}
to the radial trajectories. The proof is largely the same with one small modification to account for the fact that the right-hand side of (
3.9
) does not vanish and in fact grows logarithmically in the radial variable.

Claim 4.2
.

[
29
, Claim 2.3]

There exists
r_{2}>r_{1}
depending only on
r_{1}
,
K
(as in part (c) of Lemma
3.3
), and
N
(as in part (3) of Lemma
3.5
), and a one-parameter family of local diffeomorphisms
\bar{\Psi}_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{2}}\rightarrow\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{2}}
defined for
s\geq 0
, which satisfy

(4.7)

\frac{\partial\bar{\Psi}_{s}}{\partial s}=\bar{\nabla}\bar{f}\circ\bar{\Psi}_{s}\;\;\textrm{ and }\;\;\bar{\Psi}_{0}=\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{2}}}.

Moreover, for all
(r,\bar{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{2}}
,
r_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=r\circ\bar{\Psi}_{s}
satisfies

(4.8)

(r-1)e^{s/2}+1\leq r_{s}(r,\bar{\sigma})\leq(r+1)e^{s/2}-1.

Proof.

The proof of
[
29
, Claim 2.3]
gives the existence of
\bar{\Psi}_{s}
and the rest of their argument applies directly with the following small change: when calculating the derivative of
r_{s}
along the flow, we must use identity (
3.9
)
in place of the conservation law for gradient Ricci shrinkers

{\left\lvert\nabla f\right\rvert}^{2}=f-R
. More specifically, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial r_{s}}{\partial s}

\displaystyle=(\bar{f}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\bar{\nabla}\bar{f}|_{\bar{g}}^{2})\circ\bar{\Psi}_{s}

\displaystyle=\left(\bar{f}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\bar{f}-\tfrac{1}{3}\bar{R}+O(\log r)\,)\right)\circ\bar{\Psi}_{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{s}}{2}-\frac{2\bar{\Psi}_{s}^{*}\bar{R}}{3r_{s}}+O(r_{s}^{-1}\log r_{s})

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{s}}{2}+o(1).

Thus, for
r_{2}>r_{1}
sufficiently large, the following inequality holds

\frac{1}{2}(r_{s}-1)\leq\frac{\partial r_{s}}{\partial s}\leq\frac{1}{2}(r_{s}+1)

on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{2}}
. The rest of the proof follows as in
[
29
]
.
∎

Set
s(t)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=-\log(-t)
for
t<0
and define the family of closed
G_{2}
-structures

(4.9)

\bar{\varphi}_{t}=(-t)^{3/2}\bar{\Psi}_{s(t)}^{*}\bar{\varphi}

on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{2}}\times[-1,0)
.
We claim that
\bar{\varphi}_{t}
is a solution of Laplacian flow with initial condition

\bar{\varphi}_{-1}=\bar{\varphi}
.

To this end we compute

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\bar{\varphi}_{t}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2}(-t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\Psi}_{s(t)}^{*}\bar{\varphi}+(-t)^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{\Psi}_{s(t)}^{*}({\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{\nabla}\bar{f}}\bar{\varphi})\frac{\partial s}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=(-t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\Psi}_{s(t)}^{*}\bigg(-\frac{3}{2}\bar{\varphi}+{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{\nabla}\bar{f}}\bar{\varphi}\bigg)

\displaystyle=(-t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\Psi}_{s(t)}^{*}(\Delta_{\bar{\varphi}}\bar{\varphi})

\displaystyle=\big((-t)^{\frac{3}{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{3}}(\Delta_{\bar{\Psi}_{s(t)}^{*}\bar{\varphi}}\bar{\Psi}_{s(t)}^{*}\bar{\varphi})

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\varphi_{t}}\varphi_{t},

where the final equality follows from the scaling behavior of the Hodge Laplacian, (
2.3
).

The metric
\bar{g}_{t}
associated to
\bar{\varphi}_{t}
is given by
\bar{g}_{t}=-t\bar{\Psi}_{s(t)}^{*}\bar{g}
.
Since
\bar{\varphi}_{t}
evolves by Laplacian flow we know from
[
35
, (3.6)]
that

\bar{g}_{t}
evolves by the equation

\frac{\partial\bar{g}_{t_{ij}}}{\partial t}=-2\bar{\Ric}_{ij}-\frac{2}{3}|\bar{T}|^{2}\bar{g}_{t_{ij}}-4\tilde{T}_{i}^{\;k}\tilde{T}_{kj},

where all metric quantities on the right-hand side are those associated to the time-dependent metric
\bar{g}_{t}
. This evolution equation can also be computed directly by appealing to (
3.5
) and the scaling identities for Ricci curvature and torsion.

We now show that
\bar{\varphi}_{t}
induces a Laplacian flow on the original parametrization. Define the following family of local diffeomorphisms:

\Psi_{s}=\bar{\Phi}\circ\bar{\Psi}_{s}\circ\bar{\Phi}^{-1}.

By pulling back (
4.9
) by
(\bar{\Phi}^{-1})^{*}
, there exists
r_{3}>0
such that we obtain the following solution to Laplacian flow:

(4.10)

\varphi_{t}=(-t)^{3/2}\Psi_{s(t)}^{*}\varphi,

on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[-1,0)
with
s(t)=-\log(-t)
and
\varphi_{-1}=\varphi
, the closed shrinking
G_{2}
-structure under the original parametrization.

We prove a curvature estimate that is uniform in time, first for the special parametrization
({\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{2}},\bar{\varphi}_{t})
and then convert it into an estimate on
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}},\varphi_{t})
.

Claim 4.3
.

[
29
, Claim 2.4]

For all
m\geq 0
, there exists a constant
K_{m}
which depends only on
m
and the constant
K
in Lemma
3.3
such that the curvature tensor of
g_{t}=-t\Psi^{*}_{s(t)}g
satisfies

(4.11)

\sup_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[-1,0)}(r^{m+2}(x)+1)\Big|\nabla^{m}\Rm(g_{t})\Big|_{g_{t}}\leq K_{m}.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.3
(c) and the scaling argument employed on
[
29
, pg. 10]
, we have the following uniform curvature estimate

(4.12)

\sup_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{2}}\times[-1,0)}(r^{2}(x)+1)|\Rm(\bar{g}_{t})|_{\bar{g}_{t}}(x,t)\leq 8K.

By the local Shi-type derivative estimates in
[
36
, Theorem 6.3]
and using the fact that
|\nabla T|\leq C|\Rm|
,

|\nabla^{m}\Rm(\bar{g}_{t})|_{\bar{g}_{t}}(x,t)\leq K_{m}\;\;\text{ on }\;\;({\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{2r_{2}}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{4r_{2}})\times[-1/2,0),

for each
m\geq 0
, where
K_{m}
depends on
m
and
K
. As in
[
29
, §2.2.3]
, it follows from (
4.8
) and a scaling argument that

(4.13)

\sup_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\bar{\Sigma}}_{r_{2}}\times[-1,0)}(r^{m+2}(x)+1)\Big|\nabla^{m}\Rm(\bar{g}_{t})\Big|_{\bar{g}_{t}}\leq K_{m}.

We can pull back this equation by
(\Phi^{-1})^{*}
to obtain

\sup_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[-1,0)}((2f^{1/2})^{m+2}(x)+1)\Big|\nabla^{m}\Rm(g_{t})\Big|_{g_{t}}\leq K_{m}.

The estimates (
3.8
) and Corollary
3.3
allow us to replace
2f^{1/2}
by the radial parameter
r
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
at the cost of possibly increasing
K_{m}
, which now depends on
N_{2}
. This yields the desired inequality, (
4.11
).
∎

We may now obtain a smooth limit
\varphi_{D}=\varphi_{0}
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times\{0\}
as
t\nearrow 0
. This follows as in
[
35
, Theorem 5.1, Claim 5.3]
, by using compactness of
\Sigma
and finding a finite atlas for
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
.

We now prove the analogues of the identities obtained in Section 2.2.4 in
[
29
]
. For notational clarity, we now write the original potential
f
as
\mathring{f}
. We suppress the subscript, and simply refer to the time-dependent pair
f\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Psi^{*}_{s(t)}\mathring{f}
and
\varphi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\varphi_{t}
, which together form the structure of a shrinking gradient Laplacian soliton
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
with constant
-3/(2(-t))
in place of
-3/2
. All quantities associated to the metric
\mathring{g}
will be denoted with a
\circ
accent, e.g.
\mathring{R}
,
\mathring{T}
, etc.

Claim 4.4
.

On
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[-1,0)
,
f
satisfies

(4.14)

\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}=|\nabla_{g}f|_{g}^{2},\;\;\textrm{ and }\;\;\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}f=-\frac{g_{ij}}{2t}-\Ric_{ij}-\frac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}-2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}.

Proof.

Differentiating
f=\Psi_{s(t)}^{*}\mathring{f}
with respect to time yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}(x,t)=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\big(\mathring{f}(\Psi_{s(t)}(x))\big)

\displaystyle=(d\mathring{f})_{\Psi_{s(t)}(x)}\bigg(\frac{\partial\Psi_{s(t)}}{\partial t}(x)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(-t)}\mathring{g}(\mathring{\nabla}\mathring{f},\mathring{\nabla}\mathring{f})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(-t)^{2}}g((-t)\nabla f,(-t)\nabla f)

\displaystyle=|\nabla_{g}f|^{2}_{g}.

In the third line, we used the rescaling properties stated in Lemma
2.1
.

We now calculate the transformation of Ricci curvature and torsion under the time-dependent pair
(\varphi(t),g(t))
.

\displaystyle\Ric(g)_{ij}+\frac{1}{3}|T|_{g}^{2}g_{ij}+2g^{kl}T_{ik}T_{lj}

\displaystyle=\Psi_{s(t)}^{*}\bigg((\Ric(\mathring{g})_{ij}+\frac{1}{3}(-t)^{-1}|\mathring{T}|_{\mathring{g}}^{2}(-t)\mathring{g}_{ij}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+2(-t)^{-1}\mathring{g}^{kl}(-t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathring{T}_{ik}(-t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathring{T}_{lj}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{s(t)}^{*}\bigg(\Ric(\mathring{g})_{ij}+\frac{1}{3}|\mathring{T}|_{\mathring{g}}^{2}\mathring{g}_{ij}+2\mathring{g}^{kl}\mathring{T}_{ik}\mathring{T}_{lj}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{s(t)}^{*}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\mathring{g}_{ij}-\mathring{\nabla}_{i}\mathring{\nabla}_{j}\mathring{f}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(-t)}g_{ij}-\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}f

This proves the third identity.
∎

Corollary 4.5
.

If
(x,t)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[-1,0)
and
p\in\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
, there exist time-independent constants
N_{3},N_{4}>0
such that
-tf
satisfies

(4.15)

\frac{\mathrm{dist}_{g_{t}}(p,x)}{2}-tN_{3}\leq|\nabla(-tf)|\leq\frac{\mathrm{dist}_{g_{t}}(p,x)}{2}+tN_{3},

and

(4.16)

\frac{\mathrm{dist}_{g_{t}}(p,x)^{2}}{4}-tN_{4}(\mathrm{dist}_{g_{t}}(p,x)+1)\leq-tf\leq\frac{\mathrm{dist}_{g_{t}}(p,x)^{2}}{4}+tN_{4}(\mathrm{dist}_{g_{t}}(p,x)+1),

where all quantities are measured with respect to
g_{t}
.

Proof.

Integrating the time-dependent soliton identities exactly as in the derivation of (
3.8
) yields the estimates.
∎

Proposition 4.6
.

On
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[-1,0)
,
f
satisfies

(4.17)

|\nabla f|_{g}^{2}-\frac{f}{(-t)}=O(\log r).

Proof.

We examine the identity derived in Lemma
3.6
:

\mathring{\nabla}_{i}(\mathring{R}+|\mathring{\nabla}\mathring{f}|_{\mathring{g}}^{2}-\mathring{f})=-\frac{2}{3}|\mathring{T}|_{\mathring{g}}^{2}\mathring{\nabla}_{i}\mathring{f}+4\mathring{\nabla}_{j}\mathring{T}_{i}^{\;k}\mathring{T}_{kj}.

Pull back by
\Psi_{s(t)}^{*}
and divide both sides by
(-t)
.

\frac{1}{(-t)}\nabla_{i}(\Psi_{s(t)}^{*}\mathring{R}+|\nabla f|_{\Psi_{s(t)}^{*}\mathring{g}}^{2}-f)=\frac{1}{(-t)}\bigg(-\frac{2}{3}|\Psi_{s(t)}^{*}\mathring{T}|_{\Psi_{s(t)}^{*}\mathring{g}}^{2}\mathring{\nabla}_{i}f+4\mathring{\nabla}_{j}\Psi_{s(t)}^{*}\mathring{T}_{i}^{\;k}\Psi_{s(t)}^{*}\mathring{T}_{kj})\bigg)

Using the rescaling identities, this equation can be rewritten in terms of the time-dependent quantities as

\nabla_{i}\bigg(R+|\nabla f|^{2}_{g}-\frac{f}{(-t)}\bigg)=\frac{1}{(-t)}\bigg(-\frac{2}{3}(-t)|T|^{2}_{g}\nabla_{i}(-tf)+(-t)4\nabla_{j}T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\bigg).

As before, select a point
x_{0}
on
\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
. For any
(x,t)\in\overline{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{2r_{3}}}\times[-1,0)
, take a unit speed geodesic
\gamma\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}[0,l]\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
with respect to
g_{t}
such that
\gamma(0)=x_{0}
and
\gamma(l)=x
. Integrating the time-dependent identity along the geodesic
\gamma
, we obtain

\int_{0}^{l}\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}\bigg(R+|\nabla f|^{2}_{g}-\frac{f}{(-t)}\bigg)ds=\int_{0}^{l}\dot{\gamma}\lrcorner\bigg(-\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}_{g}\nabla_{i}(-tf)+4\nabla_{j}T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\bigg)ds

Let
{\mathscr{E}}=R+|\nabla f|^{2}_{g}-\frac{f}{(-t)}
.

\displaystyle|{\mathscr{E}}(x,t)-{\mathscr{E}}(x_{0},t)|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{l}(|T|_{g}^{2}+|\nabla T|_{g}|T|_{g})|\nabla(-tf)|_{g}|\dot{\gamma}|_{g}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{l}(|T|_{g}^{2})|\nabla(-tf)|_{g}|\dot{\gamma}|_{g}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{l}s^{-2}sds

\displaystyle=C\log(\mathrm{dist}_{g_{t}}(p,x)).

The last inequality comes from the uniform curvature estimate (
4.12
). It remains to show that
\mathrm{dist}_{g_{t}}(p,x)
is comparable to the radial parameter
r
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
. Notice that scaling of the metric gives us

\mathrm{dist}_{g_{t}}(p,x)=\sqrt{-t}\mathrm{dist}_{\mathring{g}}(\Psi_{s(t)}(p),\Psi_{s(t)}(x)).

Arguing as in the proof of Claim
4.3
and invoking the estimates (
3.8
) and Corollary
3.3
, which tell us that
\sqrt{-t}r(\Psi_{s(t)}(x))\simeq r
. We conclude that

{\mathscr{E}}(x,t)=O(\log(r(x))).

Note that
{\mathscr{E}}
is time dependent, but is uniformly bounded for all
t
by a constant times
\log r
.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

On
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[-1,0)
,

(4.18)

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(-tf)=-t({\mathscr{E}}-R),

(4.19)

|\nabla^{k}{\mathscr{E}}(x,t)|=O(r(x)^{-k})\;\;\;\text{for }k\geq 1,

and

(4.20)

|\partial_{t}\nabla^{k}{\mathscr{E}}(x,t)|=O(r(x)^{-(k+1)})\;\;\text{for }k\geq 0

Proof.

We prove the first inequality using the first identity in Claim
4.4
.

\displaystyle-t\left(|\nabla f|^{2}+\frac{f}{t}\right)

\displaystyle=-t({\mathscr{E}}-R)

\displaystyle-t(\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}+\frac{f}{t})

\displaystyle=-t({\mathscr{E}}-R)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(-tf)

\displaystyle=-t({\mathscr{E}}-R).

Now, recall the formula

(4.21)

\nabla_{i}\bigg(R+|\nabla f|^{2}_{g}-\frac{f}{(-t)}\bigg)=-\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}_{g}\nabla_{i}(-tf)+4\nabla_{j}T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj},

and note that by the asymptotically conical condition,

|\nabla_{i}{\mathscr{E}}|=\bigg|-\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}_{g}\nabla_{i}(-tf)+4\nabla_{j}T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\bigg|_{g_{t}}=O(r^{-1}).

By the asymptotics of
(g_{t})^{-1}
,

|\nabla_{l_{1}}\cdots\nabla_{l_{k}}{\mathscr{E}}|=\bigg|\nabla_{l_{1}}\cdots\nabla_{l_{k}}\bigg(-\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}_{g}\nabla_{i}(-tf)+4\nabla_{j}T_{i}^{\;l}T_{lj}\bigg)\bigg|_{g_{t}}=O(r^{-k-1}).

This yields (
4.19
). Finally, we differentiate (
4.21
) with respect to time

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\nabla_{i}\bigg(R+|\nabla f|^{2}_{g}-\frac{f}{(-t)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\bigg(-\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}_{g}\nabla_{i}(-tf)+4\nabla_{j}T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\bigg)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{3}\partial_{t}(|T|^{2}_{g})\nabla_{i}(-tf)+-\frac{2}{3}(|T|^{2}_{g})\nabla_{i}\partial_{t}(-tf)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+4\partial_{t}(\nabla_{j}T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj})

The evolution equation for the torsion given in
[
35
, (3.9)]
implies that

|\partial_{t}(|T|^{2}_{g})\nabla_{i}(-tf)|=O(r^{-3}),\;\;\text{and }|\partial_{t}(\nabla_{j}T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj})|=O(r^{-5}).

By (
4.18
),

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{3}(|T|^{2}_{g})\nabla_{i}\partial_{t}(-tf)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{3}(|T|^{2}_{g})\nabla_{i}(-t({\mathscr{E}}-R))+-\frac{2}{3}(|T|^{2}_{g})[\nabla_{i},\partial_{\tau}](-tf)

\displaystyle=O(r^{-3}).

This proves
4.20
for the cases
k\geq 1
. The same argument of integrating along geodesics as in Proposition
4.6
is sufficient to prove the case
k=0
.
∎

We now prove that
-tf
extends to a smooth function on the space-time
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[-1,0]
.

Lemma 4.8
.

As
t\rightarrow 0^{-}
, the family
-tf(x,t)
converges to a function
q\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}})
in the
C^{\infty}_{loc}
-topology. Furthermore,
q
satisfies

(4.22)

\bigg|q(x)-\frac{r^{2}(x)}{4}\bigg|\leq N\log r(x),\;\;|\nabla q|_{g_{D}}^{2}=q,\;\text{ and }\nabla^{2}_{g_{D}}q=\frac{1}{2}g_{D},

where
g_{D}=g(0)
. Finally, the metric
g_{D}
is conical with radial distance function
r_{g_{D}}=2\sqrt{q}
.

Proof.

Let
a\in(-1,0)
. Integrating (
4.18
), we obtain

(4.23)

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(-tf)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{-1}^{a}-t({\mathscr{E}}-R)dt

Observe that given any spatial compact set
K\subset{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
,
{\mathscr{E}}-R
is uniformly bounded on the space-time
K\times[-1,0)
. In particular, the right hand side of the above equation is integrable on
\{x\}\times[-1,a)
for all
a
and fixed
x\in K
. We simplify

\displaystyle-af(x,a)-f(x,-1)

\displaystyle=\int_{-1}^{a}-t({\mathscr{E}}-R)(x)dt

\displaystyle-af(x,a)

\displaystyle=f(x,-1)+\int_{-1}^{a}-t({\mathscr{E}}-R)(x)dt.

Taking the limit as
a\rightarrow 0
, we see that
-tf
converges locally uniformly to some continuous function
q
. To obtain the local uniform convergence of all higher derivatives, we spatially differentiate the equation (
4.23
) by
\nabla^{m}
. Lemma
4.7
gives us estimates on
\nabla^{m}({\mathscr{E}}-R)
that are uniform in time and space on any compact set
K\times[-1,0]
. We can then argue in the exact same way as above to obtain the local uniform convergence of all derivatives. Thus,
-tf
converges locally smoothly as
t\to 0
to the limit function
q
, and

\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(-tf)(x,t)dt=\int_{-1}^{0}-tO(\log r(x))dt\implies q(x)-f(x,-1)=O(\log r(x))

By the comparison of
r
and
f
,

\bigg|q(x)-\frac{r^{2}(x)}{4}\bigg|\leq N\log r(x).

This allows us to see that
q
is proper and positive on sufficiently distant regions. Multiplying (
4.17
) by
t^{2}
gives us

|\nabla(-tf)|^{2}+tf=t^{2}O(\log r)\implies|\nabla q|_{g_{D}}^{2}=q,

where
g_{D}=g(0)
. A similar argument with the third identity of Claim
4.4
gives us

\nabla^{2}_{g_{D}}q=\frac{1}{2}g_{D}.

All of the conditions listed in Section 2.2.4 of
[
29
]
are satisfied, and thus, we have that
g_{0}
is conical with radial distance function
r_{g_{D}}=2\sqrt{q}
. That is, there exists a diffeomorphism
\hat{\Phi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\hat{\Sigma}}_{r_{4}}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
such that

(q\circ\hat{\Phi})(\hat{r},\hat{\sigma})=\frac{\hat{r}^{2}}{4},\text{ and }\hat{\Phi}^{*}(g_{D})=\hat{g}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=d\hat{r}^{2}+\hat{r}^{2}g_{\hat{\Sigma}},

where
\hat{\Sigma}
is a closed
6
-manifold which is not necessarily the same as
\Sigma
.
∎

Lemma 4.9
.

There exists an isometry
F\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{C})\rightarrow({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{D})
from the rescaling limit to the dynamical limit.

Proof.

By integrating the evolution equation for the metric

\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{\partial\hat{g}_{ij}}{\partial t}dt=\int_{-1}^{0}-2\widehat{\Ric}_{ij}-\frac{2}{3}|\hat{T}|^{2}g_{ij}-4\hat{T}_{i}^{\;l}\hat{T}_{lj}dt,

we can estimate the difference

|\hat{\Phi}^{*}(g(-1))-\hat{g}_{c}|_{\hat{g}_{c}}\leq\frac{N}{\hat{r}^{2}+1}.

The argument given in Section 2.3 of
[
29
]
can be repeated verbatim to yield an isometry
\hat{F}
between
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{C})
and
({\mathcal{C}}^{\hat{\Sigma}}_{0},\hat{g})
. The composition
F\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\hat{\Phi}\circ\hat{F}
yields the desired isometry
F\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{C})\rightarrow({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{D})
.
∎

The remaining difficulty is to show that
\varphi_{C}=\varphi_{D}
. Unlike
[
29
]
, we cannot simply pull back the flow
\varphi(t)
by
F
to obtain a flow limiting to
\varphi_{C}
—this is due to the fact that
F
may be an isometry but not an automorphism of
G_{2}
-structures. Instead, we show that the dynamical limit of the flow we have chosen must coincide exactly with the rescaling limit.

Henceforth, we drop the
\mathring{\varphi}
notation and denote the initial, static
G_{2}
-structure simply by
\varphi
and its associated metric by
g
. The time slices at time
t
of the flow of
G_{2}
-structures will be denoted by
\varphi_{t}
and their associated metrics by
g_{t}
.

Lemma 4.10
.

[
8
, Claim 3.9]
For all
x\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
and
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
,

(4.24)

|(\nabla^{g})^{k}(g-g_{D})|_{g}(x)\leq C_{k}r_{g_{D}}^{-2-k},

where
g=g_{-1}
is the initial shrinker metric.

Proof.

The proof is identical to that of
[
8
, Claim 3.9]
after replacing
-2\Ric(g_{t})
by

-2\Ric(g_{t})-\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}g_{t}-4T*T.

Claim
4.3
and the uniform comparability of the metrics
\{g_{t}\}_{t\in[-1,0)}
allows us to make this substitution without further modifications.
∎

The next step is to prove an analogous result for the difference
\varphi-\varphi_{D}
and its derivatives. Ultimately, by Lemma
3.1
this will tell us that
\varphi
is asymptotic to
\varphi_{D}
in the
C^{k}_{loc}(C^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{D})
-topology. Then, using the fact that
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{C})
and
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{D})
are isometric, we show that this topology is equivalent to the
C^{k}_{loc}(C^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{C})
-topology. Thus, the limits
\varphi_{C}
and
\varphi_{D}
must be the same, finally proving that the dynamical limit and the rescaling limit coincide.

Lemma 4.11
.

For all
x\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
and
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
,

(4.25)

|(\nabla^{g})^{k}(\varphi-\varphi_{D})|_{g}(x)\leq C_{k}r_{g_{D}}^{-2-k},

where
\varphi=\varphi_{-1}
is the initial
G_{2}
-shrinker.

Proof.

We again follow the proof of
[
8
, Claim 3.9]
, but we must be more careful to check the validity of each step. By the equation for Laplacian flow and the uniform equivalence of the metrics
g_{t}
, we have

(4.26)

|\varphi-\varphi_{D}|_{g}(x)\leq\int_{-1}^{0}\bigg|\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial t}(t)\bigg|_{g}dt\leq C\int_{-1}^{0}\big|\Delta_{\varphi_{t}}\varphi_{t}\big|_{g}dt

By the uniform equivalence of the metrics
g_{t}
, it is sufficient to estimate
|\Delta_{\varphi_{t}}\varphi_{t}|_{g_{t}}
. Recall the operator
i_{\varphi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S^{2}T^{*}M\rightarrow\Lambda^{3}T^{*}M
from symmetric 2-tensors to 3-forms which is defined locally by

i_{\varphi}(h)=\frac{1}{2}h_{i}^{l}\varphi_{ljk}dx^{i}\wedge dx^{j}\wedge dx^{k}.

It is shown in
[
35
, (2.20)-(2.21)]
that the Hodge Laplacian satisfies

\Delta_{\varphi}\varphi=i_{\varphi}(h),

where
h
is a symmetric 2-tensor given by

h_{ij}=-\Ric_{ij}-\frac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}-2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}.

By
[
4
]
,
|i_{\varphi}(h)|^{2}=8|h|^{2}
, so it suffices to estimate the magnitude of
|h_{t}|_{g_{t}}
. By Claim
4.3
, we know that each summand in
h_{ij}
has quadratic decay and thus

|\Delta_{\varphi_{t}}\varphi_{t}|_{g}(x)\leq C|\Delta_{\varphi_{t}}\varphi_{t}|_{g_{t}}(x)=C|h|_{g_{t}}(x)\leq Cd_{g_{t}}(p,x)^{-2},

for
x\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
and
p\in\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
. Applying the estimate (
4.16
) then yields the desired estimate

|\Delta_{\varphi_{t}}\varphi_{t}|_{g}(x)\leq Cr_{g_{D}}(x)^{-2}.

Plugging into (
4.26
) gives the estimate

|\varphi-\varphi_{D}|_{g}(x)\leq Cr_{g_{D}}(x)^{-2},

which completes the proof of the case
k=0
. For the case
k=1
, we again follow the basic outline of
[
8
]
.

\displaystyle\partial_{t}|\nabla^{g}(\varphi_{t}-\varphi)|^{2}_{g}(x)

\displaystyle\geq-2|\nabla^{g}h(\varphi_{t})|_{g}(x)|\nabla^{g}(\varphi_{t}-\varphi)|_{g}(x)

\displaystyle\geq-2\bigg(|\nabla^{g_{t}}h(\varphi(t))|_{g}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\big(\big|\big(\nabla^{g}-\nabla^{g_{t}}\big)h(\varphi_{t})\big|_{g}(x)\big)\bigg)|\nabla^{g}(\varphi_{t}-\varphi)|_{g}(x)
