\displaystyle\geq-C\bigg(d_{g_{t}}(p,x)^{-3}+\big(\big|\big(\nabla^{g}-\nabla^{g_{t}}\big)h(\varphi_{t})\big|_{g}(x)\big)\bigg)|\nabla^{g}(\varphi_{t}-\varphi)|_{g}(x)

\displaystyle\geq-C\big(r_{g_{D}}(x)^{-3}+|\nabla^{g}(g-g_{t})|_{g}(x)|h(\varphi_{t})|_{g}(x))\big)|\nabla^{g}(\varphi_{t}-\varphi)|_{g}(x)

Here we invoke Proposition
2.4
to obtain an inequality of the form

|\nabla^{g}(g-g_{t})|_{g}\leq C\bigg(1+\sum_{j=0}^{1}|(\nabla^{g})^{j}(g-g_{t})|_{g}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}|(\nabla^{g})^{j}\varphi|_{g}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{1}|(\nabla^{g})^{j}(\varphi-\varphi_{t})|_{g}\bigg).

By Lemma
4.10
, the terms
\sum_{j=0}^{1}|(\nabla^{g})^{j}(g-g_{t})|_{g}
are uniformly bounded on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
and the asymptotic conicality of
\varphi
allows us to bound the summands
|(\nabla^{g})^{j}\varphi|_{g}
. The
k=0
case tells us that
|\varphi-\varphi_{D}|_{g}(x)\leq Cr_{g_{D}}(x)^{-2}
, and we know that
|h(\varphi_{t})|_{g}=O(r_{g_{D}}^{-2})
. These inequalities combine to give the following estimate

|\nabla^{g}(g-g_{t})|_{g}(x)|h(\varphi_{t})|_{g}(x))\leq C(|\nabla^{g}(\varphi-\varphi_{t})|_{g}(x)+r_{g_{D}}(x)^{-4}).

Plugging this into the our previous calculation yields

\displaystyle\partial_{t}|\nabla^{g}(\varphi_{t}-\varphi)|^{2}_{g}(x)

\displaystyle\geq-C\big(r_{g_{D}}(x)^{-3}+|\nabla^{g}(\varphi-\varphi_{t})|_{g}(x)\big)|\nabla^{g}(\varphi_{t}-\varphi)|_{g}(x)

\displaystyle\geq-C|\nabla^{g}(\varphi_{t}-\varphi)|^{2}_{g}(x)-Cr_{g_{D}}(x)^{-6},

where the final line follows from Young’s inequality. For the purposes of integrating this inequality, we consider the backward flow with time parameter
\tau=-t
and note that on the interval
\tau\in(0,1]
, the inequality

\partial_{\tau}|\nabla^{g}(\varphi_{\tau}-\varphi)|^{2}_{g}(x)\geq-C|\nabla^{g}(\varphi_{\tau}-\varphi)|^{2}_{g}(x)-Cr_{g_{D}}(x)^{-6}

holds, as all of our estimates involved taking norms and thus were insensitive to sign. Since
|\nabla^{g}(\varphi_{\tau}-\varphi)|^{2}_{g}(x)=0
when
\tau=1
, integrating this differential inequality between
\tau\in(0,1)
and
1
gives the estimate

|\nabla^{g}(\varphi_{\tau}-\varphi)|^{2}_{g}(x)\leq C\rho(x)^{-6},

for all
x\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}
and
\tau\in(0,1]
, for a uniform constant
C>0
. Sending
\tau\searrow 0
gives the lemma.
∎

Corollary 4.12
.

The initial closed
G_{2}
-structure
\varphi
is asymptotic to
\varphi_{D}
in the
C^{k}_{loc}(C^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{D})
-topology.

Proof.

The corollary follows immediately from Lemma
4.11
and Lemma
3.1
.
∎

Proposition 4.13
.

The dynamical limit and the rescaling limit coincide. That is,

\varphi_{C}\equiv\varphi_{D}\;\;\text{ on }{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0}.

Proof.

The smooth, vertex-preserving isometry
F\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{C})\rightarrow({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{D})
must be generated by an isometry
F|_{\Sigma\times\{1\}}
of the link. Thus, one can take a finite atlas
\{U_{k}\}_{k=1}^{m}
of the link
\Sigma
such that
g_{C}|_{\Sigma}
and
g_{D}|_{\Sigma}
are comparable metrics when restricted to each chart
U_{k}
. Extend this to a countable atlas of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0}
given by
\{U_{k}\times(1/j,j)\}_{j,k}
, where
k\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}\cap[2,\infty)
. As
g_{D}
and
g_{C}
are isometric, they are again comparable on each chart
U_{k}\times(1/j,j)
.

This means that the topology of convergence in
C^{k}_{loc}(U_{k}\times(1/j,j),g_{C})
is equivalent to that in
C^{k}_{loc}(U_{k}\times(1/j,j),g_{D})
on each chart
U_{k}\times(1/j,j)
. Since these charts form an atlas for the cylinder
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0}
, the
C^{k}_{loc}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{C})
-limit of
\lambda^{-3}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}\varphi
as
\lambda\rightarrow\infty
must coincide with its
C^{k}_{loc}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0},g_{D})
-limit. Since these are
\varphi_{C}
and
\varphi_{D}
respectively,
\varphi_{C}\equiv\varphi_{D}
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{0}
.
∎

Reset
r_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=r_{3}
. We conclude that given any two
G_{2}
-structures
\varphi
and
\tilde{\varphi}
asymptotic to
\varphi_{C}
, we may construct backward Laplacian flows
\varphi_{\tau}
and
\tilde{\varphi}_{\tau}
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
, for
\tau\in[0,1]
, such that
\varphi_{1}=\varphi
,
\tilde{\varphi}_{1}=\tilde{\varphi}
, and
\varphi_{\tau}
and
\tilde{\varphi}_{\tau}
coincide at
\tau=0
(i.e.
\varphi_{0}=\tilde{\varphi}_{0}=\varphi_{C}
). Then, for the flows thus constructed, to prove Theorem
1.1
, it suffices to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 4.14
.

Let
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}},\varphi_{\tau})
and
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}},\tilde{\varphi}_{\tau})
be the shrinking flows constructed above. Then there exists
r^{\prime}\geq r_{3}
and
\tau^{\prime}\in(0,1)
such that
\varphi_{\tau}\equiv\tilde{\varphi}_{\tau}
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r^{\prime}}\times[0,\tau^{\prime}]
.

4.1.
Some identities in preparation for Carleman estimates

In this section, we prove
G_{2}
-Laplacian flow versions of various lemmas and identities in
[
29
, §4]
.

Consider a flow
\varphi(x,\tau)
associated to the gradient shrinker
(M,\varphi,f)
, constructed as in the proof of Proposition
4.1
, and let
f(x,\tau)
denote the pullback of the potential function,
\Psi_{\tau}^{*}f
. As in
[
29
, Lemma 4.5]
, we introduce the
\tau
-dependent radial function

(4.27)

h(x,\tau)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}2\sqrt{\tau f(x,\tau)}&\textrm{for}\;\;\;\tau>0;\\
r(x)&\textrm{for}\;\;\;\tau=0.\end{array}\right.

By identity (
4.4
), we see immediately that

(4.28)

\frac{1}{2}r(x)\leq h(x,\tau)\leq 2r(x)

holds.

Lemma 4.15
.

On
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{0}}^{\Sigma}\times(0,1]
, the derivatives of
h
satisfy

(4.29)

\nabla h=\frac{2\tau}{h}\nabla f,\;\;\;h\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}h=g-\nabla_{i}h\nabla_{j}h+2\tau\bigg(-\Ric_{ij}-\frac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}-2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\bigg),

(4.30)

|\nabla h|^{2}=1+\frac{4\tau^{2}}{h^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}-R),\;\;\;h\Delta h=7-\frac{4}{3}\tau R-|\nabla h|^{2},

and

(4.31)

\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}=\frac{h}{2\tau}(1-|\nabla h|^{2})=\frac{2\tau}{h}(R-{\mathscr{E}}).

Remark 4.2
.

Recall that estimates for the growth of the quantity
{\mathscr{E}}(x,\tau)
and all of its derivatives are given in Lemma
4.7
. In particular, there exists
C>0
, independent of time, such that
|{\mathscr{E}}(x,\tau)|\leq C\log r
on the space-time
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times(0,1]
.

Proof.

The first identity in (
4.29
) follows directly from the definition. The second identity in (
4.29
) is a consequence of (
4.14
), the time-dependent gradient shrinker equation for the metric. The first identity in (
4.30
) is derived by squaring the first identity in (
4.29
) and substituting (
4.17
). That is,

\displaystyle|\nabla h|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\tau^{2}}{h^{2}}|\nabla f|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\tau^{2}}{h^{2}}\bigg(\frac{f}{\tau}+{\mathscr{E}}-R\bigg)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{4\tau^{2}}{h^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}-R).

The second identity in (
4.30
) is the trace of the second identity in (
4.29
), where we use the fact that
|T|^{2}=-R
and
T_{ij}
is antisymmetric for closed
G_{2}
-structures. The last identity (
4.31
) follow by combining (
4.5
), (
4.29
), and (
4.30
). Indeed,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}=2\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\sqrt{\tau f(x,\tau)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tau f(x,\tau)}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\big(\tau f(x,\tau)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{h}\bigg(f(x,\tau)+\tau\frac{\partial f}{\partial\tau}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{h\tau}\big(\tau f(x,\tau)-\tau^{2}|\nabla f|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{h\tau}\bigg(\frac{h^{2}}{4}-\frac{h^{2}}{4}|\nabla h|^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{h}{2\tau}(1-|\nabla h|^{2}).

∎

We now prove our weakened version of
[
29
, Lemma 4.6]
.

Lemma 4.16
.

There exists a universal constant
C
such that

(4.32)

|h(x,\tau)-r(x)|\leq\frac{C\tau^{2}\log{r(x)}}{r(x)}

for all
(x,\tau)\in{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{0}}^{\Sigma}\times[0,\tau_{0}).

Proof.

Fixing any
x\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
, multiplying both sides of (
4.31
) by
2h
, and integrating with respect to
\tau
yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}2h\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\tau}4\tau(R-{\mathscr{E}})d\tau

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{\partial h^{2}}{\partial\tau}-\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}r^{2}(x)d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\tau}4\tau(R-{\mathscr{E}})d\tau

\displaystyle|h^{2}(x,\tau)-r^{2}(x)-(h^{2}(x,0)-r^{2}(x))|

\displaystyle\leq C\tau^{2}\log r.

Observe that by definition
h^{2}(x,0)-r^{2}(x)=0
. Then simplify

{\left\lvert h(x,\tau)-r(x)\right\rvert}{\left\lvert h(x,\tau)+r(x)\right\rvert}\leq C\tau^{2}\log r(x).

The comparability of
r(x)
and
h(x,\tau)
yields the claim.
∎

4.2.
An ODE-PDE system

In this section, we construct an ODE-PDE system which will be used to prove the backward uniqueness problem Theorem
4.14
. One flow of
G_{2}
-structures and its associated quantities are denoted by letters with tildes, and another flow of
G_{2}
-structures and its associated quantities are denoted by letters without tildes. These two
G_{2}
-structures come from
\varphi_{\tau}
and
\tilde{\varphi}_{\tau}
in Theorem
4.14
. The ODE-PDE system is constructed from evolution equations for differences of geometric quantities from each of these two
G_{2}
-structures.

Consider the following definitions. We adopt notation for these quantities to be as consistent as possible with our source for evolution equations
[
35
, §6.2]
.

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\varphi-\tilde{\varphi}

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=g-\tilde{g}

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\nabla-\widetilde{\nabla}

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\nabla A

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=T-\widetilde{T}

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\nabla T-\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{T}

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Rm-\widetilde{\Rm}

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\nabla\Rm-\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{\Rm}

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\nabla^{2}T-\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}\widetilde{T}

The quantity
A
is the
(2,1)
-tensor
A_{ij}^{k}=\Gamma_{ij}^{k}-\widetilde{\Gamma}_{ij}^{k}
. Now, we find differential inequalities for the evolution of norms of the above quantities. All norms will be taken with respect to
g_{t}=g(t)
, i.e.
|\cdot|=|\cdot|_{g_{t}}
. Recall that
\tau=-t
. Let
K
and
\tilde{K}
be the constants from part (c) of Lemma
3.3
associated to
g
and
\tilde{g}
, respectively.

Lemma 4.17
.

There exists
r_{0}
and
C>0
, depending on
K
and
\tilde{K}
, such that the following inequalities hold on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{0}}^{\Sigma}\times[-1,0]
:

\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\gamma\Big|\leq C|V|+\frac{C}{r}|A|,

\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mu\Big|\leq C|S|+\frac{C}{r}\Big(|\gamma|+|F|\Big),

\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}A\Big|\leq C|\nabla S|+\frac{C}{r}\Big(|A|+|\gamma|+|V|+|F|\Big),

\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}B\Big|\leq C|\nabla Q|+\frac{C}{r}\Big(|A|+|B|+|\gamma|+|V|+|W|+|F|\Big),

\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}F\Big|\leq C|\nabla V|+\frac{C}{r}\Big(|A|+|\gamma|+|V|+|F|+|S|\Big),

\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}V-\Delta V\Big|\leq\frac{C}{r}\Big(|A|+|B|+|\gamma|+|V|+|W|+|F|+|S|+|Q|\Big),

\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}S-\Delta S\Big|\leq\frac{C}{r}\Big(|A|+|B|+|\gamma|+|V|+|W|+|F|+|S|\Big),

\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}Q-\Delta Q\Big|\leq\frac{C}{r}|\nabla W|+\frac{C}{r}\Big(|A|+|B|+|\gamma|+|V|+|W|+|F|+|S|+|Q|\Big),

\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}W-\Delta W\Big|\leq\frac{C}{r}\Big(|\nabla Q|+|\nabla W|\Big)\\
+\frac{C}{r}\Big(|A|+|B|+|\gamma|+|V|+|W|+|F|+|S|+|Q|\Big),

and

(4.33)

|\gamma|+|\mu|+|A|+|B|+|F|+|V|+|S|+|Q|+|W|\\
+|\nabla V|+|\nabla S|+|\nabla Q|+|\nabla W|\leq\frac{C}{r^{2}}

Proof.

We begin by verifying the last statement on the decay of all quantities. By Proposition
4.13
applied to Lemmas
4.10
and
4.11
, we have that

(4.34)

|\nabla^{k}(g-\tilde{g})|\leq|\nabla^{k}(g-g_{c})|+|\nabla^{k}(g_{c}-\tilde{g})|\leq C_{k}r^{-2-k},

and

(4.35)

|\nabla^{k}(\varphi-\tilde{\varphi})|\leq|\nabla^{k}(\varphi-\varphi_{c})|+|\nabla^{k}(\varphi_{c}-\tilde{\varphi})|\leq C_{k}r^{-2-k},

and the constants
C_{k}
are independent of time, which follows from the arguments in Lemmas
4.11
and
4.11
. By the arguments of Lemmas
4.10
and
4.11
, the estimates (
4.34
) and (
4.35
) also hold for
g_{t}-\tilde{g}_{t}
and
\varphi_{t}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}
. Now,

\Gamma(g_{s})-\Gamma(g_{t})=g_{t}^{-1}\ast\nabla(g_{t}-g_{s}).

Taking the limit as
s\to 0
, using Proposition
4.13
, and then taking the norm, we find that

|\Gamma(g_{c})-\Gamma(g_{t})|\leq|\nabla(g_{t}-g_{c})|.

Using Lemma
4.10
and applying the triangle inequality to
A=\Gamma(g_{t})-\Gamma(\tilde{g}_{t})
, we find that

(4.36)

|A|\leq\frac{C}{r^{3}}.

By the definition of torsion
[
35
, (2.11)]
,

T_{i}^{\;j}=\frac{1}{24}\nabla_{i}\varphi_{lmn}(\star_{\varphi}\varphi)^{jlmn},

and so by adding and subtracting the terms with and without tildes,

T-\widetilde{T}=(g^{-1}-\tilde{g}^{-1})\ast\nabla\varphi\ast(\star_{\varphi}\varphi)+\nabla\varphi\ast(\star_{\varphi}(\varphi-\tilde{\varphi}))+\nabla\varphi\ast\big((\star_{\varphi}-\star_{\tilde{\varphi}})\tilde{\varphi}\big)\\
+\nabla(\varphi-\tilde{\varphi})\ast(\star_{\tilde{\varphi}}\tilde{\varphi})+A\ast\tilde{\varphi}\ast(\star_{\tilde{\varphi}}\tilde{\varphi}),

where
\ast
refers to contractions in both
g
and
\tilde{g}
. Note that the AC condition gives uniform bounds on
|\tilde{\varphi}|
,
|\tilde{g}|
,
{\left\lvert\star_{\tilde{\varphi}}\tilde{\varphi}\right\rvert}
, and
|\nabla\varphi|
. Using (
4.35
), (
4.36
), and Lemma
2.3
, we find that

(4.37)

|T-\widetilde{T}|\leq\frac{C}{r^{2}}

Now, by Proposition
4.1
, we know that
|\nabla^{m}\Rm|=O(r^{-2})
for all
m\geq 0
. By (
4.36
) and (
4.37
), we find that
|B|
,
|V|
, and
|W|
are
O(r^{-2})
as well. It follows immediately from the estimates on derivatives of curvature and torsion that
|\nabla V|
,
|\nabla S|
,
|\nabla W|
, and
|\nabla Q|
are also
O(r^{-2})
. Putting it all together, we conclude that

(4.38)

\sup_{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma}\times[-1,0]}\Big(|\gamma|+|\mu|+|A|+|B|+|F|+|V|+|S|+|Q|+|W|\\
+|\nabla V|+|\nabla S|+|\nabla Q|+|\nabla W|\Big)\leq\frac{C}{r^{2}}.

Note that every constant
C
or
C_{k}
used thus far in this argument has not depended on time, which allows us to take the supremum over
t\in[-1,0]
in (
4.38
).

We may now begin to verify the stated differential inequalities, using the evolution equations of Lotay–Wei for these quantities
[
35
, Lemma 6.2]
as well as some additional estimates.

By Proposition
2.2
and bounds on
|\tilde{g}|
due to the AC condition, we know that
|\gamma|\leq C|\mu|
. Using the fact that
R=-|T|^{2}
, the decay of curvature (
4.3
), and (
4.37
), we find that for some
r_{0}
, on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{0}}^{\Sigma}
,

|T|,|\widetilde{T}|\leq\frac{C}{r}.

Applying these estimates to the calculations for
\frac{\partial\gamma}{\partial t}
and
\frac{\partial\mu}{\partial t}
in
[
35
, Lemma 6.2]
, we find the two stated differential inequalities for these quantities.

By
[
35
, (6.13)]
,

(4.39)

\frac{\partial A}{\partial t}=g^{-1}\ast\nabla S+\tilde{g}^{-1}\ast\widetilde{T}\ast V+\nabla T\ast F+\widetilde{\Rm}\ast A\\
+(\tilde{g}^{-2}\ast\widetilde{T}\ast\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{T}+\tilde{g}^{-1}\ast\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{\Rm}+\tilde{g}^{-1}\ast\nabla T\ast T)\ast\mu,

where the
\ast
here represents simple contractions in the indicated tensors. If
{\mathcal{S}}
is a
(k,l)
-tensor, then

|{\mathcal{S}}|^{2}_{g}\leq|{\mathcal{S}}|^{2}_{\tilde{g}}+C(k,l,|\tilde{g}|)|\mu||{\mathcal{S}}|^{2}_{g}

where we use the fact that for
r_{0}
large enough,
|\mu|^{m}\leq|\mu|
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{0}}^{\Sigma}
for
m\geq 1
. Since
|\mu|\ll 1
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{0}}^{\Sigma}
, we find that

|{\mathcal{S}}|^{2}_{g}\leq C|{\mathcal{S}}|^{2}_{\tilde{g}},

and this implies that we may switch between taking the norm with respect to
g
and with respect to
\tilde{g}
. This implies that
|\widetilde{\nabla}^{k}\widetilde{\Rm}|
is
O(r^{-2-k})
and
|\widetilde{\nabla}^{k}\widetilde{T}|
is
O(r^{-1-k})
, using expressions for
\widetilde{\nabla}^{k}\widetilde{T}
in terms of curvature quantities
[
35
, §2.3]
. We may now estimate every term in (
4.39
) and we find our third differential inequality for
\frac{\partial A}{\partial t}
.

Now, by Remark
2.1
,
|\nabla\mu|\leq C(|\gamma|+|\nabla\gamma|)
. We estimate the term
|\nabla\gamma|
by applying (
2.1
),

\nabla\gamma=\nabla\varphi-\widetilde{\nabla}\tilde{\varphi}+A\ast\tilde{\varphi}=(g^{-1}-\tilde{g}^{-1})\ast T\ast(\star_{\varphi}\varphi)+(T-\widetilde{T})\ast(\star_{\varphi}\varphi)\\
+\widetilde{T}\ast(\star_{\varphi}(\varphi-\tilde{\varphi}))+\widetilde{T}\ast((\star_{\varphi}-\star_{\tilde{\varphi}})\tilde{\varphi})+A\ast\tilde{\varphi}.

By Lemma
2.3
, we find that

(4.40)

|\nabla\gamma|\leq C(|\gamma|+|F|+|A|),

and so

(4.41)

|\nabla\mu|\leq C(|\gamma|+|F|+|A|),

on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{0}}^{\Sigma}
. Also, on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{0}}^{\Sigma}
,

(4.42)

|\nabla F|=|\nabla T-\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{T}-(\nabla-\widetilde{\nabla})\widetilde{T}|\leq|V|+C|A||\widetilde{T}|\leq|V|+\frac{C}{r}|A|,

(4.43)

|\nabla V|=|\nabla^{2}T-\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}\widetilde{T}-(\nabla-\widetilde{\nabla})\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{T}|\leq|W|+C|A||\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{T}|\leq|W|+\frac{C}{r^{2}}|A|,

(4.44)

|\nabla S|=|\nabla\Rm-\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{\Rm}-(\nabla-\widetilde{\nabla})\widetilde{\Rm}|\leq|Q|+C|A||\widetilde{\Rm}|\leq|Q|+\frac{C}{r^{2}}|A|.

By the argument of
[
35
, Lemma 6.6]
, we have that on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{0}}^{\Sigma}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial B}{\partial t}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\nabla\frac{\partial A}{\partial t}\Big|+|A|\Big|\frac{\partial g}{\partial t}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\nabla\frac{\partial A}{\partial t}\Big|+\frac{C}{r}|A|

By differentiating (
4.39
), and writing
\nabla\tilde{g}^{-1}=\tilde{g}^{-1}\ast\tilde{g}\ast\nabla(\tilde{g}-g)
,

\displaystyle\leq C(|\nabla^{2}S|)+\frac{C}{r}(|A|+|B|+|F|+|\nabla F|+|\nabla V|+|V|+|\nabla\mu|+|\mu|)

Applying (
4.41
), (
4.42
), and (
4.43
),

\displaystyle\leq C(|\nabla^{2}S|)+\frac{C}{r}(|A|+|B|+|\gamma|+|V|+|F|+|W|)

\displaystyle\leq C|\nabla Q|+\frac{C}{r}\Big(|A|+|B|+|\gamma|+|V|+|W|+|F|\Big),

where the last line follows by

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}S|

\displaystyle=|\nabla(\nabla\Rm-\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{\Rm})-\nabla((\nabla-\widetilde{\nabla})\widetilde{\Rm})|

\displaystyle\leq|\nabla Q|+C|B||\widetilde{\Rm}|+C|A||\nabla\widetilde{\Rm}|

\displaystyle\leq|\nabla Q|+C|B||\widetilde{\Rm}|+C|A|^{2}|\widetilde{\Rm}|+C|A||\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{\Rm}|

\displaystyle\leq|\nabla Q|+\frac{C}{r^{2}}(|A|+|B|).

This completes the derivation of the differential inequality for
\frac{\partial B}{\partial t}
.

By the derivation in
[
35
, Lemma 6.2]
,

\frac{\partial F}{\partial t}=\nabla V+\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{T}\ast A+(\widetilde{T}+\widetilde{T}\ast(\star_{\tilde{\varphi}}\tilde{\varphi}))\ast S+\widetilde{T}\ast\tilde{\varphi}\ast V+\Rm\ast\widetilde{T}\ast(\star_{\tilde{\varphi}}\tilde{\varphi}-\star_{\varphi}\varphi)\\
+\nabla T\ast T\ast\gamma+(\Rm+\Rm\ast(\star_{\tilde{\varphi}}\tilde{\varphi})+T^{2}+\widetilde{T}^{2}+T\ast\widetilde{T})\ast F

Using Lemma
2.3
and the known decay on torsion and curvature, the differential inequality for
\frac{\partial F}{\partial t}
follows.

Now, applying the estimates we have found to the evolution equation in
[
35
, Lemma 6.2]
, just as in our previous derivations, we find

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial V}{\partial t}-\Delta V\Big|-

\displaystyle\big|\text{div}\big((g^{ab}\nabla_{b}-\tilde{g}^{ab}\widetilde{\nabla}_{b})\widetilde{\nabla}_{i}\widetilde{T}_{jk}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}(|A|+|\nabla S|+|F|+|\gamma|+|V|+|S|+|\nabla V|)

Applying (
4.44
) and (
4.43
),

(4.45)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}(|A|+|\gamma|+|V|+|W|+|F|+|S|+|Q|)

Now, we estimate the divergence term as

\displaystyle\big|\text{div}\big((g^{ab}\nabla_{b}-\tilde{g}^{ab}\widetilde{\nabla}_{b})\widetilde{\nabla}_{i}\widetilde{T}_{jk}\big)\big|

\displaystyle=\big|g^{-2}\ast\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{T}-g^{-1}\tilde{g}^{-1}\ast\nabla\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}\widetilde{T}-g^{-1}\ast\nabla\tilde{g}^{-1}\ast\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}\widetilde{T}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|g^{-2}\ast\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{T}-g^{-1}\tilde{g}^{-1}\ast\nabla\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}\widetilde{T}\big|

\displaystyle\qquad+|\nabla(g^{-1}-\tilde{g}^{-1})\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}\widetilde{T}|,

When
|\mu|
is small enough, we bound
|\nabla(g^{-1}-\tilde{g}^{-1})|
by
C|\nabla\mu|
to find that

\displaystyle\leq\big|g^{-2}\ast\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{T}-g^{-1}(g^{-1}+(\tilde{g}^{-1}-g^{-1}))\ast\nabla\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}\widetilde{T}\big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C}{r}|\nabla\mu|

\displaystyle\leq\big|g^{-2}\ast\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{T}-g^{-2}\ast\nabla\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}\widetilde{T}\big|+\frac{C}{r}(|\mu|+|\nabla\mu|)

\displaystyle\leq\big|\nabla(\nabla-\widetilde{\nabla})\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{T}\big|+\frac{C}{r}(|\mu|+|\nabla\mu|)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}(|\mu|+|\nabla\mu|+|A|+|B|),

and from (
4.40
) and (
4.41
),

(4.46)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}(|\gamma|+|F|+|A|+|B|).

The differential inequality for
\frac{\partial V}{\partial t}
now follows from (
4.43
) and (
4.2
). The differential inequality for
\frac{\partial S}{\partial t}
follows similarly, using the evolution equation in
[
35
, Lemma 6.2]
and the following estimate, analogous to (
4.2
),

\displaystyle\big|\text{div}\big((g^{ab}\nabla_{b}-\tilde{g}^{ab}\widetilde{\nabla}_{b})\widetilde{Rm}_{ijk}^{l}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}(|\mu|+|\nabla\mu|+|A|+|B|)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}(|\gamma|+|F|+|A|+|B|).

Now, from
[
35
, (4.12)]
,

(4.47)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\nabla\Rm

\displaystyle=\Delta\nabla\Rm+\Rm\ast\nabla\Rm+\nabla\Rm\ast T^{2}+\Rm\ast T\ast\nabla T

(4.48)

\displaystyle\qquad+\nabla^{3}T\ast T+\nabla^{2}T\ast\nabla T,

where the
\ast
represents metric contractions by
g
. For any tensors
{\mathcal{S}}
,
\tilde{{\mathcal{S}}}
, we compute

\displaystyle\Delta{\mathcal{S}}-\tilde{\Delta}\tilde{{\mathcal{S}}}

\displaystyle=\Delta({\mathcal{S}}-\tilde{{\mathcal{S}}})+A\ast\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{T}+B\ast\widetilde{T}+A\ast\widetilde{T}+A\ast\nabla\widetilde{T}+\widetilde{\Gamma}\ast A\ast\widetilde{T}

(4.49)

\displaystyle\qquad+(g^{-1}-\tilde{g}^{-1})\ast(\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}\tilde{{\mathcal{S}}}-\widetilde{\Gamma}\ast\widetilde{\nabla}\tilde{{\mathcal{S}}}).

Note that we can replace contractions in
\tilde{g}
by contractions in
g
by estimating the difference in terms of
|\mu|
and decaying curvature and torsion terms. If
\ast
represents contractions in just
\tilde{g}
, then

(4.50)

\nabla^{3}T\ast T-\widetilde{\nabla}^{3}\widetilde{T}\ast\widetilde{T}=\nabla W\ast T+A\ast\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}\widetilde{T}\ast T+\widetilde{\nabla}^{3}\widetilde{T}\ast F

Using (
4.47
), (
4.2
), and previously found estimates on decaying terms, we find the desired differential inequality for
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}Q
:

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}Q-\Delta Q\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}|\nabla W|+\frac{C}{r}\big(|A|+|B|+|\mu|+|V|+|W|+|F|+|S|+|Q|\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}|\nabla W|+\frac{C}{r}\big(|A|+|B|+|\gamma|+|V|+|W|+|F|+|S|+|Q|\big).

Now, we have from
[
35
, (4.14)]
that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\nabla^{2}T

\displaystyle=\Delta\nabla^{2}T+\nabla^{3}T\ast T\ast\varphi+\nabla\Rm\ast(\nabla T+\nabla T\ast\psi+T^{2}\ast\varphi)+\nabla^{2}T\ast\nabla T\ast\varphi

\displaystyle\quad+\Rm\ast(\nabla^{2}T+\nabla^{2}T\ast\psi+\nabla T\ast T\ast\varphi+T^{3}\ast\varphi)+\nabla^{2}\Rm\ast(T+T\ast\psi)

(4.51)

\displaystyle\quad+\nabla T\ast T^{3}\ast\varphi+\nabla^{2}T\ast(T^{2}+T^{2}\ast\psi)+\nabla T\ast\nabla T\ast(T+T\ast\psi)

The differential inequality for
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}W
follows from (
4.2
) after applying (
4.2
) and expressing the terms involving
\nabla^{3}T
and
\nabla^{2}\Rm
using
\nabla W
and
\nabla Q
, just as in (
4.50
).
∎

Let
{\mathcal{S}}^{(k,l)}
be the vector bundle of
(k,l)
-tensors over
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{0}}^{\Sigma}
. Then, define

{\mathcal{X}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}={\mathcal{S}}^{(0,3)}\oplus{\mathcal{S}}^{(1,3)}+\oplus{\mathcal{S}}^{(1,4)}\oplus{\mathcal{S}}^{(0,4)},

{\mathcal{Y}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}={\mathcal{S}}^{(0,3)}\oplus{\mathcal{S}}^{(0,2)}+\oplus{\mathcal{S}}^{(1,2)}+\oplus{\mathcal{S}}^{(1,3)}+\oplus{\mathcal{S}}^{(0,2)}.

Now, for
t\in[-1,0]
, we define the section
\mathbf{X}(t)\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{X}})
by

\textbf{X}=V\oplus S\oplus Q\oplus W,

and we define the section
\mathbf{Y}(t)\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})
by

\textbf{Y}=\gamma\oplus\mu\oplus A\oplus B\oplus F.

These sections form our ODE-PDE system, as described in the following proposition. Recall that
\tau=-t
.

Proposition 4.18
.

There is
C>0
and
r_{0}>0
such that for all
(x,\tau)\in{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{0}}^{\Sigma}\times[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial\mathbf{Y}}{\partial\tau}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|\mathbf{X}|+|\mathbf{\nabla X}|\big)+\frac{C}{r}|\mathbf{Y}|

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial\mathbf{X}}{\partial\tau}+\Delta\mathbf{X}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}\big(|\mathbf{X}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}|+|\mathbf{Y}|\big)

Proof.

Putting together the results of Lemma
4.17
,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\mathbf{Y}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|V|+|S|+|\nabla V|+|\nabla S|+|\nabla Q|\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{C}{r}\Big(|A|+|B|+|\gamma|+|V|+|W|+|F|+|S|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(|\mathbf{X}|+|\nabla{X}|)+\frac{C}{r}|\mathbf{Y}|+\frac{C}{r}(|V|+|S|+|W|)

By adjusting the constants
C
and
r_{0}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\leq C(|\mathbf{X}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}|)+\frac{C}{r}|\mathbf{Y}|.

This gives the differential inequality for
\mathbf{Y}
. The differential inequality for
\frac{\partial\mathbf{X}}{\partial\tau}
follows immediately from Lemma
4.17
.

∎

5.
Carleman Estimates

In this section, we derive Carleman estimates analogous to those in
[
10
]
which will eventually be applied in Section 6 to show that the tensors
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{Y}
decay at least exponentially and ultimately vanish everywhere.

We will adopt the same notational conventions as in
[
29
]
for Section
5
and Section
6
—namely, we assume a backward Laplacian flow
\varphi_{\tau}
with associated metric
g_{\tau}
and potential function
f_{\tau}
on the space-time
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
, with
r_{0}\geq 1
and
\tau_{0}\in(0,1)
, that satisfies all the properties stated in Proposition
4.1
with constant
K_{0}\geq 1
. Henceforth, we take all inner products with respect to
g_{\tau}
and let all connections be Levi-Civita with respect to
g_{\tau}
. The parameter
r
will continue to denote both the radial coordinate on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
as well as the radial distance with respect to the conical metric which we continue to denote
g_{C}
(associated to
\varphi_{C}
). As in
[
29
]
we take
A_{0}
to be the volume of the link
\Sigma
with respect to the restriction of
g_{C}
to the cross-section at
\{r=1\}
. Finally, for the rest of section Section
5
, we consider a generic time-dependent section
Z
of the
(\kappa,\nu)
-tensor bundle
{\mathcal{Z}}=T^{\kappa}_{\nu}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}})
. As in
[
29
]
, all constants without explicit dependencies will depend on the constants
A_{0},K_{0},\nu,
and
\kappa
.

5.1.
Carleman estimates to imply backward uniqueness

We being by deriving the fundamental divergence identity behind the Carleman estimates that we use to prove backward uniqueness. This identity is analogous to that in
[
10
, Lemma 1]
and very similar to that in
[
29
, Lemma 4.1]
. However, Lemma
5.1
differs from
[
29
, Lemma 4.1]
precisely where the evolution equations for
\partial_{\tau}g
,
\partial_{\tau}\vol_{g(\tau)}
, and
\partial_{\tau}\Gamma^{k}_{ij}
under the Laplacian flow are used in lieu of those for Ricci flow. Keeping this in mind, we carefully re-derive the Laplacian flow version of this identity.

Let
Z\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Z}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])
and
{\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{H}}\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])
with
{\mathscr{H}}>0
and
\phi=\log{\mathscr{H}}
. We define the following operators acting on sections in
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Z}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])
.

(5.1)

{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}-\nabla_{\nabla\phi}+\frac{{\mathscr{F}}}{2}\mathrm{Id}

(5.2)

{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Delta+\nabla_{\nabla\phi}-\frac{{\mathscr{F}}}{2}\mathrm{Id}

The fundamental divergence identity (
5.5
) is built up from the identity

(5.3)

\bigg\langle{\mathcal{A}}Z,\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}+\Delta\bigg)Z\bigg\rangle-2|{\mathcal{A}}Z|^{2}=\langle{\mathcal{A}}Z,{\mathcal{S}}Z\rangle,

and the following Rellich-Nečas identity applied to a vector field
\nabla{\mathscr{H}}
,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}\big[|\nabla Z|^{2}\nabla_{i}{\mathscr{H}}-2\langle\nabla_{\nabla{\mathscr{H}}}Z,\nabla_{i}Z\rangle\big]

\displaystyle=2\langle\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}Z,\nabla_{j}Z\rangle\nabla_{i}{\mathscr{H}}+|\nabla Z|^{2}\Delta{\mathscr{H}}

\displaystyle\qquad-2\langle\nabla_{i}(\nabla_{\nabla{\mathscr{H}}}Z),\nabla_{i}Z\rangle-2\langle\nabla_{\nabla{\mathscr{H}}}Z,\Delta Z\rangle

\displaystyle=2\langle[\nabla_{i},\nabla_{j}]Z,\nabla_{j}Z\rangle\nabla_{i}{\mathscr{H}}+|\nabla Z|^{2}\Delta{\mathscr{H}}

\displaystyle\qquad-2\langle\nabla_{i}Z,\nabla_{j}Z\rangle\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}{\mathscr{H}}-2\langle\nabla_{\nabla{\mathscr{H}}}Z,\Delta Z\rangle.

Lemma 5.1
.

For all
Z\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Z}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])
and all smooth
{\mathscr{F}}
and
{\mathscr{H}}>0
, the following identity holds on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
:

(5.5)

\nabla_{i}\bigg\{2\bigg\langle\frac{\partial Z}{\partial\tau},\nabla_{i}Z\bigg\rangle{\mathscr{H}}+|\nabla Z|^{2}\nabla_{i}{\mathscr{H}}-2\langle\nabla_{\nabla{\mathscr{H}}}Z,\nabla_{i}Z\rangle+\frac{{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{H}}}{2}\nabla_{i}|Z|^{2}\\
+\frac{1}{2}({\mathscr{F}}\nabla_{i}{\mathscr{H}}-{\mathscr{H}}\nabla_{i}{\mathscr{F}})|Z|^{2}\bigg\}d\mu-\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bigg\{\bigg(|\nabla Z|^{2}+\frac{{\mathscr{F}}}{2}|Z|^{2}\bigg){\mathscr{H}}d\mu\bigg\}\\
=\bigg\{2\bigg\langle{\mathcal{A}}Z,\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}+\Delta\bigg)Z\bigg\rangle{\mathscr{H}}-2|{\mathcal{A}}Z|^{2}{\mathscr{H}}\\
+\bigg({\mathscr{F}}-{\mathscr{H}}^{-1}\bigg(\frac{\partial{\mathscr{H}}}{\partial\tau}-\Delta{\mathscr{H}}+\frac{2}{3}R{\mathscr{H}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg(|\nabla Z|^{2}+\frac{{\mathscr{F}}}{2}|Z|^{2}\bigg){\mathscr{H}}\\
-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\partial{\mathscr{F}}}{\partial\tau}+\Delta{\mathscr{F}}\bigg)|Z|^{2}{\mathscr{H}}-2\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi\langle\nabla_{i}Z,\nabla_{j}Z\rangle{\mathscr{H}}-\frac{\partial g}{\partial\tau}(\nabla Z,\nabla Z){\mathscr{H}}\\
-\frac{{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{H}}}{2}\frac{\partial g}{\partial\tau}(Z,Z){\mathscr{H}}+2E(Z,\nabla Z){\mathscr{H}}\bigg\}d\mu,

where

(5.6)

E(Z,\nabla Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\bigg\langle\bigg[\nabla_{i},\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bigg]Z,\nabla_{i}Z\bigg\rangle-\langle[\nabla_{i},\nabla_{j}]Z,\nabla_{i}Z\rangle\nabla_{j}\phi\\
=\frac{1}{2}g^{qm}\big(\nabla_{i}\eta_{pm}+\nabla_{p}\eta_{im}-\nabla_{m}\eta_{ip}+R^{j}_{pmi}\nabla_{j}\phi\bigg)\langle\Theta_{q}^{p}Z,\nabla_{i}Z\rangle,

and
\Theta_{q}^{p}
is the operator

(5.7)

\Theta_{q}^{p}(Z^{\beta}_{\alpha})=\delta_{\alpha_{1}}^{p}Z^{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{\kappa}}_{q\alpha_{2}\cdots\alpha_{\nu}}+\delta_{\alpha_{2}}^{p}Z^{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{\kappa}}_{\alpha_{1}q\alpha_{3}\cdots\alpha_{\nu}}+\cdots\delta_{\alpha_{\nu}}^{p}Z^{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots p}_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\cdots q}\\
-\delta_{q}^{\beta_{1}}Z^{p\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{\kappa}}_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\cdots\alpha_{\nu}}-\delta_{q}^{\beta_{2}}Z^{\beta_{1}p\cdots\beta_{\kappa}}_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\cdots\alpha_{\nu}}-\cdots-\delta_{q}^{\beta_{\kappa}}Z^{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots p}_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\cdots\alpha_{\nu}}.

Proof.

Combine the identities (
5.3
) and (
5.4
) with the evolution equations

\frac{\partial g_{ij}}{\partial\tau}=2\Ric_{ij}+\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+4T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj},\;\;\;\;\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\vol_{g(\tau)}=\frac{2}{3}R,\;\;\;\text{ and }

\frac{\partial}{\partial_{\tau}}\Gamma^{k}_{ij}=\frac{1}{2}g^{kl}(\nabla_{i}\eta_{jl}+\nabla_{j}\eta_{il}-\eta_{l}\eta_{ij}),

where

\eta=2\Ric+\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}g+2T*T.

This yields the identity (
5.5
) after expanding the left-hand side. The expression (
5.6
) follows by substituting the evolution equation for the Christoffel symbols in for the commutator
[\nabla_{i},\partial_{\tau}]
.
∎

Next, we show that an analogue of the inequality
[
29
, (4.6)]
from Remark 4.2 holds in our setting.

Lemma 5.2
.

If
\nabla\phi=\Upsilon\nabla f
for some function
\Upsilon
, the following identity holds:

(5.8)

|E(Z,\nabla Z)|\leq C(|\nabla\Rm|+|\Rm||T|)(|\nabla Z|^{2}+(1+\Upsilon^{2})|Z|^{2})

Proof.

Differentiating (
4.6
) yields

\nabla_{i}\Ric_{jk}+\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{k}f=-\nabla_{i}\big(\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{jk}+2T_{j}^{\;l}T_{lk}\big).

Exchanging the roles of
i
and
j
and subtracting the two equations gives

R_{ijk}^{p}\nabla_{p}f=\nabla_{i}\Ric_{jk}-\nabla_{j}\Ric_{ik}-\nabla_{i}\big(\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{jk}+2T_{j}^{\;l}T_{lk}\big)+\nabla_{j}\big(\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ik}+2T_{i}^{\;l}T_{lk}\big).

Substituting this into (
5.6
), we obtain

\Bigg(\nabla_{i}\eta_{pq}+(1+\Upsilon)(\nabla_{p}\eta_{iq}-\nabla_{i}\eta_{pq})-\Upsilon(\nabla_{p}\big(\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{iq}+2T_{i}^{\;l}T_{lq}\big)-\nabla_{i}\big(\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{pq}+2T_{p}^{l}T_{lq}\big))\\
-\Upsilon\left(\nabla_{i}\left(\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{jk}+2T_{j}^{\;l}T_{lk}\right)-\nabla_{j}\left(\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ik}+2T_{i}^{\;l}T_{lk}\right)\right)\Bigg)\langle\Theta_{q}^{p}Z,\nabla_{i}Z\rangle.

Note that

\nabla_{i}(|T|^{2}g_{jk})=g_{jk}\nabla_{i}|T|^{2}=-g_{jk}\nabla_{i}R

and

\nabla_{i}(T_{j}^{\;l}T_{lk})\leq C|\nabla T||T|\leq C|\Rm||T|.

Thus, the additional corrections terms compared to the Ricci shrinker case can be estimated by

\bigg|\nabla_{i}\bigg(\frac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{jk}+2T_{j}^{\;l}T_{lk}\bigg)-\nabla_{j}\bigg(\frac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ik}+2T_{i}^{\;l}T_{lk}\bigg)\bigg|\leq C(|\nabla\Rm|+|\Rm||T|).

After estimating the torsion terms with this bound, obtain

|E(Z,\nabla Z)|\leq C(|\nabla\Rm|+|\Rm||T|)(|\nabla Z|^{2}+(1+\Upsilon^{2})|Z|^{2})

using Cauchy-Schwarz and Young’s inequality.
∎

Remark 5.1
.

When this lemma is applied, the salient bit is that the decay
h^{2}(|\Rm|+|\nabla\Rm|)\leq CK_{0}
. So it is not so important that the bound is specifically
|\nabla\Ric|
as in
[
29
, Remark 4.2]
.

5.1.1.
Weighted
L^{2}
-inequality for the operator
\partial_{\tau}+\Delta

When we choose

(5.9)

{\mathscr{F}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}={\mathscr{H}}^{-1}\left(\partial_{\tau}{\mathscr{H}}-\Delta{\mathscr{H}}+\frac{2}{3}R{\mathscr{H}}\right),

we obtain a cancellation of terms on the right-hand side of (
5.5
).
As in
[
29
]
, we integrate (
5.5
) over
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
to obtain the following inequality, analogous to
[
29
, Lemma 4.3]
.

Lemma 5.3
.

There exists a constant
N=N(\kappa,\nu,K_{0})
such that if
Z\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Z}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])
is compactly supported in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
for each
\tau
and satisfies
Z(\cdot,0)=0
, then, for any smooth
{\mathscr{H}}>0
, we have

(5.10)

\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\tau_{0}}\int_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times\{\tau\}}\bigg|\frac{\partial Z}{\partial\tau}+\Delta Z\bigg|^{2}{\mathscr{H}}d\mu d\tau+\int_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times\{\tau_{0}\}}\bigg(|\nabla Z|^{2}+\frac{{\mathscr{F}}}{2}|Z|^{2}\bigg){\mathscr{H}}d\mu\\
\geq\int_{0}^{\tau_{0}}\int_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times\{\tau\}}(Q_{1}(\nabla Z,\nabla Z)+Q_{2}(Z,Z)-2E(Z,\nabla Z)){\mathscr{H}}d\mu d\tau,

where
{\mathscr{F}}
is defined by (
5.9
) and

\displaystyle Q_{1}(\nabla Z,\nabla Z)

\displaystyle=2(\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi)\langle\nabla_{i}Z,\nabla_{j}Z\rangle-\frac{N}{r^{2}}|\nabla Z|^{2}

\displaystyle Q_{2}(Z,Z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\partial{\mathscr{F}}}{\partial\tau}+\Delta{\mathscr{F}}\bigg)|Z|^{2}-\frac{N|{\mathscr{F}}|}{r^{2}}|Z|^{2}

Proof.

Integration by parts and Young’s inequality yield the result. Note that the decay terms of order
r^{-2}
in
Q_{1}
and
Q_{2}
come from the fact that the right-hand side of the evolution equation

\frac{\partial g_{ij}}{\partial\tau}=2\Ric_{ij}+\frac{2}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+4T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}

decays quadratically.
∎

5.1.2.
A weighted
L^{2}
-inequality for the ODE component

The
L^{2}
-inequality for the ODE component is the same as
[
29
, Lemma 4.4]
, with the same proof. We restate it here:

Lemma 5.4
.

[
29
, Lemma 4.4]

There exists a constant
N=N(\kappa,\nu,K_{0})
such that if
Z\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Z}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])
is compactly supported in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}
for each
\tau
and satisfies
Z(\cdot,0)=0
, then for all smooth
{\mathscr{H}}>0
,

(5.11)

-\int_{0}^{\tau_{0}}\int_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times\{\tau\}}\bigg(N+\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial\tau}\bigg)|Z|^{2}{\mathscr{H}}d\mu d\tau\leq\int_{0}^{\tau_{0}}\int_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times\{\tau\}}\bigg|\frac{\partial Z}{\partial\tau}\bigg|^{2}{\mathscr{H}}d\mu d\tau.

5.1.3.
First Carleman estimate for the PDE system

For this estimate, we use the same weight function
{\mathscr{H}}_{1;\alpha,\tau_{0}}
as the one defined in
[
29
, (4.19)]
. Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
and for any
\alpha>0
define the function

(5.12)

{\mathscr{H}}_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}={\mathscr{H}}_{1;\alpha,\tau_{0}}=\exp\big[\alpha(\tau_{0}-\tau)h^{2-\delta}(x,\tau)+h^{2}(x,\tau)\big].

Let
\phi_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\phi_{1;\alpha,\tau_{0}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\log{\mathscr{H}}_{1}
. Define also

(5.13)

{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}={\mathscr{F}}_{1;\alpha,\tau_{0}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\partial\phi_{1}}{\partial\tau}-\Delta\phi_{1}-|\nabla\phi_{1}|^{2}+\frac{2}{3}R

We estimate the spatial and temporal derivatives of
\phi_{1}
and obtain a pointwise estimate on
{\mathscr{F}}_{1}
. This result is analogous to
[
29
, Lemma 4.7]
.

Lemma 5.5
.

For any
\alpha
and any
\tau_{0}
,
\delta\in(0,1)
, the function
\phi_{1}=\phi_{1;\alpha,\tau_{0}}
satisfies

(5.14)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\phi_{1}}{\partial\tau}

\displaystyle=4\tau(R-{\mathscr{E}})-\alpha h^{2-\delta}\bigg(1-\frac{2(2-\delta)\tau(\tau_{0}-\tau)(R-{\mathscr{E}})}{h^{2}}\bigg),

(5.15)

\displaystyle\nabla\phi_{1}

\displaystyle=\big(\alpha(2-\delta)(\tau_{0}-\tau)h^{1-\delta}+2h\big)\nabla h

(5.16)

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha(2-\delta)(\tau_{0}-\tau)}{h^{\delta}}(g_{ij}-2\tau(\Ric_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj})-\delta\nabla_{i}h\nabla_{j}h)

(5.17)

\displaystyle\qquad+2(g_{ij}-2\tau(\Ric_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}))

These estimates imply that there exists a constant
r_{1}\geq r_{0}
depending only on
\delta
and
K_{0}
such that on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{1}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\phi_{1}

\displaystyle\geq g,\;\;\;\text{ and }

\displaystyle 0\geq{\mathscr{F}}_{1}

\displaystyle\geq-N\big(1+h^{2}+\alpha h^{2-\delta}+\alpha^{2}(\tau_{0}-\tau)^{2}h^{2-2\delta}\big)

for all
\alpha\geq 1
.

Proof.

The first three inequalities follow from the identities proven in Lemma
4.15
. The rest of the proof proceeds along the same lines as in
[
29
]
, with some small but necessary modifications. To prove that

\nabla^{2}\phi_{1}\geq g,

first note that the symmetric 2-tensors
\Ric
,
|T|^{2}g
, and
T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}
decay quadratically, so in particular, we can select
r_{1}
large enough that

|\Ric|+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}+|T*T|\leq\frac{(1-\delta)}{4}

on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
. Since
\tau_{0}<1
, this implies that

g_{ij}-2\tau\big(\Ric_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\big)\geq\frac{(1+\delta)}{2}g_{ij},

in the sense of quadratic forms. It remains to prove that the first summand in the expression for
\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi_{1}
is positive-definite. Restricting to the orthogonal complement of
\nabla h
,

(5.18)

g_{ij}-2\tau\big(\Ric_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\big)-\delta\nabla_{i}h\nabla_{j}h\\
=g_{ij}-2\tau\big(\Ric+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\big)\geq\frac{(1+\delta)}{2}g_{ij},

so it remains to check positive-definiteness on the space spanned by
\nabla h
. Evaluating on this subspace, we obtain

\big(g_{ij}-2\tau(\Ric_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj})-\delta\nabla_{i}h\nabla_{j}h\big)\nabla_{i}h\nabla_{j}h\geq\frac{(1+\delta)}{2}|\nabla h|^{2}-\delta|\nabla h|^{4}.

Substituting in the expression for
|\nabla h|^{2}
given in Lemma
4.15
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{(1+\delta)}{2}|\nabla h|^{2}-\delta|\nabla h|^{4}

\displaystyle=|\nabla h|^{2}\bigg(\frac{(1+\delta)}{2}-\delta\bigg(1+\frac{4\tau^{2}}{h^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}-R)\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle=|\nabla h|^{2}\bigg(\frac{(1-\delta)}{2}-\frac{4\delta\tau^{2}}{h^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}-R)\bigg).

Increasing
r_{1}
if necessary, we see that (
5.18
) is positive-definite and thus we can conclude that
\nabla\nabla\phi_{1}\geq g
.

To begin estimating
{\mathscr{F}}_{1}
, we first calculate

|\nabla\phi_{1}|^{2}=\big(\alpha(2-\delta)(\tau_{0}-\tau)h^{1-\delta}+2h\big)^{2}|\nabla h|^{2},

\Delta\phi_{1}=2(7-\frac{4}{3}\tau R)+\frac{\alpha(2-\delta)(\tau_{0}-\tau)}{h^{\delta}}\bigg(7-\frac{4}{3}\tau R-\delta|\nabla h|^{2}\bigg).

For
r_{1}
sufficiently large, the second summand in the expression for
\Delta\phi_{1}
is positive, and the first summand can be bounded below by
7
—so
\Delta\phi_{1}\geq 7
. Furthermore,

\frac{\partial\phi_{1}}{\partial\tau}=-\alpha h^{2-\delta}\bigg(1-\frac{2(2-\delta)\tau(\tau_{0}-\tau)(R-{\mathscr{E}})}{h^{2}}-\frac{4\tau(R-{\mathscr{E}})}{\alpha h^{2-\delta}}\bigg)\leq-\frac{\alpha}{2}h^{2-\delta},

for sufficiently large
r_{1}
. Combining these estimates, we obtain the upper bound on
{\mathscr{F}}_{1}
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
.

\displaystyle{\mathscr{F}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\phi_{1}}{\partial\tau}-\Delta\phi_{1}-|\nabla\phi_{1}|^{2}+\frac{2}{3}R

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\alpha}{2}h^{2-\delta}-7+CK_{0}r^{-2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\alpha}{2}h^{2-\delta}+7\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 0.

This establishes the upper bound. To compute the lower bound, we calculate an explicit expression for
{\mathscr{F}}_{1}
.

\displaystyle{\mathscr{F}}_{1}

\displaystyle=4\tau(R-{\mathscr{E}})-\alpha h^{2-\delta}\bigg(1-\frac{2(2-\delta)\tau(\tau_{0}-\tau)(R-{\mathscr{E}})}{h^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad-2(7-\frac{4}{3}\tau R)+\frac{\alpha(2-\delta)(\tau_{0}-\tau)}{h^{\delta}}\bigg(7-\frac{4}{3}\tau R-\delta|\nabla h|^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad-\big(\alpha(2-\delta)(\tau_{0}-\tau)h^{1-\delta}+2h\big)^{2}|\nabla h|^{2}+\frac{2}{3}R

We can rearrange the terms to group like powers of
\alpha
.

(5.19)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{F}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\bigg(-2(7-\frac{4}{3}\tau R)+\frac{2}{3}R+4\tau(R-{\mathscr{E}})+4h^{2}|\nabla h|^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad-\alpha h^{2-\delta}\bigg(1-\frac{2(2-\delta)\tau(\tau_{0}-\tau)(R-{\mathscr{E}})}{h^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{(2-\delta)(\tau_{0}-\tau)}{h^{2}}\bigg(7-\frac{4}{3}\tau R-\delta|\nabla h|^{2}\bigg)-4(2-\delta)(\tau_{0}-\tau)|\nabla h|^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad-\alpha^{2}h^{2-2\delta}\big((2-\delta)^{2}(\tau_{0}-\tau)^{2}|\nabla h|^{2}\big).

Considering the asymptotics of each term, one can see that for sufficiently large
r_{1}
, on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}\times[0,\tau_{0}]

|{\mathscr{F}}_{1}|\leq N(1+h^{2}+\alpha h^{2-\delta}+\alpha(\tau_{0}-\tau)^{2}h^{2-2\delta}).

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Next, the expression
(\partial_{\tau}+\Delta){\mathscr{F}}_{1}
must be bounded from below. In our setting, we can still obtain the lower bound given in
[
29
, Lemma 4.8]
. We give their statement accompanied by the appropriately modified proof below.

Lemma 5.6
.

For all
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists
r_{2}\geq r_{0}
depending only on
\delta
and
K_{0}
, such that the function
{\mathscr{F}}_{1}={\mathscr{F}}_{1;\alpha,\tau_{0}}
satisfies

(5.20)

\frac{\partial{\mathscr{F}}_{1}}{\partial\tau}+\Delta{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\geq 3\alpha h^{2-\delta}+\alpha^{2}(\tau_{0}-\tau)h^{2-2\delta}

on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{2}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
for all
\alpha\geq 1
and
\tau_{0}\in(0,1]
.

Proof.

First, recall that
{\mathscr{E}}_{\tau}=O(\log r)
,
\nabla{\mathscr{E}}=O(r^{-1})
, and
\Delta{\mathscr{E}}=O(r^{-2})
by Lemma
4.7
. Calculate the value of the operator
(\partial_{\tau}+\Delta)
on each individual power of
\alpha
occurring in (
5.19
).

\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bigg(-2(7-\frac{4}{3}\tau R)+\frac{2}{3}R+4\tau(R-{\mathscr{E}})+4h^{2}\bigg(1+\frac{4\tau^{2}}{h^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}-R)\bigg)\bigg)\\
\geq-8h\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}-C_{1}{\mathscr{E}}-C_{2}\frac{\partial{\mathscr{E}}}{\partial\tau}-C_{3}\frac{\partial R}{\partial\tau}-C_{4}

Plugging in
\partial_{\tau}h=2\tau h^{-1}(R-{\mathscr{E}})
, we obtain that this whole expression is bounded below by
-C_{5}\log(r)
. Take the Laplacian.

\Delta\bigg(-2(7-\frac{4}{3}\tau R)+\frac{2}{3}R+4\tau(R-{\mathscr{E}})+4h^{2}\bigg(1+\frac{4\tau^{2}}{h^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}-R)\bigg)\bigg)\\
\geq-C(h\Delta h+|\nabla h|^{2})\geq-C

by Lemma
4.15
and Lemma
4.7
. Thus,

\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}+\Delta\bigg)\bigg(-2(7-\frac{4}{3}\tau R)+\frac{2}{3}R+4\tau(R-{\mathscr{E}})+4h^{2}\bigg(1+\frac{4\tau^{2}}{h^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}-R)\bigg)\bigg)\geq-C\log(r).

This can be absorbed into the terms of higher order in
\alpha
, which we now calculate. Taking the time derivative and the Laplacian are lengthy but straightforward calculations. Plugging in the decay estimates for the curvature and the shrinker potential
{\mathscr{E}}
given in Lemma
4.7
into the resulting expressions yields the following asymptotics for the terms with coefficient
\alpha
.

-\alpha\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bigg(h^{2-\delta}\bigg(1-\frac{2(2-\delta)\tau(\tau_{0}-\tau)(R-{\mathscr{E}})}{h^{2}}\\
+\frac{(2-\delta)(\tau_{0}-\tau)}{h^{2}}\bigg(7-\frac{4}{3}\tau R-\delta|\nabla h|^{2}\bigg)-4(2-\delta)(\tau_{0}-\tau)|\nabla h|^{2}\bigg)\bigg)

=\alpha\big((4(2-\delta))h^{2-\delta}+o(h^{-\delta/2})\big)

and

-\alpha\Delta\bigg(h^{2-\delta}\bigg(1-\frac{2(2-\delta)\tau(\tau_{0}-\tau)(R-{\mathscr{E}})}{h^{2}}\\
+\frac{(2-\delta)(\tau_{0}-\tau)}{h^{2}}\bigg(7-\frac{4}{3}\tau R-\delta|\nabla h|^{2}\bigg)-4(2-\delta)(\tau_{0}-\tau)|\nabla h|^{2}\bigg)\bigg)

=\alpha\big(o(h^{-\delta/2})\big)

Turning to the terms with coefficient
\alpha^{2}
, we observe that after differentiating by
\partial_{\tau}+\Delta
, the dominant term is that of order
h^{2-2\delta}
.

\displaystyle-\alpha^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bigg(h^{2-2\delta}\big((2-\delta)^{2}(\tau_{0}-\tau)^{2}|\nabla h|^{2}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\big(2(\tau_{0}-\tau)(2-\delta)^{2}h^{2-2\delta}+o(h^{-\delta})\big)

and

\displaystyle-\alpha^{2}\Delta\bigg(h^{2-2\delta}\big((2-\delta)^{2}(\tau_{0}-\tau)^{2}|\nabla h|^{2}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\big(o(h^{-\delta})\big)

By increasing
r_{2}
as needed, we take the sum of the order 0,1, and 2 terms in
\alpha
and absorb the lower order terms into the dominant terms. Since the terms with coefficient
\alpha,\alpha^{2}\geq 1
dominate
\log(r)
, for sufficiently large
r_{2}
, the terms of order 0 in
\alpha
can be absorbed into those of order
1
and
2
in
\alpha
. Then, possibly further increasing
r_{2}
to absorb other small terms, we obtain the desired inequality,

\frac{\partial{\mathscr{F}}_{1}}{\partial\tau}+\Delta{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\geq 3\alpha(2-\delta)h^{2-\delta}+\alpha^{2}(2-\delta)^{2}(\tau_{0}-\tau)h^{2-2\delta}

on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{2}}^{\Sigma}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
.
∎

Now we can prove the first Carleman estimate, which is identical to that in
[
29
]
.

Proposition 5.7
.

[
29
, Proposition 4.9]

For all
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
r_{3}=r_{3}(\delta,K_{0})\geq r_{0}
such that for all
\alpha\geq 1
and all
Z\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Z}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])
satisfying that
Z(\cdot,0)=0
and that
Z(\cdot,\tau)
is compactly supported in
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{3}}^{\Sigma}
for each
\tau\in[0,\tau_{0}]
, we have the estimate

(5.21)

\alpha||Z{\mathscr{H}}_{1}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])}+||\nabla Z{\mathscr{H}}_{1}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])}\\
\leq\frac{1}{2}||(\partial_{\tau}+\Delta)Z{\mathscr{H}}_{1}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])}+||\nabla Z{\mathscr{H}}_{1}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times\{\tau_{0}\})}

and

(5.22)

\alpha||Z{\mathscr{H}}_{1}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])}\leq 2||\partial_{\tau}Z{\mathscr{H}}_{1}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{3}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])},

where
{\mathscr{H}}_{1}={\mathscr{H}}_{1;\alpha,\tau_{0}}
.

Proof.

After replacing
[
29
, Lemma 4.7]
and
[
29
, Lemma 4.8]
by Lemma
5.5
and Lemma
5.6
respectively, the proof of
[
29
, Proposition 4.9]
goes through and is sufficient to prove Proposition
5.7
.
∎

5.2.
Carleman estimates to imply rapid decay

Since the weight function
{\mathscr{H}}_{1}
in the first Carleman estimate grows at the rate
\exp(Ch^{2})
, we must show that our solution satisfying the ODE-PDE system actually has decay of the same order or faster. This is done by means of a second Carleman estimate, similar to that in
[
10
, (1.4)]
and
[
29
, (5.23)]
, which is given in Proposition
5.15
. This estimate differs significantly from the two cited estimates in that it contains a
\log
growth term in
|Z|^{2}
on the right-hand side of the inequality. However, it will be shown in Section
6
how the exponential decay of the solution
Z
can be salvaged from the inequality as stated in (
5.41
).

As in
[
29
]
, we begin with a perturbed version of the divergence identity given in Lemma
5.1
, which is analogous to
[
29
, Lemma 5.1]
.

Lemma 5.8
.

For all
Z\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Z}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])
,
{\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{H}}\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])
with
{\mathscr{H}}>0
, and positive functions
\sigma,\theta\in C^{\infty}([0,\tau_{0}])
with
\sigma
increasing, the following identity holds:

(5.23)

\theta\sigma^{-\alpha}\nabla_{i}\bigg\{2\bigg\langle\frac{\partial Z}{\partial\tau},\nabla_{i}Z\bigg\rangle{\mathscr{H}}+|\nabla Z|^{2}\nabla_{i}{\mathscr{H}}-2\langle\nabla_{\nabla{\mathscr{H}}}Z,\nabla_{i}Z\rangle\\
+\frac{{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{H}}}{2}\nabla_{i}|Z|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}({\mathscr{F}}\nabla_{i}{\mathscr{H}}-{\mathscr{H}}\nabla_{i}{\mathscr{F}})|Z|^{2}\bigg\}d\mu\\
-\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bigg\{\bigg(|\nabla Z|^{2}+\frac{{\mathscr{F}}}{2}|Z|^{2}\bigg)\theta\sigma^{-\alpha}{\mathscr{H}}d\mu\bigg\}\\
=\bigg\{2\bigg\langle{\mathcal{A}}Z,\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}+\Delta\bigg)Z\bigg\rangle-2|{\mathcal{A}}Z|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\partial{\mathscr{F}}}{\partial\tau}+\Delta{\mathscr{F}}\bigg)|Z|^{2}\\
\hskip 56.9055pt+\bigg({\mathscr{F}}-{\mathscr{H}}^{-1}\bigg(\frac{\partial{\mathscr{H}}}{\partial\tau}-\Delta{\mathscr{H}}\bigg)+\frac{2}{3}R-\alpha\frac{\dot{\sigma}}{\sigma}\bigg)\bigg(|\nabla Z|^{2}+\frac{{\mathscr{F}}}{2}|Z|^{2}\bigg)\\
\hskip 56.9055pt-\frac{\dot{\theta}}{\theta}\bigg(|\nabla Z|^{2}+\frac{{\mathscr{F}}}{2}|Z|^{2}\bigg)-2\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi\langle\nabla_{i}Z,\nabla_{j}Z\rangle-\frac{\partial g}{\partial\tau}(\nabla Z,\nabla Z)\\
-\frac{{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{H}}}{2}\frac{\partial g}{\partial\tau}(Z,Z)+2E(Z,\nabla Z)\bigg\}\theta\sigma^{-\alpha}{\mathscr{H}}d\mu,

where
{\mathcal{A}}
and
E(Z,\nabla Z)
are defined as before.

We choose the function
{\mathscr{F}}
to give a cancellation in the right-hand side of the identity.

{\mathscr{F}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{{\mathscr{H}}}\bigg(\frac{\partial{\mathscr{H}}}{\partial\tau}-\Delta{\mathscr{H}}\bigg)+\frac{2}{3}R-\alpha\frac{\dot{\sigma}}{\sigma},

and let

\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}={\mathscr{F}}+\alpha\frac{\dot{\sigma}}{\sigma}.

We also choose

\theta\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\sigma}{\dot{\sigma}}

for the remainder of the section. The results of
[
29
, §5.2]
and their proofs go through unchanged into our setting. The first result that we restate is the following integral inequality, which will yield the
L^{2}
Carleman estimate.

Lemma 5.9
.

[
29
, Lemma 5.2]
There exists a constant
N=N(\kappa,\nu,K_{0})
such that for any
\alpha
and any
Z\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Z}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])
that is compactly supported in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0})
and vanishes on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times\{0\}
, the inequality

(5.24)

\int_{0}^{\tau_{0}}\int_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times\{\tau\}}\frac{\sigma^{1-\alpha}}{\dot{\sigma}}\big(Q_{3}(\nabla Z,\nabla Z)+Q_{4}(Z,Z)-2E(Z,\nabla Z)\big){\mathscr{H}}d\mu d\tau\\
\leq\int_{0}^{\tau_{0}}\int_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times\{\tau\}}\frac{\sigma^{1-\alpha}}{\dot{\sigma}}\bigg|\frac{\partial Z}{\partial\tau}+\Delta Z\bigg|^{2}{\mathscr{H}}d\mu d\tau

holds, where

(5.25)

Q_{3}(\nabla Z,\nabla Z)\geq\bigg(2\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi-\frac{\sigma}{\dot{\sigma}}\ddot{\widehat{\log\sigma}}g_{ij}\bigg)\langle\nabla_{i}Z,\nabla_{j}Z\rangle-\frac{N}{r^{2}}|\nabla Z|^{2}

and

(5.26)

Q_{4}(Z,Z)\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\partial{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}}{\partial\tau}+\Delta{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}+\frac{\dot{\theta}}{\theta}{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}\bigg)|Z|^{2}-\frac{N}{r^{2}}|{\mathscr{F}}||Z|^{2}.

The next inequality helps to estimate
|Z|
by terms of the form
|\nabla Z|
and
|(\partial_{\tau}+\Delta)Z|
.

Lemma 5.10
.

There exists a constant
N=N(\kappa,\nu,K_{0})
such that, for all
\alpha>0
, all smooth positive
{\mathscr{H}}={\mathscr{H}}(x,\tau)
, and all positive increasing
\sigma=\sigma(\tau)
, the inequality

(5.27)

\int_{0}^{\tau_{0}}\int_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times\{\tau\}}\sigma^{-2\alpha}\bigg(\alpha\frac{\dot{\sigma}}{\sigma}-\tilde{{\mathscr{F}}}-\frac{N}{r^{2}}\bigg)|Z|^{2}{\mathscr{H}}d\mu d\tau\\
\leq\int_{0}^{\tau_{0}}\int_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times\{\tau\}}\sigma^{-2\alpha}\bigg(2|\nabla Z|^{2}+\frac{\sigma}{\alpha\dot{\sigma}}\bigg|\frac{\partial Z}{\partial\tau}+\Delta Z\bigg|^{2}\bigg){\mathscr{H}}d\mu d\tau

holds for every
Z\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Z}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])
that is compactly supported in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0})
and vanishes on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times\{0\}
.

5.3.
An approximate solution to the conjugate heat equation

We use precisely same function defined in
[
29
]
as our integration kernel. For any
a\in(0,1)
and
\rho\in(r_{0},\infty)
, define

(5.28)

{\mathscr{H}}_{2}(x,\tau)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}={\mathscr{H}}_{2;a,\rho}(x,\tau)=(\tau+a)^{-7/2}\exp\bigg(-\frac{(h(x,\tau)-\rho)^{2}}{4(\tau+a)}\bigg)

on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
. We note that analogously to the Ricci soliton case,
{\mathscr{H}}_{2;0,0}=\tau^{-7/2}e^{-f}
satisfies

\partial_{\tau}{\mathscr{H}}_{2;0,0}-\Delta{\mathscr{H}}_{2;0,0}+\frac{2}{3}R{\mathscr{H}}_{2;0,0}=0.

5.3.1.
Estimates on the derivatives of
{\mathscr{H}}_{2}

We compute the image of
{\mathscr{H}}_{2}
under the conjugate heat operator
\partial_{\tau}-\Delta
.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
{\mathscr{H}}_{2}
be defined as above. The following identity holds

\displaystyle{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{-1}\bigg(\frac{\partial{\mathscr{H}}_{2}}{\partial\tau}-\Delta{\mathscr{H}}_{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\tau^{2}(h-\rho)^{2}}{h^{2}(\tau+a)^{2}}+\frac{2\tau^{2}\rho}{h^{3}(\tau+a)}+\frac{\tau(h-\rho)}{h(\tau+a)}\bigg)(R-{\mathscr{E}})

(5.29)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{3\rho}{h(\tau+a)}+\frac{2\tau R}{3(\tau+a)}\bigg(\frac{\rho}{h}-1\bigg).

Proof.

We compute each term separately. First, take the time derivative:

(5.30)

{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{-1}\frac{\partial{\mathscr{H}}_{2}}{\partial\tau}=\frac{(h-\rho)^{2}}{4(\tau+a)^{2}}-\frac{7}{2(\tau+a)}-\frac{\tau(h-\rho)}{h(\tau+a)}(R-{\mathscr{E}}).

The last summand is obtained by substituting in the expression for
\tfrac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}
given in Lemma
4.15
.
The spatial second derivative is computed just as in
[
29
, (5.10)]
, by first observing that
{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{-1}\nabla{\mathscr{H}}_{2}=-(h-\rho)/(2(\tau+a))\nabla h
and plugging in expressions for the derivatives of
h
from Lemma
4.15
.

{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{-1}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}{\mathscr{H}}_{2}=\frac{1}{2(\tau+a)}\bigg(-g_{ij}+2\tau\big(\Ric_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\big)\bigg)\\
+\frac{\rho}{2(\tau+a)}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}h+\frac{(h-\rho)^{2}}{4(\tau+a)^{2}}\nabla_{i}h\nabla_{j}h.

Tracing, we find that

(5.31)

{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{-1}\Delta{\mathscr{H}}_{2}=-\frac{7}{2(\tau+a)}+\frac{2\tau R}{3(\tau+a)}+\frac{\rho}{2(\tau+a)}\Delta h+\frac{(h-\rho)^{2}}{4(\tau+a)^{2}}|\nabla h|^{2}.

Subtract (
5.31
) from (
5.30
).

\displaystyle{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{-1}\bigg(\frac{\partial{\mathscr{H}}_{2}}{\partial\tau}-\Delta{\mathscr{H}}_{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{(h-\rho)^{2}}{4(\tau+a)^{2}}(1-|\nabla h|^{2})-\frac{\rho\Delta h}{2(\tau+a)}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2\tau R}{3(\tau+a)}-\frac{\tau(h-\rho)}{h(\tau+a)}(R-{\mathscr{E}})

\displaystyle=\frac{(h-\rho)^{2}}{4(\tau+a)^{2}}(\frac{4\tau^{2}}{h^{2}}(R-{\mathscr{E}}))-\frac{\rho}{2h(\tau+a)}\bigg(7-|\nabla h|^{2}-\frac{4\tau R}{3}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2\tau R}{3(\tau+a)}-\frac{\tau(h-\rho)}{h(\tau+a)}(R-{\mathscr{E}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau^{2}(h-\rho)^{2}}{h^{2}(\tau+a)^{2}}(R-{\mathscr{E}})-\frac{6\rho}{2h(\tau+a)}+\frac{\rho}{2h(\tau+a)}(1-|\nabla h|^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2\rho\tau R}{3h(\tau+a)}-\frac{2\tau R}{3(\tau+a)}-\frac{\tau(h-\rho)}{h(\tau+a)}(R-{\mathscr{E}})

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\tau^{2}(h-\rho)^{2}}{h^{2}(\tau+a)^{2}}+\frac{2\tau^{2}\rho}{h^{3}(\tau+a)}-\frac{\tau(h-\rho)}{h(\tau+a)}\bigg)(R-{\mathscr{E}})

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{3\rho}{h(\tau+a)}+\frac{2\tau R}{3(\tau+a)}\bigg(\frac{\rho}{h}-1\bigg)

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 5.12
.

For all
a\in(0,1)
,
\gamma>0
, and
\rho>r_{0}\geq 1
, there exist constants
C=C(\gamma)>0
and
r_{4}=r_{4}(\gamma,K_{0})\geq r_{0}
, such that
{\mathscr{H}}_{2}={\mathscr{H}}_{2;a,\rho}
and
\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2}=\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2;a,\rho}
satisfy

(5.32)

\frac{1}{2}e^{-\frac{(r(x)-\rho)^{2}}{4(\tau+a)}}\leq(\tau+a)^{\frac{7}{2}}{\mathscr{H}}_{2}(x,\tau)\leq 2e^{-\frac{(r(x)-\rho)^{2}}{4(\tau+a)}},

and

(5.33)

-C\log(r)-\frac{3\rho}{h(\tau+a)}-\frac{1}{8h}\leq\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2}\leq C\log(r)-\frac{3\rho}{h(\tau+a)}+\frac{1}{8h},

and
\phi_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\log{\mathscr{H}}_{2}
satisfies

(5.34)

\nabla\nabla\phi_{2}\geq-\frac{g}{2(\tau+a)}-\frac{g}{48}

on the domain
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{\max\{r_{4},\gamma\rho\}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
.

Proof.

To prove the first inequality (
5.32
), we appeal to the estimate (
4.32
) and obtain

\frac{(r-\rho)^{2}}{(\tau+a)}-\frac{CK_{0}\tau^{2}\log(r)}{(\tau+a)r}\leq\frac{(h-\rho)^{2}}{(\tau+a)}\leq\frac{(r-\rho)^{2}}{(\tau+a)}+\frac{CK_{0}\tau^{2}\log(r)}{(\tau+a)r}.

When
\tau,a\in(0,1)
,
\tfrac{\tau^{2}}{\tau+a}\leq 1
, so for sufficiently large
r_{4}\geq r_{0}
(depending only on
K_{0}
),
\tfrac{\log r}{r}
is arbitrarily small and the error terms can be made arbitrarily close to
e^{0}=1
. This gives (
5.32
).

The second inequality (
5.33
) comes from examining the terms of (
5.3.2
). First, note that
\big|\tfrac{\tau}{\tau+a}\big|\leq 1
and
\big|\tfrac{h-\rho}{h}\big|\leq C(\gamma)
, so the coefficient of
(R-{\mathscr{E}})
in (
5.3.2
) is a constant
C(\gamma)
. Thus, this term can be bounded above and below by
\pm C\log(r)
. Observe that

\bigg|\frac{2\tau R}{3(\tau+a)}\bigg(\frac{\rho}{h}-1\bigg)\bigg|\leq|R|\big|2\gamma^{-1}-1\big|\leq\frac{CK_{0}}{h^{2}},

where
C
depends on
\gamma
. Since we can make
r_{4}\geq r_{0}
large enough that
CK_{0}h^{-1}\leq\tfrac{1}{8}
, we obtain the estimate (
5.33
).

The third inequality (
5.34
), the lower bound on the Hessian of
\phi_{2}
is obtained along the same lines as
[
29
, (5.16)]
with the requisite modifications made for the Laplacian flow case.

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}\phi_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2(\tau+a)}((h-\rho)\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}h+\nabla_{i}h\nabla_{j}h)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2(\tau+a)}\big(g_{ij}-2\tau\big(\Ric_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}-2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\rho}{2h(\tau+a)}\big(g_{ij}-2\tau\big(\Ric_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}-2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\big)-\nabla_{i}h\nabla_{j}h\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{g_{ij}}{2(\tau+a)}+\frac{\tau(h-\rho)}{h(\tau+a)}\big(\Ric_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}-2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\rho g_{ij}}{2h(\tau+a)}-\frac{\rho\nabla_{i}h\nabla_{j}h}{2h(\tau+a)}

Since
|\Ric_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}-2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}|\leq CK_{0}h^{-2}
and
|\nabla h|^{2}\leq 1+C\tau^{2}\log(r)h^{-2}
, we can obtain the following estimates (in the sense of quadratic forms) with a constant
C=C(\gamma)
.

\frac{\tau(h-\rho)}{h(\tau+a)}\big(\Ric_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}-2T_{i}^{\;k}T_{kj}\big)\geq-\frac{CK_{0}g_{ij}}{h^{2}}

and

\frac{\rho\nabla_{i}h\nabla_{j}h}{2h(\tau+a)}\leq\frac{\rho g}{2h(\tau+a)}+\frac{C\log(r)g}{h^{2}}.

For sufficiently large
r_{4}\geq r_{0}
, we can make
h^{-2}
and
\log(r)h^{-2}
arbitrarily small. Substituting these lower bounds into our equation gives the lower bound (
5.34
).
∎

5.3.2.
Estimates on the derivatives of
\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2}

To simplify our analysis, we group terms as follows. Rewrite

\displaystyle{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\tau^{2}(h-\rho)^{2}}{h^{2}(\tau+a)^{2}}+\frac{2\tau^{2}\rho}{h^{3}(\tau+a)}+\frac{\tau(h-\rho)}{h(\tau+a)}\bigg)(R-{\mathscr{E}})

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{3\rho}{h(\tau+a)}+\frac{2\tau R}{3(\tau+a)}\bigg(\frac{\rho}{h}-1\bigg)+\frac{2}{3}R,

as

(5.35)

{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}={\mathscr{I}}(x,\tau){\mathscr{E}}-\frac{\rho(n-1)}{2h(\tau+a)}+R{\mathscr{J}}\bigg(\frac{\rho}{h},\tau\bigg),

where

{\mathscr{I}}(x,\tau)=\frac{\tau^{2}(h-\rho)^{2}}{h^{2}(\tau+a)^{2}}+\frac{2\tau^{2}\rho}{h^{3}(\tau+a)}-\frac{\tau(h-\rho)}{h(\tau+a)}

and

{\mathscr{J}}(s,\tau)=\frac{2\tau}{3(\tau+a)}\cdot s+\frac{2a}{3(\tau+a)}.

Using this notation, we prove the following lemma.

Lemma 5.13
.

For all
a\in(0,1)
,
\gamma>0
, and
\rho>r_{0}
, there exist constants
N
and
r_{5}\geq r_{0}
both depending only on
\gamma
and
K_{0}
such that
{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}={\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2;a,\rho}
satisfies

(5.36)

\frac{\partial{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}}{\partial\tau}+\Delta{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}\geq-\frac{N}{(\tau+a)}\log(r)+\frac{3\rho}{h(\tau+a)^{2}}

on the set
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{\max\{r_{5},\gamma\rho\}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
.

Proof.

Note that throughout the proof we tacitly assume that
r_{5}\geq r_{0}
increases whenever necessary. We begin by applying the operator
\partial_{\tau}+\Delta
to the expression (
5.35
), and then analyzing each term.

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}}{\partial\tau}+\Delta{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}

\displaystyle=({\mathscr{I}}_{\tau}+\Delta{\mathscr{I}}){\mathscr{E}}+{\mathscr{I}}{\mathscr{E}}_{\tau}+2\langle\nabla{\mathscr{I}},\nabla{\mathscr{E}}\rangle+{\mathscr{I}}\Delta{\mathscr{E}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{3\rho}{(\tau+a)^{2}h}+\frac{3\rho}{(\tau+a)h^{2}}\bigg(\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}+\Delta h-\frac{2|\nabla h|^{2}}{h}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+{\mathscr{J}}\bigg(\frac{\partial R}{\partial\tau}+\Delta R\bigg)+{\mathscr{J}}_{\tau}R-\frac{\rho{\mathscr{J}}_{s}R}{h^{2}}\bigg(\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}+\Delta h\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2\rho{\mathscr{J}}_{s}}{h^{2}}\langle\nabla R,\nabla h\rangle+\frac{\rho R}{h^{3}}\bigg(2{\mathscr{J}}_{s}+\frac{\rho{\mathscr{J}}_{ss}}{h}\bigg)|\nabla h|^{2}.

We first estimate

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{3\rho}{(\tau+a)h^{2}}\bigg(\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}+\Delta h-\frac{2|\nabla h|^{2}}{h}\bigg)\bigg|=\frac{C(\gamma)}{(\tau+a)}\frac{\log(r)}{h}\leq\frac{N}{(\tau+a)}.

Next, we estimate the terms with
{\mathscr{J}}
and its derivatives.

\displaystyle|{\mathscr{J}}(\rho h^{-1},\tau)|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{2\tau}{3(\tau+a)}\cdot s+\frac{2a}{3(\tau+a)}\bigg|\leq C(\gamma^{-1}+1)

\displaystyle|{\mathscr{J}}_{\tau}(\rho h^{-1},\tau)|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{2\rho}{3h}\frac{\tau^{2}}{(\tau+a)^{2}}\frac{a}{\tau^{2}}-\frac{2}{3}\frac{a}{(\tau+a)^{2}}\bigg|\leq\frac{C\gamma^{-1}}{\tau+a}

\displaystyle|{\mathscr{J}}_{s}(\rho h^{-1},\tau)|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{2\tau}{3(\tau+a)}\bigg|\leq\frac{2}{3}

\displaystyle|{\mathscr{J}}_{ss}(\rho h^{-1},\tau)|

\displaystyle=0

Observing that

\frac{a}{\tau^{2}}=\frac{a}{(\tau+a)^{2}}\cdot\frac{(\tau+a)^{2}}{\tau^{2}}

gives the second inequality. The curvature decay estimates of Claim
4.3
and the identities in Lemma
4.15
give us that each term involving
{\mathscr{J}}
or its derivatives can be subsumed into a term of the form
N/(\tau+a)
.

It remains to estimate the terms involving
{\mathscr{I}}
and its derivatives. It is immediate that

|{\mathscr{I}}(x,\tau)|=\bigg|\frac{\tau^{2}(h-\rho)^{2}}{h^{2}(\tau+a)^{2}}+\frac{2\tau^{2}\rho}{h^{3}(\tau+a)}-\frac{\tau(h-\rho)}{h(\tau+a)}\bigg|\leq C,

where
C
depends on
\gamma
. This and Lemma
4.7
yields the estimates

|{\mathscr{I}}{\mathscr{E}}_{\tau}|\leq Cr^{-1},|{\mathscr{I}}\Delta{\mathscr{E}}|\leq Ch^{-2},

which can be absorbed into the term
-N\log(r)/(\tau+a)
in (
5.36
). Next, we differentiate with respect to
\tau
.

\displaystyle{\mathscr{I}}_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-\rho/h)}{(1+a/\tau)^{2}}\frac{2\rho}{h^{2}}\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}+2\frac{(1-\rho/h)^{2}}{(1+a/\tau)^{3}}\frac{a}{\tau^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{4\tau}{(\tau+a)}\frac{\rho}{h^{3}}-\frac{6\tau^{2}\rho}{h^{4}(\tau+a)}\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}-\frac{2\tau^{2}\rho}{h^{2}(\tau+a)^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{(1+a/\tau)}\frac{\rho}{h^{2}}\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}-\frac{(1-\rho/h)}{(1+a/\tau)^{2}}\frac{a}{\tau^{2}}.

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-\rho/h)}{(1+a/\tau)^{2}}\frac{2\rho}{h^{2}}\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}+2\frac{(1-\rho/h)^{2}}{(1+a/\tau)}\frac{a}{(\tau+a)^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{4\tau}{(\tau+a)}\frac{\rho}{h^{3}}-\frac{6\tau^{2}\rho}{h^{4}(\tau+a)}\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}-\frac{2\tau^{2}\rho}{h^{2}(\tau+a)^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{(1+a/\tau)}\frac{\rho}{h^{2}}\frac{\partial h}{\partial\tau}-\frac{a(1-\rho/h)}{(\tau+a)^{2}}.

We obtain the second equality by applying the identity

\frac{a}{\tau^{2}}=\frac{a}{(\tau+a)^{2}}\cdot\big(1+a/\tau)^{2}.

Thus,
|{\mathscr{I}}_{\tau}|\leq N/(\tau+a)
, where
N
depends on
\gamma
. Finally, we estimate the spatial derivatives of
{\mathscr{I}}
.

\displaystyle\nabla{\mathscr{I}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-\rho/h)}{(1+a/\tau)^{2}}\frac{2\rho}{h^{2}}\nabla h-\frac{6\tau^{2}\rho}{h^{4}(\tau+a)}\nabla h-\frac{1}{(1+a/\tau)}\frac{\rho}{h^{2}}\nabla h

\displaystyle\Delta{\mathscr{I}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1+a/\tau)^{2}}\frac{2\rho^{2}}{h^{4}}|\nabla h|^{2}-\frac{(1-\rho/h)}{(1+a/\tau)^{2}}\frac{4\rho}{h^{3}}|\nabla h|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(1-\rho/h)}{(1+a/\tau)^{2}}\frac{2\rho}{h^{2}}\Delta h+\frac{24\tau^{2}\rho}{h^{5}(\tau+a)}|\nabla h|^{2}-\frac{6\tau^{2}\rho}{h^{4}(\tau+a)}\Delta h

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2}{(1+a/\tau)}\frac{\rho}{h^{3}}|\nabla h|^{2}-\frac{1}{(1+a/\tau)}\frac{\rho}{h^{2}}\Delta h

Applying Claim
4.3
and the identities in Lemma
4.15
tell us that
|\nabla{\mathscr{I}}|+|\Delta{\mathscr{I}}|\leq N
, where
N
depends on
\gamma
. This allows us to make the final estimates

|({\mathscr{I}}_{\tau}+\Delta{\mathscr{I}})|\leq\frac{N}{(\tau+a)},|\langle\nabla{\mathscr{E}},\nabla{\mathscr{I}}\rangle|\leq|\nabla{\mathscr{E}}||\nabla{\mathscr{I}}|\leq Nr^{-1}.

Combining all these estimates gives the desired lower bound.
∎

5.3.3.
Carleman estimate for the PDE component

We now set the function
\sigma(\tau)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\tau e^{-\frac{\tau}{3}}
and define its translates
\sigma_{a}(\tau)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sigma(\tau+a)
. We repeat some useful estimates and an identity for
\sigma_{a}
from
[
29
, §5]
.

(5.37)

\frac{1}{3e}(\tau+a)\leq\sigma_{a}(\tau)\leq(\tau+a)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\frac{1}{3e}\leq\dot{\sigma}_{a}(\tau)\leq 1,

for
\tau\in[0,1]
. The following identity will also be useful.

(5.38)

-\frac{\sigma_{a}}{\dot{\sigma}_{a}}\ddot{\widehat{\log\sigma_{a}}}=\frac{1}{(\tau+a)(1-(1/3)(\tau+a))}

Let
Q_{3}
and
Q_{4}
be as in Lemma
5.9
.

Lemma 5.14
.

For all
\alpha>0
,
a\in(0,1)
, and
\gamma>0
, there exist
N
and
r_{6}\geq r_{0}
depending on
\gamma,\kappa,\nu,
and
K_{0}
, such that the quadratic forms
Q_{3}
and
Q_{4}
and the commutator term
E(Z,\nabla Z)
satisfy

(5.39)

Q_{3}(\nabla Z,\nabla Z)+Q_{4}(Z,Z)-2E(Z,\nabla Z)\geq\frac{1}{4}|\nabla Z|^{2}-\sigma_{a}^{-1}\bigg(N\log r+\frac{\alpha}{1000000}\bigg)|Z|^{2}

on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{\max\{r_{6},\gamma\rho\}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
.

Proof.

We consider all quantities on the set
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{\max\{r,\gamma\rho\}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
where
r\geq\max\{r_{3},r_{4},r_{5}\}
. The lower bound

Q_{3}(\nabla Z,\nabla Z)\geq\bigg(\frac{7}{24}-\frac{N}{r^{2}}\bigg)|\nabla Z|^{2}

follows from the same argument as in the proof of
[
29
, Lemma 5.6]
. We now estimate
Q_{4}
from below, starting with the inequality

Q_{4}(Z,Z)\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\partial{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}}{\partial\tau}+\Delta{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}+\frac{\dot{\theta}}{\theta}{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}\bigg)|Z|^{2}-\frac{N|{\mathscr{F}}_{2}|}{r^{2}}|Z|^{2}.

From (
5.33
) and (
5.36
), we know that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\partial{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}}{\partial\tau}+\Delta{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}+\frac{\dot{\theta}}{\theta}{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3\rho}{2h(\tau+a)^{2}}\bigg(1-\frac{1}{(1-(1/3)(\tau+a))}\bigg)-\frac{N}{(\tau+a)}\log r

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{(\tau+a)(1-(1/3)(\tau+a))}\bigg(N\log r+\frac{1}{16h}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq-N(\log r)\sigma_{a}^{-1}.

We now estimate
{\mathscr{F}}_{2}
.

\displaystyle|{\mathscr{F}}_{2}|=|{\widetilde{{\mathscr{F}}}}_{2}-\alpha\dot{\widehat{\log\sigma_{a}}}|\leq(N\log r+\alpha)\sigma_{a}^{-1}.

By absorbing appropriate terms and making
r
sufficiently large, we have

(5.40)

Q_{4}(Z,Z)\geq-\sigma_{a}^{-1}\bigg(N\log r+\frac{\alpha}{1000000}\bigg)|Z|^{2}.

The same arguments as in
[
29
, Lemma 5.6]
allow us to bound the commutator term
E(Z,\nabla Z)
as follows.

|E(Z,\nabla Z)|\leq\frac{N}{r^{2}}(|\nabla Z|^{2}+|Z|^{2}).

Absorbing this quantity gives us the expression (
5.39
) and completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.15
.

For any
a\in(0,1)
,
\gamma>0
, and
\rho\geq r_{0}
, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\gamma
, and
\alpha_{0}>0
,
r_{7}>r_{0}
depending only on
\gamma
,
\kappa
,
\nu
, and
K_{0}
such that for any smooth sections
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
which is compactly supported in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{\max\{r_{7},\gamma\rho\}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
, and satisfies
{\mathcal{Z}}(\cdot,0)\equiv 0
, we have

(5.41)

\sqrt{\alpha}||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha-1/2}Z\hat{{\mathscr{H}}}_{2}^{1/2}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])}+||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}\nabla Z\hat{{\mathscr{H}}}_{2}^{1/2}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])}\\
\leq C||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}(\partial_{\tau}Z+\Delta Z)\hat{{\mathscr{H}}}_{2}^{1/2}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])}+C||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha-1/2}(\log r)^{\frac{1}{2}}Z\hat{{\mathscr{H}}}_{2}^{1/2}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])}

for all
\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}
, where

(5.42)

\hat{{\mathscr{H}}}_{2}(x,\tau)=\hat{{\mathscr{H}}}_{2;a,\rho}(x,\tau)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\exp\Big(-\frac{(r(x)-\rho)^{2}}{4(\tau+a)}\Big).

Proof.

Plug the lower bounds given in (
5.39
) into (
5.24
) to obtain the following
L^{2}
estimates over the domain
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{\max\{r_{7},\gamma\rho\}}\times[0,\tau_{0}]
.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{12e}||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}\nabla Z{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}

\displaystyle\leq N||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha-1/2}(\log r)^{1/2}Z{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{\alpha}{500000}||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha-1/2}Z{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}(\partial_{\tau}Z+\Delta Z){\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})},

for any
\alpha>1
. Multiplying by constants and taking the square root, obtain

(5.43)

\displaystyle||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}\nabla Z{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}

\displaystyle\leq N||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha-1/2}(\log r)^{1/2}Z{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{5000}}||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha-1/2}Z{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}(\partial_{\tau}Z+\Delta Z){\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}.

Now, apply Lemma
5.10
to obtain the following inequality.

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha}{10}||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha-1/2}Z{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}

\displaystyle\leq N||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha-1/2}(\log r)^{1/2}Z{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}\nabla Z{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{20}{\alpha}||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}(\partial_{\tau}Z+\Delta Z){\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}.

Multiply by
1/8
and take the square root of both sides to obtain

(5.44)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{80}}||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha-1/2}Z{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}

\displaystyle\leq N||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha-1/2}(\log r)^{1/2}Z{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}\nabla Z{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{\frac{5}{2\alpha}}||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}(\partial_{\tau}Z+\Delta Z){\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{1/2}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}})}.

Add (
5.43
) and (
5.44
) together and absorb the
\sqrt{\alpha/5000}
term into the
\sqrt{\alpha/80}
term. After multiplication by a constant, this yields (
5.41
).
∎

5.3.4.
Carleman-type inequality for the ODE component.

The proof of the following Carleman inequality for the ODE component from
[
29
]
depends only on the uniform comparability of
g_{\tau}
and
g_{C}
for
\tau\in[0,\tau_{0}]
, which is known in our setting by Lemma
3.3
. We restate the proposition, which applies in its unmodified form in our setting.

Proposition 5.16
.

[
29
, Propostion 5.9]

Suppose
a_{0}\in(0,1/8)
,
\tau_{0}\in(0,1/4)
, and
r_{0}\geq 1
. Then there exist constants
N
and
r_{8}\geq r_{0}
, depending only on
\kappa,\nu,A_{0}
, and
K_{0}
, such that, for any smooth family
Z=Z(\tau)
of sections of
{\mathcal{Z}}
with compact support in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{8}}\times[0,\tau_{0})
which satisfies
Z(\cdot,0)\equiv 0
, we have, for all
\alpha>0
, that

(5.45)

||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}Z\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])}\leq N\alpha^{-1}||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}\partial_{\tau}Z\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}}\times[0,\tau_{0}])}\\
+Na^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\rho+\sqrt{a})^{\frac{n}{2}}\bigg(\frac{1}{ae^{\frac{1}{8}}}\bigg)^{\alpha}||Z||_{\infty,g_{C}}.

6.
Proof of Backward Uniqueness

In this section, we prove Theorem
4.14
by proving backward uniqueness along the lines of
[
10
]
and
[
29
]
, with some major modifications. First, we apply the second pair of Carleman estimates to prove that
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{Y}
decay quadratically exponentially in space. Then, we apply the first pair of estimates to prove that
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{Y}
vanish on some time interval containing
\tau=0
. Of these two steps, the first presents a number of difficulties due to the presence of the logarithmic growth of
||Z||_{2}
on the right-hand side of the PDE-type Carleman inequality. We compensate for this by adjusting our domain and our coefficients to absorb this term into the left-hand side. This introduces a growth term on the right-hand side, which we eventually determine can be absorbed into the quadratic exponential decay at the cost of increasing a universal constant. The second step, the proof of vanishing, on the other hand runs along the same lines as its counterpart in
[
29
]
.

We briefly note a few choices and conventions that will hold throughout the remainder of the section. As before, the quantities
\varphi
,
f
,
g
will refer to the time-dependent versions with the subscript
\tau
suppressed, unless necessary for the sake of clarity. We will henceforth denote the annular region given by
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{1}}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{2}}
with
r_{1}<r_{2}
by
A(r_{1},r_{2})
. We set a constant
r_{9}=r_{9}(\epsilon,K_{0},R_{0})\geq r_{0}
such that

(6.1)

|\mathbf{X}|+|\mathbf{\nabla X}|+|\mathbf{Y}|\leq N,

by (
4.38
), and

(6.2)

\bigg|\frac{\partial\mathbf{X}}{\partial t}+\Delta\mathbf{X}\bigg|\leq\epsilon\big(|\mathbf{X}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}|+|\mathbf{Y}|\big),\;\;\;\bigg|\frac{\partial\mathbf{Y}}{\partial t}\bigg|\leq N\big(|\mathbf{X}|+|\mathbf{\nabla X}|\big)+\epsilon|\mathbf{Y}|,

by Proposition
4.18
. Here
N
depends only on
K_{0}
. Next, we choose the same spatial cutoff function as in
[
29
, Lemma 6.1]

Lemma 6.1
.

Given
\rho>12r_{0}
and
\xi>4\rho
, there exists
\psi_{\rho,\xi}\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{0}},[0,1])
satisfying
\psi_{\rho,\xi}\equiv 1
on
A(\rho/3,2\xi)
and
\psi_{\rho,\xi}\equiv 0
on
A(r_{0},\rho/6)\cup{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{3\xi}
whose derivatives satisfy

(6.3)

|\nabla\psi_{\rho,\xi}|+|\Delta\psi_{\rho,\xi}|\leq N\rho^{-1}

for
N
depending on
K_{0}
. Furthermore, (
6.3
) holds with the same constant for norms and covariant derivatives taken with respect to
g_{\tau}
and the Levi-Civita connection
\nabla^{g_{\tau}}
respectively, for each
\tau\in[0,1]
.

6.1.
Proof of exponential decay

In this section, we show that
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{Y}
have quadratic exponential decay in space. We prove a statement that is essentially identical to
[
29
, Claim 6.2]
—however, the proof has significant differences.

Proposition 6.2
.

There exists an absolute constant
s_{0}
and constants
N=N(K_{0})
, and
r_{10}=r_{10}(K_{0})
, with
s_{0}\in(0,1]
and
r_{10}\geq r_{0}
, such that

(6.4)

|||\mathbf{X}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}|+|\mathbf{Y}|||_{L^{2}(A((1-\sqrt{s})\rho,(1+\sqrt{s})\rho)\times[0,s])}\leq Ne^{-\frac{\rho^{2}}{Ns}}

for any
s\in(0,s_{0}]
and
\rho>13r_{10}
.

Proof.

Our setup will largely follow that of the proof of
[
29
, Claim 6.2]
, with some important differences. Choose
\gamma=1/12
and
r_{10}=\max\{r_{7},r_{8}\}
, where
r_{7}
and
r_{8}
are the quantities given in Proposition
5.15
and Proposition
5.16
