. Note that this quantity is allowed to increase as needed throughout the proof. Let
\rho>0
satsify
\rho\geq 13r_{1}0
, and let
\xi
be a number such that
\xi\geq 4\rho
. The constants given by a
C
will be universal, whereas those given by
N
will depend on
A_{0}
and
K_{0}
.

Let
\vartheta\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}},[0,1])
be a temporal cutoff function such that
\vartheta\equiv 1
for
\tau\leq 1/6
and
\vartheta\equiv 0
for
\tau\geq 1/5
. Let
\psi_{\rho,\xi}
be the spatial cutoff function given in Lemma
6.1
. Define
\mathbf{X}_{\rho,\xi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\vartheta\psi_{\rho,\xi}\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,\xi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\vartheta\psi_{\rho,\xi}\mathbf{Y}
. These tensor fields are compactly supported on the space-time neighborhood
A(\rho/6,3\xi)\times[0,1/4)
and vanish on the time slice where
\tau=0
. Thus, we may apply Proposition
5.15
and Proposition
5.16
to
\mathbf{X}_{\rho,\xi}
and
\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,\xi}
respectively and add the results. Note that in the inequality below, we replace the parameter
\alpha\geq 1
by an integer
k\geq k_{0}\geq\alpha_{0}
, we let
a\in(0,1/8)
, and we bound the
||Z||_{\infty}
term in Proposition
5.16
using (
6.1
).

\displaystyle k^{\frac{1}{2}}||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{X}_{\rho,\xi}\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},3\xi)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}+||\sigma_{a}^{-k}\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,\xi}\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},3\xi)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad+||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,\xi}\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},3\xi)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\leq N||\sigma_{a}^{-k}(\partial_{\tau}+\Delta)\mathbf{X}_{\rho,\xi}\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},3\xi)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}+N(\rho+k^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-\frac{1}{2}}(ae^{\frac{1}{8}})^{-k}

\displaystyle+Nk^{-1}||\sigma_{a}^{-k}\partial_{\tau}\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,\xi}\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},3\xi)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}+N||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}(\log r)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{X}_{\rho,\xi}\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},3\xi)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])},

where
\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}=\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2;a,\rho}
as defined in (
5.42
). Next, instead of sending
\xi\rightarrow\infty
as in
[
29
]
, we let
\xi=8\rho
. Then, we choose a number
k_{1}
depending on
\rho
such that
4N^{2}\log(24\rho)\leq k_{1}\leq 16N^{2}\log(24\rho)
. Observing that the maximum of
\log r
on
A(\rho/6,24\rho)
is
\log(24\rho)
, we obtain the following absorption for
k\geq k_{1}
.

k^{\frac{1}{2}}-N(\log(24\rho))^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq\frac{k^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}.

Therefore, after increasing
N
by a factor of 2 and taking
k\geq k_{1}
,

\displaystyle k^{\frac{1}{2}}||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}+||\sigma_{a}^{-k}\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad+||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\leq N||\sigma_{a}^{-k}(\partial_{\tau}+\Delta)\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad+N(\rho+k^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-\frac{1}{2}}(ae^{\frac{1}{8}})^{-k}+Nk^{-1}||\sigma_{a}^{-k}\partial_{\tau}\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}.

Although we have absorbed the logarithmic growth term, we have allowed
k_{1}
to depend on
\rho
which will introduce a new growth term later on. Next, we apply (
6.2
) on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r}\times[0,1/4]
where
r\geq r_{9}(\epsilon)
to obtain the following inequalities.

\bigg|\frac{\partial\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}}{\partial t}+\Delta\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}\bigg|\leq\epsilon\big(|\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}|\big)+\psi_{\rho,8\rho}|\vartheta^{\prime}||\mathbf{X}|\\
+\vartheta\big(2|\nabla\psi_{\rho,8\rho}||\nabla\mathbf{X}|+\epsilon|\nabla\psi_{\rho,8\rho}||\mathbf{X}|+|\Delta\psi_{\rho,8\rho}||\mathbf{X}|\big)

\bigg|\frac{\partial\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}}{\partial t}\bigg|\leq N\big(|\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|\big)+\epsilon|\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}|+N\vartheta|\nabla\psi_{\rho,8\rho}||\mathbf{X}|+\psi_{\rho,8\rho}|\vartheta^{\prime}||\mathbf{Y}|

Making
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, and taking
k_{1}
large enough that
N/k_{1}<\epsilon
, we can reabsorb the terms involving
|\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|,|\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|,
and
|\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}|
into the right hand side and obtain the following inequality.

\displaystyle||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}(|\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}+||\sigma_{a}^{-k}\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq N||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}(|\mathbf{X}|+|\mathbf{Y}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[\frac{1}{6},\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+N||\sigma_{a}^{-k}(|\mathbf{X}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},\frac{\rho}{3})\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+N||\sigma_{a}^{-k}(|\mathbf{X}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(16\rho,24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+N(\rho+k^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-\frac{1}{2}}(ae^{\frac{1}{8}})^{-k}.

Note that we use the fact that
\sigma_{a}\leq 1
and that as before,
a\in(0,1/8)
. Since
\sigma_{a}\leq 1
and
1\leq e^{(\tau+a)/3}\leq e
, we can further estimate:

(6.5)

\displaystyle||(\tau+a)^{-k}(|\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C^{k}N||(\tau+a)^{-k}(|\mathbf{X}|+|\mathbf{Y}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[\frac{1}{6},\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C^{k}N||(\tau+a)^{-k}(|\mathbf{X}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},\frac{\rho}{3})\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C^{k}N||(\tau+a)^{-k}(|\mathbf{X}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(16\rho,24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+N(\rho+k^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-\frac{1}{2}}(ae^{\frac{1}{8}})^{-k}.

The first term on the right-hand side of the inequality can be estimated by the bounds (
6.1
) and
(\tau+a)\geq 1/6
.

||(\tau+a)^{-k}(|\mathbf{X}|+|\mathbf{Y}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[\frac{1}{6},\frac{1}{5}])}\leq 6^{k}||N||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[\frac{1}{6},\frac{1}{5}])}\leq C^{k}N\rho^{\frac{7}{2}}

for all
k\geq k_{1}
and
a\in(0,1/8)
.

On the domains
A(\frac{\rho}{6},\frac{\rho}{3})\times[0,\frac{1}{5}]
and
A(16\rho,24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}]
, we have that

e^{-(r-\rho)^{2}/(8(\tau+a))}\leq e^{-\rho^{2}/(18(\tau+a))}.

We compute using Stirling’s formula that

\max_{s>0}s^{-k}e^{-\rho^{2}/(18s)}=\rho^{-2k}(18k)^{k}e^{-k}\leq\rho^{-2k}C^{k}k!.

This observation yields the following bound.

||(\tau+a)^{-k}(|\mathbf{X}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},\frac{\rho}{3})\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}\\
+||(\tau+a)^{-k}(|\mathbf{X}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(16\rho,24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}\leq N\rho^{-2k}C^{k}k!\rho^{\frac{7}{2}},

for
k\geq k_{1}
and
a\in(0,1/8)
. Let
l
be such that
k=k_{1}+l
. Plugging the previous two estimates back into (
6.5
), we find

(6.6)

\displaystyle||(\tau+a)^{-k}(|\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq N\rho^{\frac{7}{2}}C^{k_{1}+l}(1+\rho^{-2(k_{1}+l)}(k_{1}+l)!)+N(\rho+(k_{1}+l)^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-\frac{1}{2}}(ae^{\frac{1}{8}})^{-(k_{1}+l)}

for all
l\geq 0
and all
a\in(0,1/8)
. We use several inequalities which we collect here:

(l+k_{1})!\leq C^{k_{1}+l}(l!)(k_{1}!),\;\;l^{\frac{7}{4}}e^{-\frac{l}{16}}\leq C,\;\;(\tau+a)^{-k_{1}}\geq 1.

In addition, for
r_{10}
sufficiently large, if we recall that
k_{1}\leq 16N^{2}\log 24\rho
we can deduce that

\displaystyle(\rho+(k_{1}+l)^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{7}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(\rho+k_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+l^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{7}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(2\rho+l^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{7}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{\frac{7}{2}}(1+\big(\tfrac{l}{\sqrt{2\rho}}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{7}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{\frac{7}{2}}l^{\frac{7}{4}}

Note that in each of these inequalities,
C>0
is a universal constant. Plugging these in to (
6.6
), we find

\displaystyle||(\tau+a)^{-k}(|\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|)\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq N\rho^{\frac{7}{2}}(C^{k_{1}}C^{l}+C^{k_{1}}(k_{1}!)C^{l}\rho^{-2l}(l!)+a^{-\frac{1}{2}-k_{1}}(l^{\frac{7}{4}}e^{-\frac{l}{16}})(ae^{\frac{1}{16}})^{-l})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq N\rho^{\frac{7}{2}}(C^{k_{1}}C^{l}+C^{k_{1}}(k_{1}!)C^{l}\rho^{-2l}(l!)+a^{-\frac{1}{2}-k_{1}}(ae^{\frac{1}{16}})^{-l})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq N(k_{1}!)^{2}\rho^{\frac{7}{2}}(C^{l}+C^{l}\rho^{-2l}(l!)+a^{-\frac{1}{2}-k_{1}}(ae^{\frac{1}{16}})^{-l})

Note that since
C>0
is a universal constant, we may set
r_{10}
to be sufficiently large that
k_{1}!\geq C^{k_{1}}
: thus, we can bound all terms involving
k_{1}
by
(k_{1}!)^{2}
, with the possible exception of
a^{-(k_{1}+1/2)}
. We multiply both sides by
\rho^{2l}/((2C)^{l}l!)
and sum over all
l\geq 0
yielding

\displaystyle||(|\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|)e^{\frac{\rho^{2}}{C(\tau+a)}-\frac{(r-\rho)^{2}}{8(\tau+a)}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq N(k_{1}!)^{2}\rho^{\frac{7}{2}}\bigg(1+e^{\frac{\rho^{2}}{2}}+a^{-(k_{1}+\frac{1}{2})}e^{\frac{\rho^{2}}{Cae^{1/6}}}\bigg).

Write
e^{1/16}=1+2\delta
and restrict the time interval from
[0,1/5]
to
[0,\delta a]
. Then

\displaystyle||(|\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|)e^{-\frac{(r-\rho)^{2}}{8(\tau+a)}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\delta\alpha])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq N(k_{1}!)^{2}\rho^{\frac{7}{2}}\bigg(\bigg(1+e^{\frac{\rho^{2}}{2}}\bigg)e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}}{Ca(1+\delta)}}+a^{-(k_{1}+\frac{1}{2})}e^{\frac{-\delta\rho^{2}}{Ca(1+2\delta)(1+\delta)}}\bigg).

All that remains is to find a constant depending on
k_{1}
which bounds

a^{-(k_{1}+1/2)}e^{-\delta\rho^{2}/(2Ca(1+2\delta)(1+\delta))}

from above. The maximum of this quantity as a function of
a
occurs at its sole critical point on the positive real axis.

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{da}\big(a^{-(k_{1}+1/2)}e^{-\delta\rho^{2}/(2Ca(1+2\delta)(1+\delta))}\big)

\displaystyle=-(k_{1}+1/2)a^{-1}a^{-(k_{1}+1/2)}e^{-\delta\rho^{2}/(2Ca(1+2\delta)(1+\delta))}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\delta\rho^{2}}{2Ca^{2}(1+2\delta)(1+\delta)}a^{-(k_{1}+1/2)}e^{-\delta\rho^{2}/(2Ca(1+2\delta)(1+\delta))}

\displaystyle=-(k_{1}+1/2)a^{-1}+\frac{\delta\rho^{2}a^{-2}}{2C(1+2\delta)(1+\delta)}

Thus, the critical point occurs at

(6.7)

a_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=a=\frac{2C(1+2\delta)(1+\delta)(k_{1}+1/2)}{\delta\rho^{2}}

To find an upper bound, we plug
a_{0}
into
a^{-(k_{1}+1/2)}e^{-\delta\rho^{2}/(2Ca(1+2\delta)(1+\delta))}
.

\displaystyle a_{0}^{-(k_{1}+1/2)}e^{-\delta\rho^{2}/(2Ca_{0}(1+2\delta)(1+\delta))}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{C(k_{1}+1/2)}{\rho^{2}}\bigg)^{-(k_{1}+1/2)}\leq(\rho^{2})^{2k_{1}}

This estimate allows us to obtain the following upper bound.

||(|\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|)e^{-\frac{(r-\rho)^{2}}{8(\tau+a)}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\delta\alpha])}\leq N(\rho^{4k_{1}})(k_{1}^{k_{1}})^{2}e^{\frac{-\rho^{2}}{Ca}},

for all
0<a\leq 1/C
. Recall that
k_{1}\leq 16N^{2}\log(24\rho)\leq 17N^{2}\log(\rho)
. Then,

\displaystyle\rho^{4k_{1}}\cdot k_{1}^{2k_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{68N^{2}\log(\rho)}\cdot 17N^{2}\log(\rho)^{17N^{2}\log(\rho)}

\displaystyle=\exp(68N^{2}\log(\rho)\log(\rho)+17N^{2}\log(\rho)(\log(17N^{2})+\log(\log(\rho))))

\displaystyle\leq\exp(N(\log\rho)^{2}),

where we have simply increased
N
if necessary. This grows much more slowly than quadratic exponential. By possibly increasing
r_{10}
, we finally obtain

||(|\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\mathbf{Y}_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}_{\rho,8\rho}|)e^{-\frac{(r-\rho)^{2}}{8(\tau+a)}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\delta\alpha])}\leq Ne^{\frac{-\rho^{2}}{Ca}},

Observe that
e^{-|r-\rho|^{2}/(8(\tau+a))}\geq N^{-1}
on the domain
A((1-\sqrt{\delta a})\rho,(1+\sqrt{\delta a})\rho)\times[0,\delta a]
. Furthermore, on this set,
\psi_{\rho,8\rho}\equiv 1
and
\varphi\equiv 1
. Thus, the cut-off tensor fields agree with their unmodified counterparts on this restricted domain and we obtain the estimate

(6.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\||\mathbf{X}|+|\nabla\mathbf{X}|+|\mathbf{Y}|\|_{L^{2}(A((1-\sqrt{s})\rho,(1+\sqrt{s})\rho)\times[0,s])}\leq Ne^{-\frac{\rho^{2}}{Cs}}\end{split}

for
s\in[0,1/C]
and arbitrary
\rho\geq 12r_{10}
, which was the desired exponential decay.
∎

6.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

The main theorem, Theorem
1.1
, follows immediately from the following proposition:

Proposition 6.3
.

[
29
, Claim 6.3]

There exist
\tau^{\prime}\in(0,1)
and
r_{11}=r_{11}(K_{0})
such that
\mathbf{X}\equiv 0
and
\mathbf{Y}\equiv 0
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{11}}\times[0,\tau^{\prime}]
.

Proof.

The proof is identical to that of
[
29
, Claim 6.3]
, after replacing
[
29
, Claim 6.2]
by Proposition
6.2
,
[
29
, (6.2)]
by (
6.2
),
[
29
, (4.13)]
by (
4.28
), and
[
29
, Proposition 4.9]
by Proposition
5.7
.
∎

This immediately implies Theorem
4.14
. By the reduction of Theorem
1.1
to Theorem
4.14
in Section
4
, this proves Theorem
1.1
.

6.3.
Proof of Corollary
1.2

We may prove Corollary
1.2
using Theorem
1.1
.

From Theorem
1.1
, we have that there exist ends
W_{1}\subset V_{1}
and
W_{2}\subset V_{2}
and a diffeomorphism
\Psi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W_{1}\to W_{2}
such that
\Psi^{*}\varphi_{2}=\varphi_{1}
.

From the proof of Theorem
1.1
, we have that
W_{1}
is diffeomorphic to
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}
for some
r_{1}>r
, but by assumption
V_{1}
is diffeomorphic to
C_{r}^{\Sigma}
for
r\leq r_{1}
. Thus, the inclusion map
\iota\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W_{1}\hookrightarrow V_{1}
induces an isomorphism
\pi_{1}(W_{1},x_{0})\cong\pi_{1}(V_{1},x_{0})
. Likewise,
\pi_{1}(W_{2},y_{0})\cong\pi_{1}(V_{2},y_{0})
. Thus, the homomorphisms

(6.9)

\pi_{1}(W_{1},x_{0})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(M_{1},x_{0}),\,\,\pi_{1}(W_{2},x_{0})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(M_{2},x_{0})

induced by inclusion are surjective.

Since
\Psi^{*}\varphi_{2}=\varphi_{1}
, we have that
\Psi^{*}g_{\varphi_{2}}=g_{\varphi_{1}}
. Thus,
\Psi
is an isometry from
W_{1}
to
W_{2}
. By
[
30
, Theorem 5.2]
, (
6.9
) implies that
\Psi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W_{1}\to W_{2}
extends to an isometry
\Psi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}(M_{1},g_{\varphi_{1}})\to(M_{2},g_{\varphi_{2}})
. Note that this is in particular a diffeomorphism.

By
[
36
, Cor. 1.2]
, Laplacian shrinkers are real analytic. Since an isometry between real analytic manifolds is itself real analytic, we find that
\Psi
is real analytic. Defining
h\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Psi^{*}\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{1}
, we see that
h
is real analytic, and
h\equiv 0
on
W_{1}
. Since our shrinkers are connected by assumption, we find that
h\equiv 0
everywhere on
M_{1}
. This gives a diffeomorphism
\Psi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}M_{1}\to M_{2}
such that
\Psi^{*}\varphi_{2}=\varphi_{1}
and concludes the Corollary.

7.
Automorphisms of AC
G_{2}
-Shrinkers

For a closed
G_{2}
-structure
(M,\varphi)
, we define
\Aut(M,\varphi)
by

\Aut(M,\varphi)=\{F\in\Diff(M)\,|\,F^{*}\varphi=\varphi\}.

We will call any such
F
satisfying
F^{*}\varphi=\varphi
an automorphism of the
G_{2}
-structure
\varphi
. Note that if
F\in\Aut(M,\varphi)
, then
F\in\Isom(M,g_{\varphi})
.

In this section, we adapt the arguments of
[
30
]
to the Laplacian flow setting. We prove the following theorem, which implies Theorem
1.3
.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi,f)
be a gradient Laplacian shrinker which is dynamically asymptotic to a closed
G_{2}
-cone
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
. Then,
\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi)=\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
as subsets of
\Diff({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma})
.

7.1.
Theorem
7.1
implies Theorem
1.3

If
(M,\varphi,f)
is asymptotic to
({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
along the end
V
, then by Proposition
4.1
, there is
r>0
, an end
W\subset V
, and a diffeomorphism
F\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma}\to W
such that
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},F^{*}\varphi,F^{*}f)
is dynamically asymptotic to
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
. For
\gamma\in\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},F^{*}\varphi)
,
F\circ\gamma\circ F^{-1}
is an automorphism of
(W,\varphi)
, and in fact,

\gamma\mapsto F\circ\gamma\circ F^{-1}

is an isomorphism from
\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},F^{*}\varphi)
to
\Aut(W,\varphi)
. Assuming Theorem
7.1
,

\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},F^{*}\varphi)=\Aut({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C}).

This shows that
\Aut({\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
and
\Aut(W,\varphi)
are isomorphic by the desired map, and thus that Theorem
7.1
implies Theorem
1.3
.

7.2.
Real analytic structure of the flow

A
G_{2}
-structure
(M,\varphi)
is real analytic if
M
is a real analytic manifold, and there is a subatlas of real analytic coordinate systems on
M
, such that the components
(g_{\varphi})_{ij}
of
g_{\varphi}
and
\varphi_{ijk}
of
\varphi
are real analytic functions in these coordinates. In fact, when we say that
(M,\varphi)
is real analytic, we always mean that
g_{\varphi}
and
\varphi
are real analytic relative to the subatlas of normal coordinates induced by
g_{\varphi}
, i.e.
(g_{\varphi})_{ij}
and
\varphi_{ijk}
are real analytic functions in
g_{\varphi}
-normal coordinates. See
[
36
, §3]
for additional background.

In this subsection, we follow the idea of
[
30
, §3]
to prove the constancy in time of the real analytic structure of Laplacian flow. This will be used to prove Theorem
7.1
. The next lemma follows the idea of
[
30
, §3]
.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
\varphi_{t}
be a smooth, possibly incomplete solution to Laplacian flow on
{\mathcal{D}}\subset M
for
t\in[0,T_{0}]
. Let
t_{0}\in(0,T_{0})
, and let
U=B_{r}(x_{0})
be a
g_{t_{0}}
-normal coordinate patch in
({\mathcal{D}},\varphi_{t_{0}})
of radius
r>0
around
x_{0}
such that in
U
-coordinates
(g_{t_{0}})_{ij}
are real analytic in
U
.

Then, for any
x_{0}
in
V=B_{r/4}(x_{0})
, there exists
\epsilon>0
, depending only on
r
,
T_{0}-t_{0}
, and
C_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sup_{U\times[0,T_{0}]}|\Rm|_{g_{t}}(x,t)
, such that in
U
-coordinates,

\sup_{V\times[t_{0},t_{0}+\epsilon]}\Big(|\partial^{k}g|(x,t)+|\partial^{k}\varphi|(x,t)\Big)\leq CL^{k}k!

for all
k\geq 0
, where
C
and
L
are constants depending only on
r
,
T_{0}-t_{0}
,
C_{0}
, and
g_{t_{0}}
. Moreover, if
C_{0}
and
r
are fixed, then
\epsilon=T_{0}-t_{0}
for all
t_{0}
close enough to
T_{0}
.

Remark 7.1
.

The dependence of
\epsilon
on
r
,
C_{0}
, and
T_{0}-t_{0}
is such that
\epsilon
is uniformly bounded below in terms of a lower bound on
r
and
T_{0}-t_{0}
and and upper bound on
C_{0}
.

Proof.

First, we recall that for any closed
G_{2}
-structure,
\nabla T=\Rm\ast\varphi+T\ast T\ast\varphi
, where
T
is the torsion of the
G_{2}
-structure. Since
|\varphi|_{g}=7
and
R=-|T|^{2}
, there exists a universal constant
C
such that
|\nabla T|\leq C|\Rm|
. If

\Lambda(x,t)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(|\Rm|^{2}(x,t)+|\nabla T|^{2}(x,t))^{1/2},

then
\Lambda(x,t)\leq C|\Rm|(x,t)
for some universal constant
C
. Thus,

(7.1)

\sup_{V\times[0,T_{0}]}\Lambda(x,t)\leq CC_{0}.

Combining (
7.1
) with Lotay–Wei’s local estimates of real analyticity
[
36
, Theorem 6.3]
, for any
s_{0}\in(0,T_{0}]
, there exist
C,L,\epsilon_{1}>0
, depending only on
T_{0}-s_{0}
,
C_{0}
, and
r
, such that

\sup_{V}\,(t-s_{0})^{k/2}\Big(|\nabla^{k}\Rm|(x,t)+|\nabla^{k+1}T|(x,t)+|\nabla^{k+2}\varphi|(x,t)\Big)\leq CL^{k/2}(k+1)!

for all
k\geq 0
and
t\in(0,T_{0}]
such that
t\in(s_{0},s_{0}+\epsilon_{1}]
. By the argument in
[
36
, Theorem 6.3]
, if
C_{0}
and
r
are bounded, then for all
T_{0}-s_{0}
small enough,
\epsilon_{1}=T_{0}-s_{0}
. Since
\epsilon_{1}
only depends on
T_{0}-s_{0}
,
C_{0}
, and
r
, then for any
t_{0}\in(0,T_{0}]
, there exists
s_{0}\in[0,t_{0})
and
\epsilon>0
such that

[t_{0}-\epsilon,t_{0}+\epsilon]\cap(0,T_{0}]\subset(s_{0}+\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{2},s_{0}+\epsilon_{1})\cap(0,T_{0}].

Then, there exists a relabelled constant
L
, only modified by a constant depending on
\epsilon_{1}
, such that

(7.2)

\sup_{V\times{\mathcal{I}}}\Big(|\nabla^{k}\Rm|+|\nabla^{k+1}T|+|\nabla^{k+2}\varphi|\Big)\leq CL^{k/2}(k+1)!

where
{\mathcal{I}}=[t_{0}-\epsilon,t_{0}+\epsilon]\cap(0,T_{0}]
.

Now, recall from
[
28
, §3]
that there exists a universal constant
a>0
such that

m!n!\leq(m+n)!\leq a^{m+n}m!n!

This implies that

CL^{k/2}(k+1)!\leq 6Ca(La^{2})^{k/2}(k-2)!

We rewrite the bound (
7.2
) as

(7.3)

\sup_{V\times{\mathcal{I}}}\Big(|\nabla^{k}\Rm|+|\nabla^{k+1}T|+|\nabla^{k+2}\varphi|\Big)\leq CL^{k/2}(k-2)!

where
C
and
L
are relabelled constants which have only been modified by universal constants. Now that we have estimates of the form (
7.3
), we may apply results from
[
28
, §8]
, as in
[
30
, Proof of Theorem 1.3]
.

First, define

B_{ij}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Ric_{ij}+\frac{1}{3}|T|^{2}g_{ij}+2T_{i}^{\;l}T_{lj},

so that
\frac{\partial g_{ij}}{\partial t}=-2B_{ij}
. Taking covariant derivatives of
B_{ij}
with respect to
g(t)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{V\times{\mathcal{I}}}|\nabla^{k}B|-\sup_{V\times{\mathcal{I}}}|\nabla^{k}\Ric|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{V\times{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{n=0}^{k}\binom{k}{n}|\nabla^{k-n}T||\nabla^{n}T|

\displaystyle\leq a^{k}\sup_{V\times{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{n=0}^{k}|\nabla^{k-n}T||\nabla^{n}T|

\displaystyle\leq a^{k}C^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{k}L^{\frac{k-n-1}{2}}L^{\frac{n-1}{2}}(k-n-3)!(n-3)!

\displaystyle\leq a^{k}C^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{k}L^{\frac{k}{2}-1}(k-6)!

(7.4)

\displaystyle\leq a^{k}(k+1)C^{2}L^{\frac{k}{2}-1}(k-6)!

After modifying
C
and
L
by universal constants,

(7.5)

\sup_{V\times{\mathcal{I}}}|\nabla^{k}B|\leq CL^{\frac{k}{2}}(k-2)!

for all
k\geq 0
. Now, let
\nabla
be the Levi-Civita connection of
g_{t}
, and for
t_{0}\in(0,T_{0})
, let
\bar{g}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=g_{t_{0}}
,
\nabla^{\bar{g}}=\overline{\nabla}
, and
G\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\nabla-\overline{\nabla}
. Then, for
t_{0}\in(0,T_{0})
, we apply
[
28
, Proposition 27]
with (
7.5
) on
[t_{0},t_{0}+\epsilon]\cap(0,T_{0}]
to find relabelled constants
\epsilon
,
C
, and
L
, depending only on
r
,
T_{0}-t_{0}
, and
C_{0}
, such that

(7.6)

\sup_{V\times[t_{0},t_{0}+\epsilon]}|\nabla^{k}G|(x,t)\leq CL^{k/2}(k-2)!

for all
k\geq 0
. Here,
[t_{0},t_{0}+\epsilon]\subset(0,T_{0}]
by the new choice of
\epsilon
. Indeed, by the argument in
[
30
, Proposition 27]
, if
C
and
L
are bounded, then for all
t_{0}
close enough to
T_{0}
, we have that
\epsilon=T_{0}-t_{0}
. By
[
28
, Proposition 25]
, (
7.3
) and (
7.6
) imply that there exist relabelled constants
C
and
L
depending only on
r
,
T_{0}-t_{0}
, and
C_{0}
, such that

(7.7)

\sup_{V\times[t_{0},t_{0}+\epsilon]}|\overline{\nabla}^{k}\varphi|\leq CL^{k/2}(k-2)!

Since
|\nabla^{k}g|=0
for all
k\geq 0
, estimates of the form (
7.3
) automatically hold for
|\nabla^{k}g|
as well. Just as for (
7.7
), Proposition 25 of
[
28
]
goes to show that

(7.8)

\sup_{V\times[t_{0},t_{0}+\epsilon]}|\overline{\nabla}^{k}g|\leq CL^{k/2}(k-2)!

By assumption,
\bar{g}
is analytic in
U
, so the Christoffel symbols
\bar{\Gamma}_{ij}^{k}
are also analytic in
U
. Thus, for each
x_{0}\in U
,

|\partial^{k}\bar{\Gamma}|\leq\tilde{C}\tilde{L}k!

in some neighborhood of
x_{0}
, where
\tilde{C}
and
\tilde{L}
are constants not depending on
k
. Using a covering argument on the
\bar{g}
-closure of
V
, there exist constants
\tilde{C}
and
\tilde{L}
such that in a neighborhood of
x_{0}
,

(7.9)

|\partial^{k}\bar{\Gamma}|\leq\tilde{C}\tilde{L}k!

for all
k\geq 0
. Writing
\bar{\Gamma}=\bar{\nabla}-\partial
, we may combine (
7.9
) with (
7.8
) and (
7.7
) to find constants
C
and
L
such that

(7.10)

\sup_{V\times[t_{0},t_{0}+\epsilon]}\Big(|\partial^{k}g|(x,t)+|\partial^{k}\varphi|(x,t)\Big)\leq CL^{k}k!

for all
k\geq 0
. The constants
\tilde{C}
and
\tilde{L}
from (
7.9
) depend on
\bar{g}=g_{t_{0}}
in
U
, and the constants
C,L
in (
7.8
) and (
7.7
) depend on
T_{0}-t_{0}
,
C_{0}
, and
r
. This gives the desired dependence for
C
,
L
in (
7.10
). Similarly,
\epsilon
depends on
\epsilon_{1}
, which itself depends only on
T_{0}-t_{0}
,
r
, and
C_{0}
. This gives the desired dependence for
\epsilon
, and the statement for
t_{0}
close to
T_{0}
follows from the arguments in
[
36
, Theorem 6.3]
and
[
28
, Proposition 27]
.
∎

Proposition 7.3
.

Suppose
({\mathcal{D}},\varphi_{t})
is a smooth, possibly incomplete solution to Laplacian flow on
{\mathcal{D}}\subset M
for
t\in[0,T_{0}]
. Then,
({\mathcal{D}},\varphi_{t})
is real analytic with respect to the same analytic structure for all
t\in(0,T_{0}]
. In fact,
g_{t}
and
\varphi_{t}
are real analytic relative to the subatlas of normal coordinates induced by
g_{t_{0}}
for any
t_{0}\in(0,T_{0}]
.

Proof.

By
[
36
, Theorem 1.1]
, for each
t\in(0,T_{0}]
, the Laplacian flow
({\mathcal{D}},\varphi_{t})
is real analytic with respect to the analytic structure
{\mathcal{A}}_{t}
induced by the subatlas of
g_{t}
-normal coordinate systems. To conclude the lemma, we will show that
({\mathcal{D}},\varphi_{t})
is real analytic with respect to the subatlas of
g_{t_{0}}
-normal coordinate systems for any
t_{0}\in(0,T_{0}]
.

The key fact is the following from
[
30
, §3]
: if a fixed metric
g
is analytic in both of the overlapping smooth charts
(U,\phi)
and
(V,\psi)
, then these two charts belong to the same analytic structure. The reason is that the transition function between the two charts is an isometry on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
of analytic metrics and is therefore real analytic itself. Since the transition function is real analytic, the two charts belong to the same analytic structure.

Fix
x_{0}\in M
. By
[
36
, Remark 6.4]
, if
({\mathcal{D}},\varphi_{t})
is a smooth solution to Laplacian flow in an open set
{\mathcal{D}}\subset M
for
t\in[0,T_{0}]
, then
\sup_{{\mathcal{D}}\times[0,T_{0}]}|\Rm|_{g_{t}}(x,t)<\infty
. Let
U
be a normal coordinate chart for
g_{t_{0}}
, for
t_{0}\in(0,T_{0})
. By Lemma
7.2
,
g_{t}
is analytic with respect to
U
-coordinates in
V\subset U
for
t\in[t_{0},t_{0}+\epsilon]
. For
t_{0}
close enough to
T_{0}
, we have that
\epsilon=T_{0}-t_{0}
so that
g_{t}
is analytic with respect to
U
-coordinates in
V\subset U
for
t\in[t_{0},T_{0}]
.

For
t\in[t_{0},t_{0}+\epsilon]
, let
W_{t}
be a
g_{t}
-normal coordinate chart centered at
x_{0}
. Since
g_{t}
is analytic in
W_{t}
-coordinates and in
U=W_{t_{0}}
-coordinates, which overlap in a neighborhood of
x_{0}
, the chart
W_{t}
belongs to the same real analytic structure as
U
. This argument applies for any choice of
t_{0}\in(0,T_{0})
. By the connectedness of
(0,T_{0}]
, for each
t\in(0,T_{0}]
, the chart
W_{t}
belongs to the same real analytic structure as the chart
U=W_{t_{0}}
for any given
t_{0}\in(0,T_{0}]
. Moreover, both
g_{t}
and
\varphi_{t}
are real analytic in the chart
U
for any given
t_{0}\in(0,T_{0}]
. This argument holds for any
x_{0}\in M
, so the real analytic structure on
M
associated to the subatlas of
g_{t}
-normal coordinate charts is the same for each
t\in(0,T_{0}]
. The conclusion of the proposition follows.
∎

The next corollary follows just as in
[
30
, Corollary 3.2]
.

Corollary 7.4
.

Let
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi,f)
be a gradient Laplacian shrinker which is dynamically asymptotic to a closed
G_{2}
-cone
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
. Then,
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi)
and
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
are real analytic relative to the same analytic structure.

Proof.

By the definition of dynamically asymptotic, we may apply Proposition
7.3
to conclude this corollary.
∎

7.3.
Proof of Theorem
7.1

From Proposition
4.1
, we let
\varphi_{\tau}=\tau^{3/2}\Psi_{\tau}^{*}\varphi
be the family satisfying (
4.1
). Let
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi,f)
be a gradient Laplacian shrinker which is dynamically asymptotic to a closed
G_{2}
-cone
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
F\in\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{\tau})
for some
\tau\in(0,1]
. Then, either
g_{\tau}
is flat or
F^{*}(\nabla f)=\nabla f
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma}
.

Proof.

To prove this lemma, we follow the framework given in
[
30
, Lemma 4.1]
. The proof of
[
30
, Lemma 4.1]
depends on
[
30
, Proposition 2.1]
. Our analogue of that proposition is Proposition
4.1
. The proof of this lemma works exactly the same as in
[
30
]
using Proposition
4.1
and the fact that
F\in\Isom({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},g_{\tau})
if
F\in\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{\tau})
, modulo two minor changes. To find estimates on the time-dependent potential function
f_{\tau}
as in
[
30
, Lemma 4.1]
, we simply treat the
T\ast T
terms in (
4.6
) as decay terms at infinity using (
4.3
) and the fact that
R=-|T|^{2}
. By the argument in
[
30
, Lemma 4.1]
, we find
r_{1}>r
such that
g(\tau)
is flat on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}
. By
[
36
, Corollary 1.2]
, Laplacian solitons are real analytic and in particular, the associated metrics are also real analytic. This implies that
g_{\tau}
is flat on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma}
.
∎

Remark 7.2
.

The proof in
[
30
, Lemma 4.1]
shows that
f\circ F=f+C
for some constant
C
. This is equivalent to
F^{*}(\nabla f)=\nabla f
using the fact that
F
is an isometry.

Lemma 7.6
.

Suppose that
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi,f)
is a gradient Laplacian shrinker which is dynamically asymptotic to a closed
G_{2}
-cone
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
. Then, for any
F\in\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
, there is
r_{1}\geq r
such that
F|_{{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}}\in\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma},\varphi)
.

Proof.

As in
[
30
, Proposition 4.2]
, we have that
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},F^{*}\varphi,F^{*}f)
is dynamically asymptotic to
({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
. Applying the backward uniqueness found in the proof of Theorem
1.1
,
\varphi=F^{*}\varphi
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{a}\times[0,T_{0}]
for some
a>r
and
T_{0}\in(0,1)
. As in
[
30
, Proposition 4.2]
, we find that
\varphi_{1}=F^{*}\varphi_{1}
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{b}
for some
b\geq a
. This concludes the lemma.
∎

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
T_{1}\in[0,1]
, and let
r_{2}\geq r_{1}\geq r
. Suppose
F\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma}
is an injective local diffeomorphism such that
F^{*}\varphi_{T_{1}}=\varphi_{T_{1}}
. If
F^{*}\varphi_{T_{2}}=\varphi_{T_{2}}
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{2}}^{\Sigma}
for some
T_{2}\in[0,1]
, then
F^{*}\varphi_{T_{2}}=\varphi_{T_{2}}
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}
.

Proof.

This follows much the same idea as in
[
30
, Proposition 4.3]
.

By Corollary
7.4
,
\varphi_{T_{1}}
and
\varphi_{T_{2}}
and the metrics
g_{T_{1}}
and
g_{T_{2}}
are all real analytic with respect to the same analytic structure
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Since
F^{*}\varphi_{T_{1}}=\varphi_{T_{1}}
,
F
preserves the metric
g_{T_{1}}
and thus is a local isometry of
g_{T_{1}}
. This implies that
F
is real analytic on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{r_{1}}
with respect to
{\mathcal{A}}
, and so

h\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=F^{*}\varphi_{T_{2}}-\varphi_{T_{2}}

is also real analytic on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}
. By assumption,
h\equiv 0
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{2}}^{\Sigma}
, so the real analyticity of
h
implies that
h\equiv 0
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}
.
∎

Recall that
\varphi_{\tau}=\tau^{3/2}\Psi_{\tau}^{*}\varphi
, where
\Psi_{\tau}
is the map defined in Proposition
4.1
. Note that
\varphi_{1}=\varphi
.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
T_{0}\in(0,1]
, and let
r_{1}\geq r
. Suppose
F\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}
is an injective local diffeomorphism such that
F^{*}\varphi_{T_{0}}=\varphi_{T_{0}}
. Then,
F^{*}\varphi=\varphi
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}
.

Proof.

This follows much the same as in
[
30
, Proposition 4.4]
.

If
\varphi_{T_{0}}
is torsion-free on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}
, then
\varphi
is torsion-free on
\Psi_{T}({\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma})
. By the local real analyticity of Laplacian solitons
[
36
, Corollary 1.2]
,
\varphi
must be torsion-free on all of
{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma}
. This then implies that the Laplacian flow
\tau\mapsto\varphi_{\tau}
constructed in Proposition
4.1
is a flow of torsion-free
G_{2}
-structures and hence a fixed point for the Laplacian flow. Thus,
\varphi=\varphi_{T_{0}}
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}
, and the lemma follows in this case.

Now, we may suppose that
\varphi_{T_{0}}
is not torsion-free. Since
R=-|T|^{2}
, this implies that
g_{T_{0}}
is not flat. As in
[
30
, Proposition 4.4]
, this allows us to apply Lemma
7.5
as well as Remark
7.2
and (
4.4
) to find that there is
b\geq r_{1}
depending on
T_{0}
,
r_{1}
, and
N_{0}
from (
4.4
), such that

F\circ\Psi_{\tau}=\Psi_{\tau}\circ F

on
{\mathcal{C}}_{b}^{\Sigma}
for all
\tau\in(0,1]
. It follows immediately that
F^{*}\varphi=\varphi
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\Sigma}_{b/\sqrt{T}}
. An application of Lemma
7.7
shows that
F^{*}\varphi=\varphi
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}
.∎

Proof of Theorem
7.1
.

This follows with the same idea as
[
30
, Proof of Theorem 1.2]
.

Suppose that
F\in\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
. Lemma
7.6
gives
r_{1}\geq r
such that
F|_{{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}}\in\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma},\varphi)
. Then, Lemma
7.7
implies that
F\in\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi)
. Thus,

\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})\subseteq\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi).

Now, suppose that
F\in\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi)
. If
\varphi
is torsion-free, then by construction,
\varphi_{\tau}
is torsion-free and thus
\tau\mapsto\varphi_{\tau}
is a fixed point of the Laplacian flow. This implies that
\varphi=\varphi_{0}=\varphi_{C}
, and the Theorem follows in this case.

We may assume that
\varphi
is not torsion-free. By Lemma
7.5
,
F^{*}\nabla f=\nabla f
. Now, for the local diffeomorphisms
\Psi_{\tau}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma}
defined by Proposition
4.1
, both
\Psi_{\tau}
and
F^{-1}\circ\Psi_{\tau}\circ F
satisfy equation (
4.7
) since
F
is an isometry. This implies that
\Psi_{\tau}=F^{-1}\circ\Psi_{\tau}\circ F
, and so

F\circ\Psi_{\tau}=\Psi_{\tau}\circ F

for all
\tau\in(0,1]
.

Thus, for any
\tau\in(0,1]
,

F^{*}\varphi_{\tau}=\tau^{3/2}F^{*}\Psi^{*}_{\tau}\varphi=\tau^{3/2}\Psi^{*}_{\tau}F^{*}\varphi=\tau^{3/2}\Psi^{*}_{\tau}\varphi=\varphi_{\tau}.

Taking the limit as
\tau\to 0
,
F^{*}\varphi_{C}=\varphi_{C}
. This shows that
F\in\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C})
, and so

\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi)\subseteq\Aut({\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{\Sigma},\varphi_{C}),

thus proving the theorem.∎

7.4.
Proof of Corollary
1.4

The idea of this proof follows
[
30
, Theorem 1.2]
.

By Theorem
1.3
, there is
r_{1}>0
, an end
W\subset V
of
(M,\varphi)
, and a diffeomorphism
F\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}\to W
such that
\Aut(W,\varphi)\cong\Aut(C,\varphi_{C})
. We have that
W
is diffeomorphic to
{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{1}}^{\Sigma}
, but by assumption
V
is diffeomorphic to
C_{r}^{\Sigma}
for
r\leq r_{1}
. Thus, the inclusion map
\iota\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W\hookrightarrow V
induces an isomorphism
\pi_{1}(W,x_{0})\cong\pi_{1}(V,x_{0})
. By assumption, the map
\pi_{1}(V,x_{0})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(M,x_{0})
induced by inclusion is surjective, so the induced inclusion map
\pi_{1}(W,x_{0})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(M,x_{0})
is surjective. Then, by
[
30
, Theorem 5.2, Proof of Theorem 1.2]
, we have that any isometry
\phi\in\Isom(W,g_{\varphi}|_{W})
can be extended to a unique isometry
\bar{\phi}\in\Isom(M,g_{\varphi})
such that this correspondence gives an isomorphism

\Isom(W,g_{\varphi}|_{W})\cong\Isom(M,g_{\varphi}).

Now, if
F\in\Aut(W,\varphi)
, then
F
is by definition an isometry. Thus,
F\in\Isom(W,g_{\varphi}|_{W})
, and this naturally extends to
\bar{F}\in\Isom(M,g_{\varphi})
. Since
\bar{F}
is an isometry, it is real analytic. Thus, by the real analyticity of
\varphi
,

h\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\bar{F}^{*}\varphi-\varphi

is also real analytic and defined everywhere on
M
. By assumption,
h\equiv 0
on
W
. Thus, by the completeness and connectedness of
M
,
h\equiv 0
on
M
. This implies that
\bar{F}\in\Aut(M,\varphi)
. Thus,
\Aut(W,\varphi)
embeds into
\Aut(M,\varphi)
, and by Theorem
1.3
,
\Aut(C,\varphi_{C})
embeds into
\Aut(M,\varphi)
.

Appendix A
Backward Uniqueness for Subsolutions of Parabolic Equations with Unbounded Coefficients

With the technique used in the proof of Proposition
6.2
, we can prove the following improvement to the backward uniqueness result of
[
10
]
.

Theorem A.1
.

Let
Q_{R,T}
be the parabolic domain
({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus B_{R})\times[0,T]
, and let
u\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}Q_{R,T}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
satisfy

(A.1)

|\Delta u+\partial_{t}u|\leq M|\nabla u|+(N|x|^{\delta}+M)|u|\;\;\text{ and }\;\;|u(x,t)|\leq Me^{M|x|^{2}},

for constants
M,T,R,C,D>0
and
0<\delta<1/2
. Then, if
u(x,0)\equiv 0
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus B_{R}
,
u
vanishes identically in
Q_{R,T}
.

With the rescaling
u=\tfrac{1}{M}\tilde{u}(\omega x,\omega^{2}t)
for some small parameter
\omega
, we may assume that

(A.2)

|\Delta u+\partial_{t}u|\leq\varepsilon(|\nabla u|+|u|)+N|x|^{\delta}|u|\;\;\text{ and }\;\;|u(x,t)|\leq e^{\varepsilon|x|^{2}}.

We will not use the Carleman inequality (1.4) in
[
10
]
, as it discards the crucial coefficient of
\sqrt{\alpha}
on the left-hand side. We instead consider the inequality on page 4 of
[
29
]
, which we restate here.

Lemma A.2
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1
and let
\sigma_{a}(t)=(t+a)e^{-\frac{(t+a)}{3}}
. Then, for all
\rho\geq 1
,
a\in(0,1)
,
\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}(\gamma,n)\geq 0
and
u\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\{|x|\geq\gamma\rho\}\times[0,1))
such that
u(x,0)\equiv 0
, the following inequality holds.

(A.3)

\sqrt{\alpha}||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}u||_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times(0,1))}+||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}\nabla u||_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times(0,1))}\\
\leq C(\gamma,n)||\sigma_{a}^{-\alpha}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}(\partial_{t}+\Delta)u||_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times(0,1))}

Proof.

This inequality is (modulo constants) the inequality (5.23) in
[
29
]
with respect to the static Ricci flow of the Euclidean metric on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
(where
r_{c}^{2}(x)=h(x,t)=|x|
) applied to the
0
-order tensor bundle
{\mathcal{Z}}=C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times(0,1))
.
∎

We prove the
L^{2}
exponential decay property analogous to Proposition
6.2
. As before, we have
A(r_{1},r_{2})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=B_{r_{2}}\setminus B_{r_{1}}
where
0<r_{1}<r_{2}
.

Proposition A.3
.

There exist dimensional constants
s_{0}
,
C
, and
r_{0}
, with
s_{0}\in(0,1]
, such that

(A.4)

|||u|+|\nabla u|||_{L^{2}(A((1-\sqrt{s})\rho,(1+\sqrt{s})\rho)\times[0,s])}\leq Ce^{-\frac{\rho^{2}}{Cs}}

for any
s\in(0,s_{0}]
and
\rho>12r_{0}
.

Proof.

The proof is a simplified version of the proof of Proposition
6.2
. We choose
\gamma=1/12
and set
r_{0}=R
initially. Let
\vartheta\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}},[0,1])
be a temporal cutoff function such that
\vartheta\equiv 1
for
\tau\leq 1/6
and
\vartheta\equiv 0
for
\tau\geq 1/5
. Let
\psi_{\rho,\xi}
be the spatial cutoff function given in Lemma
6.1
. Define
u_{\rho,\xi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\vartheta\psi_{\rho,\xi}u
. As before, in (
A.3
) we replace the parameter
\alpha
by an integer
k\geq\alpha_{0}
and we restrict
a
to the interval
(0,1/8)
. This yields

k^{\frac{1}{2}}||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}u_{\rho,\xi}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},3\xi)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}+||\sigma_{a}^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}\nabla u_{\rho,\xi}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},3\xi)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}\\
\leq C||\sigma_{a}^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}(\partial_{t}+\Delta)u_{\rho,\xi}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},3\xi)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}.

Now, set
\xi=8\rho
. Choose
k_{1}
depending on
\rho
such that
4C^{2}(24\rho)^{2\delta}\leq k_{1}\leq 16C^{2}(24\rho)^{2\delta}
. Since the maximum of
|x|^{\delta}
on
A(\rho/6,3\xi)=A(\rho/6,24\rho)
is
(24\rho)^{\delta}
, the following inequality holds for
k\geq k_{1}
.

(A.5)

k^{\frac{1}{2}}-C(24\rho)^{\delta}\geq\frac{k^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}.

We use (
A.2
) to obtain

|(\partial_{t}+\Delta)u_{\rho,8\rho}|\leq\varepsilon(|u_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla u_{\rho,8\rho}|)+C|x|^{\delta}|u_{\rho,8\rho}|\\
+\psi_{\rho,8\rho}|\vartheta^{\prime}||u|+\vartheta(|u|(|\Delta\psi_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla\psi_{\rho,8\rho}|)+2|\nabla\psi_{\rho,8\rho}||\nabla u|).

For
\varepsilon
sufficiently small and
k\geq k_{1}
,

\displaystyle k^{\frac{1}{2}}||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}u_{\rho,8\rho}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}+||\sigma_{a}^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}\nabla u_{\rho,8\rho}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\leq C(24\rho)^{\delta}||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}u_{\rho,8\rho}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad+C||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}u||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[\frac{1}{6},\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C||\sigma_{a}^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}(|u|+|\nabla u|)||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},\frac{\rho}{3})\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C||\sigma_{a}^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}(|u|+|\nabla u|)||_{L^{2}(A(16\rho,24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

By (
A.5
), we can absorb the first term on the right-hand side into the left-hand side to obtain

\displaystyle k^{\frac{1}{2}}||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}u_{\rho,8\rho}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}+||\sigma_{a}^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}\nabla u_{\rho,8\rho}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\leq C||\sigma_{a}^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}u||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[\frac{1}{6},\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C||\sigma_{a}^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}(|u|+|\nabla u|)||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},\frac{\rho}{3})\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C||\sigma_{a}^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}(|u|+|\nabla u|)||_{L^{2}(A(16\rho,24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

Since
\sigma_{a}\leq 1
and
1\leq e^{(\tau+a)/3}\leq e
, we can further estimate:

\displaystyle||(t+a)^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}u_{\rho,8\rho}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}+||\sigma_{a}^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}\nabla u_{\rho,8\rho}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\leq C^{k}||(t+a)^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}u||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[\frac{1}{6},\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C^{k}||(t+a)^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}(|u|+|\nabla u|)||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},\frac{\rho}{3})\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C^{k}||(t+a)^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}(|u|+|\nabla u|)||_{L^{2}(A(16\rho,24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

The first term on the right-hand side of the inequality can be estimated by the bounds (
6.1
) and
(\tau+a)\geq 1/6
.

||(t+a)^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}u||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[\frac{1}{6},\frac{1}{5}])}\leq 6^{k}||C||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[\frac{1}{6},\frac{1}{5}])}\leq C^{k}\rho^{\frac{n}{2}}

for all
k\geq k_{1}
and
a\in(0,1/8)
.

On the domains
A(\frac{\rho}{6},\frac{\rho}{3})\times[0,\frac{1}{5}]
and
A(16\rho,24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}]
, we have that

e^{-(r-\rho)^{2}/(8(\tau+a))}\leq e^{-\rho^{2}/(20(\tau+a))}.

We compute using Stirling’s formula that

\max_{s>0}s^{-k}e^{-\rho^{2}/(20s)}=\rho^{-2k}(20k)^{k}e^{-k}\leq\rho^{-2k}C^{k}k!.

This observation yields the following bound.

||(t+a)^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}(|u|+|\nabla u|)||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},\frac{\rho}{3})\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}\\
+||(t+a)^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}(|u|+|\nabla u|)||_{L^{2}(A(16\rho,24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}\leq C^{k}\rho^{-2k}k!\rho^{\frac{n}{2}},

for
k\geq k_{1}
and
a\in(0,1/8)
. Let
l
be such that
k=k_{1}+l
. Plugging all this information back into the primary inequality, we find that

||(t+a)^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}|u_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla u_{\rho,8\rho}|||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}\\
\leq\rho^{\frac{n}{2}}C^{k_{1}+l}(1+\rho^{-2(k_{1}+l)}(k_{1}+l)!)

for all
l\geq 0
and all
a\in(0,1/8)
. Noting that
(l+k_{1})!\leq C^{k_{1}+l}(l!)(k_{1}!)
,

\displaystyle||(t+a)^{-k}e^{-\frac{(|x|-\rho)^{2}}{8(t+a)}}|u_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla u_{\rho,8\rho}|||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\rho^{\frac{n}{2}}C^{k_{1}+l}(1+\rho^{-2l}C^{k_{1}+l}(k_{1}!)(l!))

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\rho^{\frac{n}{2}}(k_{1}!)^{2}(C^{l}+C^{l}\rho^{-2l}l!).

Remark A.1
.

Note that
C^{k_{1}}
can be bounded by
k_{1}!
for sufficiently large
\rho
. Thus, we increase
r_{0}>0
so that this may be true.

We multiply both sides by
\rho^{2l}/((2C)^{l}l!)
and sum over all
l\geq 0
yielding

\displaystyle||(|u_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla u_{\rho,8\rho}|)e^{\frac{\rho^{2}}{C(\tau+a)}-\frac{(r-\rho)^{2}}{8(\tau+a)}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,\frac{1}{5}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C(k_{1}!)^{2}\rho^{\frac{7}{2}}\bigg(1+e^{\frac{\rho^{2}}{2}}\bigg).

Restrict the time interval from
[0,1/5]
to
[0,a]
.

\displaystyle||(|u_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla u_{\rho,8\rho}|)e^{-\frac{(r-\rho)^{2}}{8(\tau+a)}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,a])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C(k_{1}!)^{2}\rho^{\frac{7}{2}}\bigg(1+e^{\frac{\rho^{2}}{2}}\bigg)e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}}{2Ca}}.

For sufficiently small
a>0
we obtain the following upper bound

||(|u_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla u_{\rho,8\rho}|)e^{-\frac{(r-\rho)^{2}}{8(\tau+a)}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,a])}\leq k_{1}^{2k_{1}}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}}{Ca}}.

Recall that
k_{1}\leq 16C^{2}(24\rho)^{2\delta}
. After increasing
C>0
, we have

\displaystyle k_{1}^{2k_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq(C\rho^{2\delta})^{C\rho^{2\delta}}

\displaystyle=\exp(C\rho^{2\delta}(\log(C)+2\delta\log(\rho)))

\displaystyle\leq\exp(C\rho^{2\delta^{\prime}}),

where
0<\delta<\delta^{\prime}<1
. This grows more slowly than quadratic exponential. By possibly increasing
r_{0}
, we finally obtain

||(|u_{\rho,8\rho}|+|\nabla u_{\rho,8\rho}|)e^{-\frac{(r-\rho)^{2}}{8(\tau+a)}}||_{L^{2}(A(\frac{\rho}{6},24\rho)\times[0,a])}\leq Ce^{-\frac{\rho^{2}}{Ca}}.

Observe that
e^{-|r-\rho|^{2}/(8(\tau+a))}\geq C^{-1}
on the domain
A((1-\sqrt{a})\rho,(1+\sqrt{a})\rho)\times[0,\delta a]
. Furthermore, on this set,
\psi_{\rho,8\rho}\equiv 1
and
\varphi\equiv 1
. Thus, the function
u_{\rho,8\rho}
agrees with its unmodified counterpart on this restricted domain and we obtain the estimate

(A.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\||u|+|\nabla u|\|_{L^{2}(A((1-\sqrt{s})\rho,(1+\sqrt{s})\rho)\times[0,s])}\leq Ce^{-\frac{\rho^{2}}{Cs}}\end{split}

for
s\in[0,1/C]
and arbitrary
\rho>12r_{0}
, which was the desired exponential decay.
∎

Remark A.2
.

Note that the proof of exponential decay only requires that
\delta\in(0,1)
, not the more restrictive condition
\delta\in(0,1/2)
that is required for the proof of vanishing.

In the proof of vanishing, we will use the following Carleman inequality with weights growing quadratic exponentially.

Proposition A.4
.

For each
\eta\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
r_{1}=r_{1}(\eta)\geq R
such that for all
\alpha\geq 1
and all
u\in C^{\infty}(Q_{R,T_{0}})
satisfying that
u(\cdot,0)\equiv 0
and that
u(\cdot,t)
is compactly supported in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus B_{r_{1}}
for each
t\in[0,T_{0}]
, we have the estimate

(A.7)

\alpha\Big\|ue^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(Q_{r_{1},T_{0}})}+\Big\||\nabla u|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(Q_{r_{1},T_{0}})}\\
\leq\frac{1}{2}\Big\|(\partial_{t}u+\Delta u)e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(Q_{r_{1},T_{0}})}\\
+\Big\||\nabla u|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus B_{r_{1}})\times\{T_{0}\})}.

Proof.

This inequality is (modulo constants) the inequality in
[
29
, Proposition 4.9]
with respect to the static Ricci flow of the Euclidean metric on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
(where
r_{c}^{2}(x)=h(x,t)=|x|
) applied to the
0
-order tensor bundle
{\mathcal{Z}}=C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times(0,1))
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
A.1
.

We now prove that the function
u
must vanish uniformly. We will follow the method of
[
29
]
, modified by a similar technique to that in the proof of Proposition
A.3
. We first show that the space-time
W^{1,2}
-norm, weighted by
e^{8|x|^{2}}
, is finite. By Proposition
A.3
, there exists a constant
s_{1}(n)
such that

|||u|+|\nabla u|||_{L^{2}(A(\rho-\sqrt{s},\rho+\sqrt{s})\times[0,s])}\leq e^{-16\rho^{2}}

for
s\in(0,s_{1}]
and
\rho>12r_{0}
. Now, choosing
r^{\prime\prime}>13r_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{4|x|^{2}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{r^{\prime\prime}}\setminus B_{R}\times[0,s_{1}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{4|x|^{2}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{r^{\prime\prime}}\setminus B_{13r_{0}}\times[0,s_{1}])}+\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{4|x|^{2}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{13r_{0}}\setminus B_{R}\times[0,s_{1}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{i=0}^{k^{\prime}}\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{4|x|^{2}}\|_{L^{2}(A(13r_{0}+2i\sqrt{s_{1}},13r_{0}+2(i+1)\sqrt{s_{1}})\times[0,s_{1}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C\|u\|_{W^{1,2}(B_{13r_{0}}\setminus B_{R}\times[0,s_{1}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{i=0}^{k^{\prime}}C(13r_{0}+2(i+1)\sqrt{s_{1}})^{\frac{n}{2}}e^{-12((13r_{0})^{2}+i^{2}s_{1}}+C,

where we have chosen
k^{\prime}
to be the least integer greater than
\tfrac{1}{2\sqrt{s_{1}}}(r^{\prime\prime}-13r_{0})
. By sending
r^{\prime\prime}
to infinity, we find that

(A.8)

\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{4|x|^{2}}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{R,s_{1}})}\leq C,

where
C
is a dimensional constant.

Let
\psi_{\rho,\xi}
be the spatial cutoff function given in Lemma
6.1
such that
\psi_{\rho,\xi}\equiv 1
on
[2\rho,\xi]
and
\equiv 0
on
(-\infty,\rho]\cup[2\xi,\infty)
. Define the compactly supported function
u_{\rho,\xi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\psi_{\rho,\xi}u
and choose
\rho>13r_{0}
sufficiently large. Now apply the inequality (
A.7
).

\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|u_{\rho,\xi}e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\xi)\times[0,T_{0}])}+\Big\||\nabla u_{\rho,\xi}|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\xi)\times[0,T_{0}])}\\
\leq 2\Big\|(\partial_{t}u_{\rho,\xi}+\Delta u_{\rho,\xi})e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\xi)\times[0,T_{0}])}\\
+\Big\||\nabla u_{\rho,\xi}|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\xi)\times\{T_{0}\})}

Observe that

|(\partial_{t}+\Delta)u_{\rho,\xi}|\leq C|x|^{\delta}|u_{\rho,\xi}|+\varepsilon|\nabla u_{\rho,\xi}|+|u|(|\nabla\psi_{\rho,\xi}|+|\Delta\psi_{\rho,\xi}|)+2|\nabla\psi_{\rho,\xi}||\nabla u|.

Combining these two inequalities yields

\displaystyle\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|u_{\rho,\xi}e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\xi)\times[0,T_{0}])}+\Big\||\nabla u_{\rho,\xi}|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\xi)\times[0,T_{0}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\Big\||x|^{\delta}u_{\rho,\xi}e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\xi)\times[0,T_{0}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C\Big\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\rho)\times[0,T_{0}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C\Big\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\xi,2\xi)\times[0,T_{0}])}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\Big\||u|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\rho)\times\{T_{0}\})}+C\Big\||u|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\xi,2\xi)\times\{T_{0}\})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C\Big\||\nabla u|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\xi)\times\{T_{0}\})}.

We now choose
\xi
such that
C(2\xi)^{\delta}\leq\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}/2
. We may increase
C
by an amount depending on
\delta
so that
\xi=C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}}
satisfies this relation. Absorbing the first term on the right-hand side yields the simplified inequality

\displaystyle\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|ue^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(2\rho,C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}})\times[0,T_{0}])}+\Big\||\nabla u|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(2\rho,C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}})\times[0,T_{0}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\Big\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\rho)\times[0,T_{0}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C\Big\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}},2C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}})\times[0,T_{0}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C\Big\||u|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\rho)\times\{T_{0}\})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C\Big\||u|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}},2C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}})\times\{T_{0}\})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C\Big\||\nabla u|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}})\times\{T_{0}\})}.

Now we choose
\eta
and
T_{0}
in order to bound the left-hand side. First, we choose
T_{0}
. By the mean value theorem and (
A.8
), we can choose
T_{0}\in(s_{1}/2,s_{1})
such that

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus B_{R})\times\{T_{0}\}}(|u|+|\nabla u|)^{2}e^{8|x|}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{s_{1}}\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{4|x|^{2}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(Q_{R,s_{1}})}

\displaystyle\leq C,

where
C>0
is a dimensional constant. Since
\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}=|x|^{2}
when
t=T_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle C\Big\||u|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\rho)\times\{T_{0}\})}+C\Big\||u|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}},2C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}})\times\{T_{0}\})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C\Big\||\nabla u|e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}})\times\{T_{0}\})}\leq 3C.

Next, we choose
\eta
so that

\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}\leq 4|x|^{2}

on the domain
A(C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}},2C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}})\times[0,T_{0}]
for sufficiently large values of
\alpha
. Factoring out
|x|^{2}
from this expression gives the following condition.

\displaystyle\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{-\eta}+1

\displaystyle\leq 4

To find a sufficient condition for
\eta
, we assume that
|T_{0}-t|\leq 1
and plug in
|x|=2C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}}
. We then find
\eta\in(0,1)
such that the following inequality is fulfilled for sufficiently large values of
\alpha
.

\displaystyle C^{\eta}\alpha\alpha^{-\frac{\eta}{2\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq 3

Since
1<C^{\eta}<C
, this inequality is guaranteed for
\alpha
large if

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\eta}{2\delta}

\displaystyle<0

\displaystyle\implies 2\delta

\displaystyle<\eta.

Since
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
, it is always possible to find such
\eta(\delta)\in(0,1)
. Choosing and fixing such a value for
\eta
and applying (
A.8
) yields the following inequality.

\displaystyle\Big\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}},2C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}})\times[0,T_{0}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{4|x|^{2}}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{R,s_{1}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C.

Combining results gives the inequality

\displaystyle\Big\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(2\rho,C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}})\times[0,T_{0}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\Big\|(|u|+|\nabla u|)e^{\frac{\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\rho)\times[0,T_{0}])}+C.

Now, we can directly apply the method of
[
10
]
and
[
29
]
to obtain the vanishing. Let
\theta\geq 1
be a constant depending on
\rho
to be determined. On the domain
Q_{8\theta\rho,\tfrac{T_{0}}{2}}
, the following lower bound holds.

\exp(4\alpha T_{0}(\theta\rho)^{2-\eta})\leq\exp(\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2}).

On
A(\rho,2\rho)\times[0,T_{0}]
, the following upper bound holds.

\exp(\alpha(T_{0}-t)|x|^{2-\eta}+|x|^{2})\leq\exp(4\rho^{2}(\alpha T_{0}+1)).

Plug these inequalities in to obtain

\displaystyle\Big\||u|+|\nabla u|\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(8\theta\rho,C^{-1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2\delta}})\times[0,T_{0}/2])}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq Ce^{-4\alpha T_{0}(\theta\rho)^{2-\eta}}e^{4\rho^{2}(\alpha T_{0}+1)}\Big\||u|+|\nabla u|\Big\|_{L^{2}(A(\rho,2\rho)\times[0,T_{0}])}+Ce^{-4\alpha T_{0}(\theta\rho)^{2-\eta}}.

Choose
\theta>1
such that
\theta^{2-\eta}>2\rho^{\eta}
. Then,

4\rho^{2}(1+\alpha T_{0}-\alpha T_{0}\theta^{2-\eta}\rho^{-\eta})<4\rho^{2}(1-\alpha T_{0}).

Send
\alpha\rightarrow+\infty
to obtain that

(A.9)

\Big\||u|+|\nabla u|\Big\|_{L^{2}(Q_{8\theta\rho,T_{0}/2})}=0.

This tells us that
u
vanishes identically outside of a large annulus
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus B_{8\theta\rho}
. However, since
u
now must be supported in
B_{8\theta\rho}
, it must also satisfy the following differential inequality.

|\Delta u+\partial_{t}u|\leq M|\nabla u|+(N|8\theta\rho|^{\delta}+M)|u|.

We may then apply
[
10
, Theorem 1]
to obtain that
u
vanishes on all of
Q_{R,T_{0}/2}
. Since
T_{0}
is uniformly bounded below by
s_{1}/2
, iterating as in the proof of
[
10
, Theorem 1]
suffices to prove that
u
vanishes on all of
Q_{R,T}
.
∎
